306 — Out-of-Order CPU Simulator

Goal: Implement, from scratch in Rust, a cycle-accurate superscalar out-of-order CPU microarchitecture simulator executing RISC-V RV64IM — fetch with branch prediction (BTB/RAS/ gshare/TAGE-lite), decode, register renaming (RAT + free list + physical register file), dispatch into a reorder buffer, out-of-order issue from reservation stations (wakeup + select), pipelined functional units with a bypass network, a load/store queue with memory disambiguation + store-to-load forwarding, a cache/TLB hierarchy, and in-order commit with precise exceptions and full speculation recovery. Co-validated every commit against a functional golden model and differential-tested vs Spike; reports IPC + microarch counters. It is a timing simulator (a software model), not an RTL design.

Project Number: 306

Project Name: Out-of-Order CPU Simulator

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Build a from-scratch, cycle-accurate, superscalar out-of-order timing model of an RV64IM core in Rust, co-validated commit-for-commit against a functional golden model, reporting IPC + microarchitectural statistics.

1.1 Precise engineering definition

A software simulator (not RTL, not an emulator) that models a modern OoO RISC-V core at per-cycle granularity:

Property Definition (LOCKED)
Cycle-accurate every structure (ROB/RS/LSQ/FU/cache) advances on a discrete Cycle; latencies modeled, not abstracted
Superscalar configurable fetch_width/issue_width/commit_width ≥ 1 (target 4-wide default)
OoO dynamic scheduling à la Tomasulo: in-order fetch/rename/commit, out-of-order issue/execute
ISA RV64I + M-extension only (Add/Sub/Mul/Div/Lw/Sw/Beq/Jal/Jalr/...); see Opcode
Golden-validated committed ArchState sequence === execute_functional step sequence, exactly, every commit
Reports Stats { cycles, committed, mispredicts, cache_misses }; ipc = committed / cycles

Microarch modeled: fetch + branch prediction (Btb, Ras, Gshare→TageLite), decode, rename (Rat+FreeList+PhysRegFile), dispatch→ReorderBuffer, OoO issue (Scheduler wakeup+select), pipelined FunctionalUnits + BypassNetwork, LoadStoreQueue (disambiguation + store-to-load forwarding), Cache/Tlb hierarchy, in-order CommitUnit with precise exceptions + full speculation recovery (Flush + RenameCheckpoint restore).

Load-bearing determinism rule: OooCore::tick() advances stages in reverse pipeline order (Commit→Writeback→Execute→Issue→Dispatch→Rename→Decode→Fetch) so a value produced in cycle C is consumed only in C+1 — models real latches, forbids same-cycle teleporting.

1.2 System boundaries

ELF / hex program  ─┐
ConfigParams knobs ─┼─►  OooCore (9 ooo-* crates)  ─►  Stats (IPC, counters) + trace + cosim verdict
seed (determinism) ─┘                                  golden ArchState (co-sim oracle, in-process)

In scope: single-core timing + functional semantics + memory hierarchy + prediction + recovery + co-sim + conformance. Out of scope: see 1.3.

1.3 NON-goals (explicit)

Excluded Reason / disposition
RTL / Verilog / synthesis this is a software timing model, not a hardware design
Full RV64GC FD float, A atomics, C compressed = PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §1.6")
OS / privileged modes only a minimal Trap/CSR subset; no M/S/U privilege ladder, no virtual memory beyond Tlb shell
Multi-core / cache coherence single-core only; MESI is project 187
Power / thermal / analog no energy, voltage, or timing-at-the-gate modeling
JIT / binary translation interpreting timing model; no host codegen

1.4 Deployment model

  • CLI binary oooc — subcommands run, trace, stats, cosim, config, bench. Input: ELF or hex image + ConfigParams. Output: IPC/counters/per-cycle pipeline dump/cosim verdict.
  • Embeddable Rust crates — ooo-core (+ deps) usable as a library for architecture experiments; ooo-isa is no_std + alloc (reusable golden model / decoder). Construct OooCore, call run(max_cycles) -> Stats.

1.5 Target users + prerequisites

Users: computer-architecture researchers/students (sweep ConfigParams, study IPC sensitivity), performance engineers (microbenchmark behavior, prediction accuracy), course staff (teaching Tomasulo/ROB/renaming on a real, runnable model).

Prerequisites (reader): RISC-V RV64IM ISA; Tomasulo / ROB / register renaming; branch prediction (BTB/RAS/gshare/TAGE); memory disambiguation + store-to-load forwarding; discrete-event / clocked simulation; H&P "Computer Architecture: A Quantitative Approach" Ch. 3.

1.6 Deferred features (gated)

Each returns/asserts PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §X") at the boundary; stubs compile, never silently mis-execute:

Feature Gate
F/D floating point PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §1.3")
A atomics (LR/SC, AMO) PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §1.3")
C compressed encoding PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §1.3")
Multi-core / coherence deferred to project 187

1.7 Phase-0 deliverable — the nine ooo-* crates

  1. ooo-isa — RV64IM decode + ArchState + execute_functional (golden model); no_std + alloc.
  2. ooo-frontend — FetchUnit, FetchBuffer, BranchPredictor, Btb, Ras, Gshare/TageLite, Prediction.
  3. ooo-rename — Rat, FreeList, PhysReg, PhysRegFile, RenameUnit, RenameCheckpoint.
  4. ooo-schedule — Scheduler/ReservationStation, RsEntry, wakeup+select, IssueQueue, IssuePort.
  5. ooo-execute — FunctionalUnit, FuKind, ExecResult, BypassNetwork, latency tables, writeback.
  6. ooo-mem — LoadStoreQueue, LsqEntry, disambiguation + store-to-load forwarding, Cache, Tlb, MemRequest/MemResponse.
  7. ooo-rob — ReorderBuffer, RobEntry, CommitUnit, precise exceptions, Flush/recovery.
  8. ooo-core — OooCore, tick()/cycle loop, latches, Cycle, ConfigParams, Stats.
  9. ooo-cli — the oooc binary.

Phase-0 smoke: decode + functionally execute a "sum 1..N" program via the ooo-isa golden model, print the result. No timing yet.

1.8 What later phases unlock

After phase Capability
1 (§13) full RV64IM functional golden model + traps skeleton
2 (§14) in-order 5-stage timing baseline (reverse-order tick(), latches, stalls)
3 (§15) fetch + branch prediction; measured prediction accuracy
4-5 (§16-17) rename (WAR/WAW eliminated) + ROB dispatch
6-7 (§18-19) OoO issue (wakeup/select) + pipelined FUs + bypass
8-9 (§20-21) LSQ + cache/TLB; memory latency feeds wakeup
10 (§22) precise exceptions + mispredict flush + checkpoint restore
11 (§23) superscalar widening + full perf-counter Stats
12-13 (§24-25) golden co-sim + determinism; conformance/diff-vs-Spike/fuzz/IPC benches

1.9 Language + rationale

Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78. Tight structs for per-cycle state; sum types + exhaustive match model Opcode/FuKind/stage state/ROB-RS-LSQ entries at zero cost; ownership prevents the aliasing bugs that plague large mutable timing models; deterministic stepping (no GC). Maps gem5's C++ O3CPU (Fetch/Decode/Rename/IEW/Commit) cleanly. Rejected: C/C++ (memory-unsafe at this mutable scale), Python (too slow for the >1M cycles/s target; project 188 covered Python), Go (weaker sum types; GC pauses perturb deterministic ticks).

1.10 Targets + references

  • Throughput: ≥ 1M simulated cycles/s single-thread (release build).
  • Default config: 4-wide; rob_size 128, rs_size 64, lsq_size 32, ~180 phys regs.
  • Branch prediction accuracy: gshare ≥ 90%, TageLite ≥ 95% on SPEC-like microbenchmarks.
  • Co-sim: zero architectural divergence across riscv-tests + fuzzed programs.

References: gem5 O3CPU / DerivO3CPU; Tomasulo; Smith/Sohi "The Microarchitecture of Superscalar Processors"; H&P Ch. 3; TAGE papers; riscv-tests + RISC-V ISA manual; ChampSim / Sniper / Spike (golden ISA).

2. Why Build This

2.1 The unique systems knowledge

OoO execution is the dominant ILP-extraction mechanism of every high-performance CPU. Building a cycle-accurate model forces internalizing mechanisms that no functional emulator (project 190) or in-order pipeline sim (project 188) ever exercises. The load-bearing ideas, one per row:

Mechanism What it actually does Why it matters Where in this sim
Register renaming Maps 32 arch regs → larger PhysRegFile; each writer gets a fresh PhysReg Kills WAR + WAW false deps; only true RAW remains. ILP ∝ exposed independent ops ooo-rename: Rat + FreeList
Reorder buffer Circular in-order queue of RobEntry; alloc at dispatch, retire at head Decouples execution order (OoO) from retirement order (in-order) ⇒ precise exceptions over reordered exec ooo-rob: ReorderBuffer
Wakeup / select Completing FU broadcasts dst PhysReg; matching RsEntry srcs go ready; select picks oldest-ready per port The scheduler heart — the actual dataflow engine that finds runnable ops each cycle ooo-schedule: Scheduler
Speculation + recovery Fetch past unresolved branches; RenameCheckpoint at rename; on mispredict restore RAT, roll FreeList, squash younger ROB/RS/LSQ, redirect fetch Without recovery, speculation is unsound; misprediction penalty dominates real IPC ooo-rob flush + ooo-frontend
Memory disambiguation Load scans older LsqEntry stores; exact addr ⇒ store-to-load forward; unknown older addr ⇒ wait or speculate + detect violation Memory deps aren't known at rename (addresses computed late) — the hardest ordering problem ooo-mem: LoadStoreQueue
Reverse-order tick() Stages advanced Commit→…→Fetch; result in cycle C visible at C+1 only Models real pipeline latches; the determinism rule that makes the whole model correct ooo-core: OooCore::tick

The reverse-order tick is the single most important and most counter-intuitive fact: it is why the model is cycle-accurate rather than a same-cycle dataflow oracle. Producing a value and consuming it in the same call would "teleport" data backward through the pipeline — the reverse sweep makes each latch a one-cycle delay for free, with no explicit double-buffering.

The second non-obvious lesson: timing and architecture are orthogonal. Out-of-order execution must still produce the exact same sequence of committed architectural states as a naive in-order interpreter. The golden co-sim (ooo-isa::execute_functional) checked at every commit is the proof — Phase 12 elevates "looks right on a benchmark" to "provably equivalent to the ISA, independent of pipeline depth, width, or predictor."

2.2 Why OoO cores matter — historically + architecturally

  • Tomasulo (IBM 360/91, 1967) introduced reservation stations + register tags + CDB — the direct ancestor of Scheduler wakeup/select and the BypassNetwork.
  • Smith & Pleszkun / Sohi added the reorder buffer for precise exceptions over OoO retirement.
  • Pentium Pro (1995) + MIPS R10000 made full OoO mainstream; every high-perf core since (x86, Apple M-series, modern ARM, high-end RISC-V) is a descendant of this exact organization: rename → ROB → unified/distributed scheduler → bypass → LSQ → in-order commit.
  • gem5 DerivO3CPU (Fetch/Decode/Rename/IEW/Commit) is the academic + industrial standard for microarchitecture research; this sim deliberately mirrors its stage decomposition so the knowledge transfers directly. Hennessy & Patterson Ch. 3 + the TAGE papers are the curriculum.

Understanding why a workload gets IPC 0.4 vs 2.6 — branch mispredict rate, ROB/RS/LSQ capacity stalls, cache miss latency exposed through wakeup, port contention — is a skill that only a mechanistic model teaches; analytical formulas and counters alone do not.

2.3 Engineering muscles exercised

Muscle Concretely
Large mutable deterministic state machines ~10 interacting structures (ROB, RS, LSQ, RAT, FreeList, PRF, caches) mutated per cycle; Rust ownership prevents the aliasing/stale-pointer bugs that plague C++ timing models
Co-simulation / differential testing Lock-step golden compare; divergence detection + bisection to first diverging commit (Phase 12)
Performance modeling Translating a microarchitecture into Cycle-resolution timing; deriving IPC + counter-attributable stall breakdown (Stats)
Determinism + replay Seed/config-reproducible runs; reverse-order tick as the invariant that guarantees bit-identical replay
Conformance + fuzzing riscv-tests, differential vs Spike, random program generator that must never diverge under cosim (Phase 13)

2.4 Portfolio cross-links

Project Relationship
188 OoO Pipeline Sim (Python) Direct predecessor; this is the Rust, cycle-accurate, superscalar, co-validated successor — >1M cycles/sec where Python could not
190 RISC-V Emulator Supplies the functional mental model; ooo-isa is the timing-free golden core, the cosim oracle here
189 Branch Predictor ooo-frontend (Btb, Ras, Gshare, TageLite) reuses its predictor designs; here mispredict cost is measured in real cycles
187 MESI Coherence Explicit NON-GOAL boundary — single-core here; 187 owns multi-core coherence. ooo-mem is the single-core seam they'd join at
51 JIT / 31 VM Contrast: those translate/interpret for function; this is an interpreting timing model (NON-GOAL: binary translation/JIT)
75 Profiler Consumes the same IPC/stall taxonomy; this sim is the ground-truth generator profilers approximate

2.5 Calibration value

A from-scratch, golden-validated OoO core is a top-tier calibration artifact: it spans ISA semantics, the full superscalar microarchitecture, deterministic systems engineering, and statistical performance reasoning — and it is checkable (cosim equivalence + riscv-tests are pass/fail, not subjective). It is the capstone that ties the architecture-track portfolio (187, 188, 189, 190) into one coherent, provably-correct model.

What Mastery Looks Like

After reading this blueprint and building through it, the reader commands:

  • Full ISA-to-microarchitecture translation (Phase 1, §8): can map any RV64IM encoding to its DecodedInst, explain every immediate format's bit permutation, and identify where execute_functional + decode diverge from a microarchitecture's speculative behavior — the exact skill tested at RISC-V CPU design interviews.
  • Dynamic scheduling by construction (Phase 6, §11.4): can design a wakeup-select scheduler from scratch — CAM semantics, age-ordered priority, RS sizing — and explain why each invariant (oldest-ready, src-ready at issue, no double-issue) is load-bearing, not stylistic.
  • Speculation invariant reasoning (Phase 10, §6.6, §7.7): can trace a branch misprediction from fetch through the RenameCheckpoint restore and FreeList rollback, identifying exactly which physical registers become zombies and which commit-gate prevents speculative stores from reaching the cache.
  • Memory disambiguation mastery (Phase 8, §11.6): can reason about all four outcomes of a load-vs-older-stores scan (forward / wait / cache / partial-conflict), implement store-to-load forwarding correctly with size semantics, and explain why the conservative default is a safety invariant, not a performance choice.
  • Cycle-accurate IPC attribution (Phase 11, §28.2): given a stall-cause histogram from a real run, can identify the binding structural constraint (ROB-full, RS-full, FU-busy, LSQ-full, FreeList-empty, branch-MPKI) and prescribe the correct knob — a skill that directly maps to performance-engineering interviews at CPU-design companies.
  • Formal co-simulation discipline (Phase 12, §24): can implement a lock-step golden co-sim, design a binary-search bisection harness, and explain why replay(seed) -> commit_crc determinism is the property that makes divergence debugging tractable — transferable to any complex stateful simulation system.
  • Deterministic simulation systems engineering (Phase 0–Phase 12, §6.3, §4.4): understands why HashMap iteration, wall-clock reads, and uninitialized memory are correctness threats in a deterministic simulator, and can enumerate the full set of nondeterminism sources and their mitigations — a principal-engineer-level skill for any timing-model codebase.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 Canonical pipeline

    +--------+   +---------+   +--------+   +----------+   +-----------+   +---------+   +--------+
    | Fetch  |-->| Decode  |-->| Rename |-->| Dispatch |-->| Issue/Sch |-->| Execute |-->| Commit |
    | +BPred |   |         |   | RAT/FL |   |  ->ROB    |   |  RS/wakeup|   |  FUs    |   |  ROB   |
    +--------+   +---------+   +--------+   +----------+   +-----------+   +---------+   +--------+
       ^  BTB/RAS                  PhysRegFile           select  ^  bypass     | WB        | precise
       |  Gshare/TAGE                                            |  network    v           v  exceptions
       +<--------------------- mispredict flush / checkpoint restore ----------+   free old_phys
                                                          LSQ <-> Cache/TLB (ooo-mem) hangs off Execute/Mem
    [ ooo-core drives Tick() in reverse stage order; Stats tracks IPC; golden ArchState co-sim checks commit ]

Crate-to-box map: Fetch+BPred → ooo-frontend; Decode → ooo-isa; Rename → ooo-rename; Dispatch→ROB alloc → ooo-rename+ooo-rob; Issue/Sched → ooo-schedule; Execute+bypass → ooo-execute; LSQ<->Cache/TLB → ooo-mem; Commit → ooo-rob; clock/wiring/Stats/cosim → ooo-core.

3.2 System boundaries

Boundary Location Discipline
Trust guest ELF/hex bytes loaded into modeled memory untrusted input; decoder must total-decode (illegal→Trap, never panic); all guest loads/stores bounds-checked against modeled address space; no host escape — guest cannot read/execute host memory
Concurrency none (single-threaded) one deterministic clocked model; no threads, no async, no shared mutable host state; Tick() reverse-order traversal IS the synchronization discipline — a value written in cycle C is visible only at C+1 (latch semantics), so there is no data race to reason about, only stage-ordering
Persistence none pure in-memory model; outputs = Stats report (stdout/JSON) + optional per-cycle trace dump; no DB, no checkpoint files; RenameCheckpoint is in-memory recovery state, NOT durable persistence; deterministic replay reconstructs a run from (seed, ConfigParams, program), not from a saved snapshot

Reverse-order tick() (Commit→Writeback→Execute→Issue→Dispatch→Rename→Decode→Fetch) is the load-bearing invariant for the entire model: it guarantees no same-cycle teleporting of results, making the simulator deterministic and order-independent within a cycle. Every stage below assumes this discipline.

3.3 Stage-by-stage

Fetch + BranchPredictor (ooo-frontend)

Responsibilities: drive fetch PC, read up to fetch_width instructions/cycle from modeled instruction memory (via I-TLB / L1-I in ooo-mem once Phase 9 lands), consult BranchPredictor (Btb for target, Ras for returns, Gshare/TageLite for direction) to pick the next-fetch PC speculatively, and push entries into FetchBuffer. Inputs: current fetch PC, predictor state, redirect requests from branch resolve / commit. Outputs: FetchBuffer of DecodedInst + Prediction{taken,target} tag per fetched branch. Key invariant: fetch is purely speculative — it may run far ahead down a wrong path; every fetched instruction carries enough provenance (its predicted target + the predictor snapshot needed for update) so that a later flush can roll the front-end back exactly. Predictor accuracy target: gshare ≥90%, TageLite ≥95% on riscv-tests branch-heavy kernels. Referenced by: Phase 3 builds this; Phase 10 wires the mispredict-redirect edge; Phase 13 measures MPKI / accuracy.

Decode (ooo-isa)

Responsibilities: turn Inst{raw:u32} into DecodedInst{op,rd,rs1,rs2,imm,is_branch,is_load, is_store,fu} via decode(); classify FuKind; extract + sign/zero-extend immediates per RV64IM format (I/S/B/U/J). Also home of the golden model: ArchState{xregs,pc,csr} and execute_functional(&DecodedInst,&mut ArchState) — the architectural truth the timing model is validated against. Inputs: raw 32-bit words from FetchBuffer. Outputs: DecodedInst stream to Rename; for golden cosim, a one-instruction architectural step. Key invariant: decode is a total function — every 32-bit pattern maps to either a legal Opcode or an illegal-instruction Trap; x0 decodes normally but is hardwired-zero downstream. Referenced by: Phase 1 (full RV64IM semantics + golden model); every later phase consumes DecodedInst; Phase 12 cosim calls execute_functional.

RenameUnit (ooo-rename)

Responsibilities: map each architectural RegId to a PhysReg to eliminate WAR/WAW false dependencies, leaving only true RAW deps tracked via PhysRegFile.ready. Per instruction: look up rs1/rs2 in Rat (→ source PhysRegs), pop a fresh PhysReg from FreeList for rd, record the previous mapping (old_phys) for later reclaim, install the new mapping in Rat. Captures a RenameCheckpoint{rat,free_head} at each branch for recovery. Inputs: in-order DecodedInst. Outputs: renamed instruction (phys sources + phys dst + old_phys) into Dispatch. Key invariant: x0 is never renamed (always reads physical zero, never allocated); the RAT is always a consistent arch→phys snapshot recoverable from the nearest younger-than-branch checkpoint. Stalls when FreeList empty (insufficient phys_regs). Referenced by: Phase 4 builds RAT/FreeList/checkpoint; Phase 10 restores checkpoints on mispredict; Phase 11 widens to issue_width-wide rename.

Dispatch + ReorderBuffer (ooo-rob + ooo-rename)

Responsibilities: allocate one RobEntry{pc,dst_arch,dst_phys,old_phys,done,exception,is_store, mispredict} per renamed instruction at the ROB tail (in program order), allocate the matching RsEntry (and LsqEntry for loads/stores), and stall on structural hazards (ROB full, RS full, LSQ full). Inputs: renamed instruction from Rename. Outputs: live RobEntry (in-order list) + RsEntry handed to the Scheduler + LsqEntry to the LSQ. Key invariant: ROB allocation is strictly in program order on a circular buffer (head/tail); rob_size bounds in-flight instructions; the ROB is the single source of program-order truth used by commit and squash. Referenced by: Phase 5 (allocation + structural stalls); Phase 10 (commit + squash walk the ROB); Phase 11 multi-wide dispatch.

Scheduler / ReservationStation (ooo-schedule)

Responsibilities: hold not-yet-issued RsEntrys; wakeup — when an FU broadcasts a completing PhysReg, set src1_ready/src2_ready on every matching entry; select — each cycle pick the oldest ready entry per FU / IssuePort (age-ordered), mark issued, and route to Execute. Inputs: RsEntrys from Dispatch, wakeup broadcasts from Execute/bypass, ready bits from PhysRegFile. Outputs: up to issue_width issued instructions/cycle to FunctionalUnits. Key invariant: an entry issues exactly once and only when both sources are ready and a matching FU port is free; out-of-order issue is permitted but select is age-priority to avoid starvation. Referenced by: Phase 6 (wakeup/select, OoO issue); Phase 9 (memory latency feeds back into wakeup timing); Phase 11 (multiple IssuePorts + port contention).

FunctionalUnits + BypassNetwork (ooo-execute)

Responsibilities: execute issued ops on FunctionalUnit{kind,latency,pipelined,busy_until} — Alu(1c), Mul(3c, pipelined), Div(iterative, ~20–40c, non-pipelined), Branch(resolve + flag mispredict), Agu(address gen for LSQ), LoadStore; produce ExecResult{dst,value, rob_idx,mispredict} at writeback. BypassNetwork forwards a producer's result to a dependent's issue the same/next cycle so back-to-back dependent ALU ops don't pay full writeback latency. Inputs: issued RsEntry + operand values. Outputs: ExecResult → writeback (sets PhysRegFile.ready, marks RobEntry.done) + wakeup broadcast. Key invariant: latency model honors busy_until (pipelined FUs accept 1/cycle; iterative FUs block until free); writeback drives exactly the wakeup that lets dependents issue at the right cycle. Referenced by: Phase 7 (FU set + latency table + bypass); Phase 11 (replicated FUs per port).

LoadStoreQueue + Cache/Tlb (ooo-mem)

Responsibilities: order memory ops via LoadStoreQueue{loads,stores} of LsqEntry{rob_idx,is_load, addr,value,addr_ready,committed}; compute addresses (via Agu); memory disambiguation — a load checks older stores: exact-address hit → store-to-load forwarding of the store value; unknown older-store address → load waits (conservative) or speculates + detects violation at commit. Backed by Cache(set-assoc L1/L2, MSHRs, miss latency) + Tlb(va→pa) via MemRequest/MemResponse. Inputs: Agu addresses, store data, cache/TLB responses. Outputs: load values (forwarded or from cache) back to writeback/wakeup; ordering constraints to ROB. Key invariant: stores write memory only at commit (architectural state never updated speculatively); loads observe a correct value-ordering vs older stores. Referenced by: Phase 8 (LSQ + disambig + forwarding); Phase 9 (Cache/TLB latency → wakeup feedback); Phase 10 (LSQ squash on flush).

CommitUnit (ooo-rob)

Responsibilities: retire up to commit_width RobEntrys/cycle from the ROB head in program order only when done; on commit, copy result to architectural state, free old_phys back to the FreeList, let committed stores drain to memory; on exception at head, take a precise trap (architectural state reflects all older instructions, none younger); on mispredict at head (or resolve), trigger flush. Inputs: ROB head entries. Outputs: architectural-state updates, freed PhysRegs, trap/flush signals, Stats.committed++. Key invariant: commit is the only place architectural state changes, and always in program order → precise exceptions for free. Referenced by: Phase 10 (commit + precise exceptions + recovery: free old_phys, RenameCheckpoint restore, ROB/RS/LSQ squash, fetch redirect); Phase 12 (cosim compares committed state).

OooCore (ooo-core)

Responsibilities: own all stage units + ROB/RS/LSQ + ConfigParams + Stats + the golden ArchState; drive tick() (advance all stages in reverse pipeline order) and run(max_cycles) -> Stats; maintain the global Cycle counter; wire inter-stage latches; run golden cosim — each commit, lock-step the just-committed architectural delta against execute_functional and assert exact equivalence. Inputs: program image + ConfigParams (+ seed for replay). Outputs: Stats{cycles,committed,mispredicts,cache_misses}, ipc = committed as f64 / cycles, optional trace. Key invariant: reverse-order tick makes the model deterministic and replayable from (seed, config, program); the golden cosim guarantees the committed architectural-state sequence EXACTLY matches the functional model regardless of timing. Referenced by: Phase 0 (tick stub + Cycle/Config/Stats shells); Phase 2 (reverse-order loop + latches); Phase 11 (full Stats set); Phase 12 (determinism + divergence bisection); Phase 13 (IPC/perf benchmarks). Sim-throughput target: ≥1M simulated cycles/sec single-thread.

4. System Constraints

Hard, quantified limits binding every phase. A constraint violated is a defect, not a tuning knob. "Default config" below is the reference ConfigParams used for all throughput/IPC targets unless a phase overrides it explicitly.

4.1 Default microarchitecture (reference ConfigParams)

Field (ConfigParams) Default Notes
fetch_width 4 insts fetched/cycle from FetchBuffer
issue_width 4 select picks ≤4 oldest-ready RsEntries/cycle
commit_width 4 CommitUnit retires ≤4 RobEntries/cycle, in order
rob_size 192 ReorderBuffer entries (circular)
rs_size 60 unified Scheduler RsEntry slots
lsq_size 32×2 32 loads + 32 stores in LoadStoreQueue
phys_regs 128 PhysRegFile size; ≥ 32 arch + rob_size headroom
predictor Gshare PredictorKind::Gshare default; TageLite opt-in
FU (FuKind) Latency (cycles) Pipelined
Alu 1 yes (1 issue/cycle)
Branch 1 yes
Mul 3 yes (back-to-back issue)
Div 20 no (iterative; FU busy_until blocks)
Agu 1 yes (address gen)
LoadStore (Lw) 2 + cache 2-cycle AGU+L1 hit; +miss penalty on MSHR

Cache/TLB defaults (Phase 9): L1 32 KiB 8-way 4-cycle, L2 256 KiB 8-way 12-cycle, DRAM 100-cycle, 64 B lines, 8 MSHRs/level; TLB 64-entry 4-way, miss = page-walk stub 30 cycles. x0 (RegId(0)) never renamed, always reads 0.

4.2 Performance targets

Metric Target Condition
Simulated-cycle throughput > 1,000,000 cycles/sec single host core, default config, release build
Instruction throughput > 200,000 committed insts/sec implies sim IPC ≥ ~0.2 floor on host work
tick() cost < 1 µs/cycle amortized reverse-stage loop, no per-cycle heap alloc
Cold start to first tick() < 5 ms zero global/static init (§4.5)
Co-sim overhead (cosim) < 2× the run path per-commit ArchState compare only

Throughput measured on a contemporary x86-64 / arm64 host, single thread; degradation acceptable for trace (per-cycle pipeline dump) and cosim modes — both excluded from the > 1M cycles/sec bar.

4.3 Memory footprint

Per-cycle live state is O(rob_size + rs_size + lsq_size + phys_regs) — bounded, no growth with program length (state is recycled via free list + circular ROB).

Structure Element size (approx) Count Bytes
ReorderBuffer (RobEntry) ~40 B 192 ~7.7 KiB
Scheduler (RsEntry) ~32 B 60 ~1.9 KiB
LoadStoreQueue (LsqEntry) ~48 B 64 ~3.1 KiB
PhysRegFile (u64 + ready) 9 B 128 ~1.2 KiB
Predictor (Btb/Ras/Gshare) — — ~16–64 KiB
Caches/TLB (default) — — ~300 KiB
Core resident (excl. guest memory) < 1 MiB

Guest memory: sparse, page-granular (4 KiB) backing map; only touched pages allocated. Total host RSS bound: guest-resident-pages + < 1 MiB core. No per-instruction allocation on the steady-state tick() path; all entry pools pre-sized from ConfigParams at construction.

4.4 Determinism

  • Identical (program, ConfigParams, seed) → byte-identical Stats and identical committed-state sequence, every run, every host. Load-bearing: reverse-order tick() (§ spine) prevents same-cycle value teleporting.
  • No wall-clock, no thread scheduling, no HashMap iteration order, no uninitialized reads in any decision. Any randomness (e.g. fuzz program gen, replacement tie-breaks) draws from one explicitly-seeded PRNG.
  • Deterministic replay (Phase 12): (seed, config) reproduces a run exactly; divergence bisection relies on this.
  • Select/wakeup ties broken by age (oldest ROB index first), never by container address or insertion accident.

4.5 Startup & initialization

  • Zero global/static mutable init; no lazy_static/once_cell on the hot path. All state hangs off an OooCore instance built from ConfigParams.
  • Construction allocates all pools once (ROB/RS/LSQ/PhysRegFile/caches sized from config); tick() allocates nothing.
  • Multiple independent OooCore instances in one process are legal and isolated (enables parallel batch sims, fuzzing shards).

4.6 Program size & ISA scope

  • ISA: RV64IM only. RV64GC (F/D float, A atomics, C compressed) is PhaseDeferred — decoding an out-of-scope opcode raises a defined Trap, never panics.
  • Guest image ≤ 256 MiB virtual; ≤ 4 GiB addressable VA (sparse). Loaded from ELF or flat hex via oooc run.
  • Run length bounded by run(max_cycles); default cap 1e9 cycles. No deadlock: an empty pipeline with non-empty fetch and no forward progress for N cycles aborts with a diagnostic.
  • Single-core only — no multi-core, no cache coherence (MESI is project 187), no SMP guest.

4.7 Modeling fidelity

  • Architectural correctness is absolute: committed ArchState must EXACTLY equal the ooo-isa golden model at every commit (Phase 12 co-sim). Timing is a model; correctness is not negotiable.
  • IPC fidelity target: within ±10% of gem5 DerivO3CPU on shared traces with matched config (width, ROB, latencies, predictor), median across the riscv-tests + microbenchmark suite; per-benchmark outliers documented, not silently tuned.
  • Branch prediction accuracy targets (Phase 3): Gshare ≥ 90%, TageLite ≥ 95% on standard branch-heavy microbenchmarks.
  • Cache/latency model is first-order (fixed per-level latency + MSHR occupancy); no analog, no power/thermal, no DRAM bank/row modeling — explicitly out of scope.

4.8 Out of scope (stated, not budgeted)

No NUMA, no network, no file-descriptor/syscall budget, no async I/O, no real-time deadlines, no RTL/Verilog, no JIT/binary translation (interpreting timing model). These carry zero engineering budget and no constraint targets; listed only to bound expectations.

4.9 Robustness & recovery

Hostile input Mandated behavior
Illegal/unsupported opcode Decode → defined Trap; ROB takes precise exception at head; sim continues
Misaligned / out-of-bounds load/store Trap via LSQ→ROB; no host segfault
Division by zero (Div) RV64IM-defined result (no trap per ISA), modeled exactly
Infinite guest loop Bounded by max_cycles; clean Stats return
Truncated/corrupt ELF Loader error, non-zero exit; never panics mid-load
Internal invariant break (debug) debug_assert!; release path degrades to defined trap, not UB

A malformed guest program traps; it never crashes, panics, or corrupts the simulator. All guest-derived addresses are bounds-checked against the guest memory map before host access. unsafe is forbidden outside vetted, documented hot-path leaf functions (none expected in the reference design).

5. Required Reading

Curated, prioritized. Each entry: what to read, why it maps to this simulator, what to extract. Cite consistently across phases. Reading is mandatory before the phase that depends on it (cross-refs in ## 12–## 25).

5.1 Books

# Work Read Maps to Extract
B1 Hennessy & Patterson, Computer Architecture: A Quantitative Approach (6th ed.) Ch. 3 (ILP, Tomasulo, speculation, dynamic scheduling); App. C (pipelining); App. B (cache); App. K (RISC-V) whole pipeline: rename → ROB → RS → commit; cache hierarchy (## 21) Tomasulo + reservation-station model; ROB precise-exception mechanism; IPC vs CPI; cache miss-rate / AMAT formulas; branch-penalty math. The canonical mental model for the entire OooCore.
B2 Shen & Lipasti, Modern Processor Design: Fundamentals of Superscalar Processors Ch. 4 (superscalar org), Ch. 5 (superscalar techniques), Ch. 9 (advanced instruction flow), Ch. 10 (advanced register data flow) rename + wakeup/select (## 16,## 18), superscalar widening (## 23) dispatch vs issue distinction; physical-register data-flow; wakeup CAM + select-tree structure; finite-RS / finite-ROB stall taxonomy; multi-port issue contention. Most directly matches our crate decomposition.
