6. Design Principles

Twelve+ non-negotiable invariants. Each governs every phase; violations are bugs, not style choices. Referenced by name in Phases 0–13 (## 12–## 25). Order = priority on conflict (lower number wins).

6.1 The golden model is the oracle

Statement. The sequence of committed architectural states (ArchState: xregs, pc, csr) produced by OooCore MUST equal, value-for-value, the state produced by stepping ooo-isa::execute_functional over the same program. Timing never changes architectural results.

Aspect Detail
Authority ooo-isa functional model; not the timing pipeline
Check point every commit, Phase 12 (## 24) lock-step compare
Granularity per retired instruction: (pc, rd, value) and any store/CSR effect
On mismatch divergence = hard failure; bisect to first divergent commit
  • Tradeoff: lock-step co-sim costs ~10–20% sim throughput; gate behind cosim subcommand / config flag for fast runs.
  • Op implication: any new FU, predictor, or LSQ optimization is "correct" only if cosim stays green over riscv-tests + fuzz corpus.
  • Violation: a sign-extension bug in Mul makes the OoO core commit 0xFFFF_FFFF_... where golden has a positive value → IPC still "looks fine," but every dependent computation is silently wrong. Without the oracle this ships.

6.2 Reverse-order tick (model latches, no same-cycle teleporting)

Statement. OooCore::tick() advances stages in REVERSE pipeline order: Commit → Writeback → Execute → Issue → Dispatch → Rename → Decode → Fetch. A value produced in cycle C is observable to consumers only in cycle C+1.

tick(): commit(); writeback(); execute(); issue(); dispatch(); rename(); decode(); fetch();
        Cycle += 1;   // each stage reads PREV-cycle outputs of the stage feeding it
  • Why: models real pipeline latches; consuming a producer's result in the same cycle it is computed fabricates impossible 0-cycle paths.
  • Tradeoff: the single exception is the BypassNetwork (§7.6 INV-5; built in §19 Phase 7), which intentionally shortens producer→consumer to same/next cycle — but only for already-computed results, never for results computed later in the same tick.
  • Op implication: stage code reads input latches, writes output latches; never reads a latch a later-in-tick stage will overwrite this cycle.
  • Violation: if dispatch() ran before commit(), a ROB slot freed this cycle could be reused this cycle → ROB occupancy understated, IPC inflated, and a structural-hazard stall that real HW would take vanishes.

6.3 Determinism is sacred

Statement. Same (program, ConfigParams, seed) → byte-identical Stats and identical per-cycle trace, on every run, every host, every thread count.

Source of nondeterminism Mitigation
HashMap iteration order use ordered structures (Vec/BTreeMap) in any cycle-visible path
floating IPC accumulation integer counters; ipc = committed as f64 / cycles computed once at end
RNG (fuzzer, replacement) seeded PRNG threaded through ConfigParams
wall-clock / thread races single-threaded stepping; no Instant-driven logic
  • Tradeoff: forbids opportunistic parallelism inside tick(); acceptable — correctness + reproducibility dominate.
  • Op implication: cosim divergence bisection (Phase 12) only works if replay is bit-reproducible; trace dumps must be diffable.
  • Violation: iterating a HashMap<PhysReg,_> during wakeup makes select pick a different "oldest" ready entry across runs → IPC jitters ±2%, and a fuzzer-found divergence cannot be reproduced for debugging.

6.4 Renaming removes only false dependencies

Statement. RenameUnit (RAT + FreeList + PhysRegFile) eliminates WAR and WAW hazards by mapping each arch-reg write to a fresh PhysReg. RAW (true) dependencies are preserved exactly, tracked via PhysRegFile.ready[phys].

  • x0 is NEVER renamed: always maps to a phys reg reading 0; writes to x0 allocate nothing (or a sink).
  • A consumer issues only when both src1_ready && src2_ready (RsEntry); readiness is set by wakeup broadcast of the producer's dst PhysReg.
  • Tradeoff: physical register pressure rises; finite phys_regs (§6.10) becomes a structural limit that can stall rename.
  • Op implication: every DecodedInst with rd != x0 consumes one free PhysReg at rename and frees old_phys only at commit (§6.5).
  • Violation: if rename "optimized away" a RAW by reusing a phys reg too early, a consumer reads a stale value → committed state diverges from golden (catches via §6.1), or worse, intermittently passes.

6.5 Commit is strictly in order (precise exceptions)

Statement. Instructions execute out of order but CommitUnit retires ReorderBuffer entries strictly in program order from head. Architectural state changes only at commit. On a Trap or mispredict at the head, all younger entries are squashed before any side effect.

Rule Consequence
only head may commit a not-done head stalls commit; younger done entries wait
state mutates at commit xregs/csr/memory of a store updated in program order
old_phys freed at commit guarantees no premature free of an in-flight mapping
exception precise PC of faulting inst exact; all older committed, all younger squashed
  • Tradeoff: in-order commit caps retirement at commit_width/cycle; a long-latency head op throttles IPC even with younger work done.
  • Violation: committing a younger instruction past a stalled, faulting older one → exception is imprecise; restart PC is wrong; OS/trap model and golden cosim both break.

6.6 Speculation must be fully recoverable

Statement. Any speculative state (branch direction, target, load order) MUST be restorable to the exact pre-speculation state. At rename of a branch, capture a RenameCheckpoint { rat, free_head }. On mispredict resolution: restore RAT, roll back FreeList to free_head, squash ROB/RS/LSQ entries younger than the branch, redirect fetch to the correct target.

  • Tradeoff: checkpoint storage (RAT snapshot per in-flight branch) costs memory; bound by max in-flight branches or use a single tail-walk rollback.
  • Op implication: misprediction penalty = cycles from branch fetch to redirect-refill; counted in Stats.mispredicts; target accuracy reported per-predictor.
  • Violation: failing to roll back the FreeList leaks physical registers on every mispredict → FreeList drains, rename stalls permanently, sim live-locks at low IPC. Failing to squash LSQ → a squashed speculative store corrupts memory, diverging golden.

6.7 Structural hazards stall, never silently drop

Statement. When a bounded resource is full (ROB full, RS full, no IssuePort, FU busy_until > cycle, FreeList empty, LSQ full), the upstream stage STALLS (back-pressures). It MUST NOT drop, reorder, or fabricate an instruction.

Resource exhausted Stalls
rob_size dispatch
rs_size / no free RsEntry dispatch
FreeList empty (phys_regs) rename
IssuePort busy / FU busy_until issue/select
lsq_size dispatch of loads/stores
  • Tradeoff: stalls model real back-pressure and reduce IPC honestly; resisting the temptation to "make room" keeps numbers credible.
  • Violation: silently overwriting a full ROB slot loses an in-flight instruction → committed count wrong, golden diverges, and the modeled IPC is physically impossible.

6.8 Memory ordering is conservative-by-default

Statement. LoadStoreQueue disambiguation MUST NEVER forward a wrong value. A load checks older stores: exact-address match → store-to-load forward the store value; an older store with unknown address (addr_ready == false) → the load WAITS (conservative) or speculates with a commit-time violation check that squashes on misorder.

  • Loads/stores allocate LSQ entries in program order (rob_idx); ordering is resolved against rob_idx, not issue order.
  • Stores write memory only at commit (§6.5); pre-commit forwarding reads the buffered value.
  • Tradeoff: conservative waiting loses MLP/IPC; speculative load + violation detection recovers it but requires the squash machinery of §6.6.
  • Op implication: memory-order-violation squashes counted separately from branch squashes in Stats.
  • Violation: forwarding from a partially-overlapping or wrong-address store gives a load a bad value → silent data corruption, golden divergence; forwarding past an unknown older store address is a classic real-CPU bug.

6.9 Timing and function are separated

Statement. Functional semantics live ONLY in ooo-isa (execute_functional, decode). The pipeline crates (ooo-frontend … ooo-rob, ooo-core) model only when things happen — latencies, widths, occupancy, structural limits. The two interact only through the cosim oracle.

  • A FU's latency / pipelined affects timing, never the computed ExecResult.value (the value comes from functional semantics).
  • Tradeoff: some duplication (timing pipeline carries decoded info the golden model also decodes) in exchange for a clean correctness boundary.
  • Op implication: adding RV64 ops = touch ooo-isa once; timing tables in ooo-execute reference FuKind, not opcode semantics.
  • Violation: computing a load's result value inside the cache/LSQ timing path (instead of from functional memory) couples a latency model to correctness → a cache-replacement change silently alters results.

6.10 Bounded resources model real hardware

Statement. Every structure is FINITE and sized by ConfigParams: rob_size, rs_size, lsq_size, phys_regs, fetch_width, issue_width, commit_width. No unbounded Vec growth in the steady-state datapath.

  • Tradeoff: finite sizing is what makes IPC realistic; infinite resources would model an impossible ideal machine (useful only as an explicit "ideal" config knob).
  • Op implication: occupancy of each structure is a reported counter; saturation explains IPC loss in stats.
  • Violation: an "elastic" ROB never stalls dispatch → IPC approaches issue_width unrealistically; the model stops predicting real performance and conformance-vs-published-IPC (Phase 13) fails.

6.11 Observable microarchitecture

Statement. Every cycle and every structure is inspectable. trace emits per-cycle, per-stage state (fetch PC, rename mappings, RS occupancy, issued ports, FU activity, commit retirements, squashes). Every event increments a named counter in Stats.

Observable Surfaced via
per-cycle pipeline state oooc trace
IPC, mispredicts, cache misses oooc stats / Stats
golden divergence oooc cosim
occupancy / port contention per-structure counters
  • Tradeoff: trace generation costs throughput; gate verbosity by config; counters are always-on and near-free (integer increments).
  • Violation: an un-counted squash path makes mispredicts understate reality → IPC analysis blames the wrong stage; debugging a divergence with no per-cycle trace is intractable.

6.12 Config-driven, not hard-coded

Statement. All microarchitectural parameters flow from ConfigParams (+ predictor selection PredictorKind); none are magic constants in datapath code. oooc config exposes the knobs; sweeps vary one parameter at a time.

  • Widths, sizes, latency tables, predictor kind, cache geometry, TLB size → all config.
  • Tradeoff: more plumbing up front; pays back as design-space exploration (Phase 11 widening, Phase 13 sweeps) without recompiles.
  • Op implication: a benchmark run is fully described by (program, ConfigParams, seed) (also satisfies §6.3 determinism).
  • Violation: a hard-coded rob_size = 128 makes a "small core" config secretly large → comparative studies are meaningless and reproducibility claims are false.

6.13 No same-instruction double effect

Statement. Each instruction has exactly one RobIdx from dispatch to commit/squash; it consumes resources once, broadcasts its dst PhysReg once at writeback, and frees old_phys once at commit. Wakeup of a PhysReg is idempotent on already-ready entries.

  • Op implication: replaying a writeback (e.g., FU pipeline stage error) must not double-wake or double-free.
  • Violation: double-freeing old_phys returns a live PhysReg to the FreeList → a later rename reuses it, corrupting a still-needed value; golden diverges intermittently and irreproducibly absent §6.3.

6.14 Crate dependency direction is acyclic and downstream-only

Statement. Dependencies flow strictly: ooo-isa → frontend/rename → schedule → execute → mem → rob → core → cli. No upstream or cyclic deps. ooo-isa is no_std + alloc and depends on nothing internal.

  • Tradeoff: some shared types (Cycle, RobIdx, PhysReg, FuKind) live at their owning crate and are re-exported; resist the urge to add a "common" grab-bag crate that everything depends on.
  • Op implication: a change to scheduling cannot force a rebuild of ooo-isa; the golden model stays stable and trustworthy.
  • Violation: a cycle (schedule depending on core) couples the timing engine to the scheduler, making §6.9 separation unenforceable and breaking the layered build.

7. System Invariants

Global correctness contracts. Each holds for every OooCore::tick() regardless of config (ConfigParams), program, or speculation depth. Numbered INV-1..INV-12; severity FATAL = abort + dump, DIVERGE = co-sim mismatch, STALL = backpressure (not a bug). Each: statement / why / enforced where / detection / telemetry / recovery / debug.

7.1 Invariant index

ID One-line statement Severity if violated Primary enforcer
INV-1 Committed arch state ≡ ooo-isa golden model at every commit DIVERGE→FATAL CommitUnit co-sim hook
INV-2 ROB commits strictly in program order FATAL ReorderBuffer head ptr
INV-3 x0 reads 0, never gets a PhysReg FATAL RenameUnit
INV-4 RAT bijection + no PhysReg leak/double-free FATAL Rat/FreeList/old_phys
INV-5 Result of cycle C consumed no earlier than C+1 FATAL (determinism) reverse tick() order + latches
INV-6 Squash everything younger than offender; RAT restored exactly FATAL Flush + RenameCheckpoint
INV-7 Load never reads a younger store; STLF only on exact addr DIVERGE→FATAL LoadStoreQueue disambig
INV-8 ROB/RS/LSQ/FreeList never over-allocated STALL (FATAL if breached) dispatch admission checks
INV-9 Stats counters exact (committed/cycles/mispredicts) FATAL CommitUnit accounting
INV-10 Simulation terminates (ecall/halt or max_cycles) FATAL (livelock) run() bound
INV-11 Predictor speculative-state (history/RAS) restores exactly on squash FATAL (silent accuracy leak) BranchPredictor checkpoint/restore
INV-12 Cache MSHR: ≤1 entry/line in flight; every waiter signalled exactly once FATAL (hang or double-signal) Cache MSHR coalescing

7.2 INV-1 — Architectural equivalence (MASTER)

  • Statement: At each in-order commit of inst I, the resulting architectural state (xregs, pc, written CSRs) produced by the timing model EXACTLY equals execute_functional(&I.decoded, &mut golden) applied to a shadow ArchState advanced in program order. Timing must never change what is computed.
  • Why: Defines correctness. All OoO/speculation/forwarding machinery is an optimization over the golden model; any visible deviation is a bug, not a perf tradeoff.
  • Enforced: CommitUnit::commit() calls a co-sim hook (Phase 12): step golden one inst, compare dst_arch write value + pc + any CSR delta against the committed RobEntry.
  • Detection: field-wise compare golden.xregs[rd] == committed_value, golden.pc == next_pc; first mismatch → record (cycle, rob_idx, pc, arch_reg, expected, got).
  • Telemetry: cosim subcommand logs per-commit OK/DIVERGE; Stats.committed must equal golden inst count at end.
  • Recovery: none — DIVERGE is FATAL; abort with the divergence record. No silent continue.
  • Debug: binary-search (bisection) on cycle of first divergence (Phase 12); re-run deterministically from seed; dump the offending inst's full pipeline lifetime (fetch→commit) via trace.

7.3 INV-2 — In-order commit

  • Statement: ReorderBuffer retires entries only from head, advancing one slot per committed inst; entry at head commits only when done && exception.is_none() (or it is itself the trap). No entry retires before an older one.
  • Why: Precise state + precise exceptions require program-order retirement even though execution is OoO.
  • Enforced: CommitUnit reads strictly from ReorderBuffer.head; circular buffer (head..tail) defines program order; head only ever increments (mod capacity).
  • Detection: assert committed pc sequence is monotone in program order (golden lock-step in INV-1 catches reordering); debug assert entry.idx == head on commit.
  • Telemetry: trace prints commit rob_idx=H pc=... each cycle; commit width ≤ ConfigParams.commit_width.
  • Recovery: FATAL — head pointer corruption is unrecoverable.
  • Debug: dump ROB ring (head, tail, occupancy); check no path mutates head outside CommitUnit.

7.4 INV-3 — x0 hardwired zero

  • Statement: Reads of RegId(0) yield 0; writes to x0 are dropped. x0 is NEVER allocated a PhysReg, never placed on the FreeList, never wakes a dependent.
  • Why: RV64I semantics; allocating x0 would leak phys regs and corrupt wakeup (a spurious ready broadcast).
  • Enforced: RenameUnit short-circuits rd == RegId(0) → no FreeList::alloc, RobEntry.dst_phys = old_phys = PhysReg(0) (a reserved zero reg, always ready=true, value 0); src rs==0 maps to PhysReg(0).
  • Detection: assert FreeList never returns PhysReg(0); assert no ExecResult.dst == PhysReg(0) ever broadcasts wakeup.
  • Telemetry: rename stats: count of x0-write drops (sanity, should be > 0 on real code).
  • Recovery: FATAL.
  • Debug: check PhysRegFile.regs[0] == 0 && ready[0] == true invariant at every tick.

7.5 INV-4 — RAT bijection + PhysReg conservation

  • Statement (two parts):
    • (a) Mapping: every architectural reg x1..x31 maps via Rat.map[i] to exactly one current PhysReg; at most one in-flight producer is the "youngest writer" of each arch reg.
    • (b) Conservation: the multiset {FreeList free regs} ⊎ {RAT-mapped regs} ⊎ {regs pending free in not-yet-committed ROB old_phys} = the full phys_regs set, with NO duplicates (no double-free) and NO loss (no leak). Total count invariant = ConfigParams.phys_regs.
  • Why: Renaming kills WAR/WAW; a leak starves rename (false deadlock), a double-free corrupts two instructions' data (RAW on stale value).
  • Enforced: alloc at rename (FreeList::alloc → new dst_phys, save displaced as RobEntry.old_phys); free at commit (CommitUnit pushes old_phys back to FreeList AFTER retire); on squash, freed regs are the squashed dst_phys, RAT restored from RenameCheckpoint.
  • Detection: periodic (debug build, every N cycles) audit: free.len() + distinct(RAT) + pending_old_phys == phys_regs and the three sets are pairwise disjoint.
  • Telemetry: Stats-adjacent gauge: free-list occupancy histogram; rename stalls due to empty free list.
  • Recovery: FATAL — accounting break is a structural bug.
  • Debug: maintain a per-PhysReg owner tag {Free, MappedTo(arch), PendingFree(rob_idx)}; on any transition assert the legal predecessor state; log offending reg's last 8 transitions.

7.6 INV-5 — Reverse-tick (no same-cycle teleport)

  • Statement: A value produced in cycle C (writeback/exec result, wakeup broadcast, predictor update, ROB alloc) becomes visible to consumers no earlier than cycle C+1. tick() advances stages in REVERSE pipeline order: Commit → Writeback → Execute → Issue → Dispatch → Rename → Decode → Fetch.
  • Why: Models real pipeline latches; load-bearing determinism rule. Forward order would let a result fetched/produced this cycle be consumed downstream the same cycle (combinational teleport), inflating IPC and breaking cycle-accuracy.
  • Enforced: OooCore::tick() fixed call order (reverse); inter-stage state passes through explicit pipeline latches written this cycle / read next; wakeup of ExecResult.dst marks src_ready for issue at C+1 (bypass narrows this to same-cycle issue → next-cycle exec only via BypassNetwork, never violating producer-before-latch).
  • Detection: debug counters: assert no RsEntry issues in the same tick its producing ExecResult is computed unless via the modeled BypassNetwork path; latch double-write assert (one write per latch per cycle).
  • Telemetry: trace per-cycle dump shows each stage's input latch contents; cycle-by-cycle reproducible.
  • Recovery: FATAL (silent IPC inflation otherwise).
  • Debug: flip stage order in a unit test → IPC must change/break; "latch written twice this cycle" assert pinpoints a stage reading post-update state.

7.7 INV-6 — Squash + exact RAT restore on recovery

  • Statement: On branch mispredict resolution or a trap at the ROB head, ALL entries younger than the offending inst are squashed across ROB, RS (Scheduler.entries), and LSQ; the Rat is restored EXACTLY to the RenameCheckpoint captured at the branch's rename; FreeList rolled back; fetch redirected to the correct target.
  • Why: Precise state. A partial squash leaves stale RS entries that wake phantom consumers; an inexact RAT restore makes subsequent renames read wrong phys regs.
  • Enforced: ReorderBuffer flush of (branch_idx+1 .. tail); Scheduler drops RsEntry with rob_idx younger than branch; LoadStoreQueue squashes younger LsqEntry; RenameUnit swaps in RenameCheckpoint.rat and resets free_head; FetchUnit redirected.
  • Detection: post-squash audit: no surviving RS/LSQ/ROB entry has rob_idx younger than offender; RAT equals checkpoint (compare arrays); INV-4 conservation re-checked.
  • Telemetry: Stats.mispredicts++; trace logs squash branch_rob=B target=T flushed_n=K.
  • Recovery: the squash IS the recovery; if audit fails post-squash → FATAL.
  • Debug: dump checkpoint vs live RAT diff; verify mispredict penalty (cycles from resolve to first correct-path fetch) matches expected pipeline depth.

7.8 INV-7 — Memory ordering / store-to-load forwarding

  • Statement: A load returns the value of the youngest older store with an exact matching address (store-to-load forwarding), else the cache value; a load NEVER observes a younger store, and never forwards on a partial/aliased address match. An older store with an unknown (!addr_ready) address blocks (conservative) or, if speculated, a violation is detected at commit and triggers squash.
  • Why: Memory RAW correctness; mis-forwarding silently corrupts loaded data → INV-1 divergence.
  • Enforced: LoadStoreQueue disambiguation: load scans stores older by rob_idx for exact addr match → forward value; unknown older-store addr → wait or speculate; ordering checked against ROB program order.
  • Detection: on commit, re-validate speculative loads (replay address match); mismatch → memory-order violation → squash from the load.
  • Telemetry: counters: STLF hits, STLF blocks (unknown addr), memory-order-violation squashes.
  • Recovery: squash load + younger (uses INV-6 machinery); re-execute non-speculatively.
  • Debug: dump LSQ snapshot {rob_idx, is_load, addr, value, addr_ready} at the load; confirm forwarded source store is the youngest older exact match.

7.9 INV-8 — Finite-resource non-overflow

  • Statement: Occupancy never exceeds capacity for ROB (rob_size), RS (rs_size), LSQ (lsq_size), FreeList (phys_regs - 32 mappable). When any required resource is full at dispatch/rename, the front-end STALLS (bubble) rather than over-allocating.
  • Why: Over-allocation overwrites a live entry → data loss; correct backpressure is the normal mechanism, not an error.
  • Enforced: admission checks BEFORE allocation: dispatch checks ROB+RS+LSQ slots + FreeList non-empty atomically; if any short, no allocation occurs and rename stalls; structural-hazard stall propagates upstream.
  • Detection: debug asserts rob.occupancy <= rob_size (and peers) post-allocation; STALL is expected (not logged as error).
  • Telemetry: Stats-adjacent stall counters per structure (rob_full, rs_full, lsq_full, freelist_empty cycles) — diagnose IPC limiters.
  • Recovery: none needed — stall self-resolves as entries retire/free.
  • Debug: if an assert trips, a stage allocated without checking; correlate stall histogram with IPC to validate sizing (config knobs).

7.10 INV-9 — Exact statistics

  • Statement: Stats.committed = number of golden-validated retired insts; Stats.cycles = tick() count; Stats.mispredicts = squash-on-branch events; Stats.cache_misses = L1/L2 misses. ipc = committed as f64 / cycles. Counters are exact integers, incremented once per event, never on squashed/replayed work.
  • Why: IPC and counters are the product output; double-counting speculative work or replays falsifies results.
  • Enforced: committed incremented ONLY in CommitUnit on successful retire (post-INV-1 check); mispredicts only on confirmed mispredict resolution; speculative re-execution does NOT bump committed.
  • Detection: end-of-run Stats.committed == golden inst count (ties to INV-1); cross-check mispredicts <= total branches.
  • Telemetry: stats subcommand prints full counter set + IPC; bench compares IPC vs published microbenchmark expectations.
  • Recovery: FATAL on mismatch (indicates miscount or INV-1 break).
  • Debug: counter delta per cycle in trace; ensure squash path decrements nothing it didn't commit (it commits nothing).

7.11 INV-10 — Termination

  • Statement: run(max_cycles) always returns: either the program halts (an ecall/EBREAK halt trap retires at ROB head) or cycles reaches max_cycles. No infinite loop, no permanent deadlock.
  • Why: A model that never returns is unusable; a true deadlock (mutual stall with no retirement) is a bug, not termination.
  • Enforced: OooCore::run loop bounded by max_cycles; halt detected at commit; forward-progress guard: if committed does not advance within a watchdog window while ROB non-empty → deadlock FATAL.
  • Detection: watchdog: N consecutive cycles with non-empty ROB and zero commits → flag (distinguishes legit long-latency stall, tunable, from true deadlock).
  • Telemetry: run/stats report termination cause {halt, max_cycles, deadlock}; final cycle + IPC.
  • Recovery: max_cycles bound guarantees return; deadlock → FATAL dump (ROB head entry + why blocked: !done, unmet src, full structure cycle).
  • Debug: inspect ROB head's blocking reason; trace the oldest non-done inst's missing dependency chain to the root stall (e.g., load missing in cache, FU never selected).

7.12 INV-11 — Predictor speculative-state consistency across squash

  • Statement: Speculative predictor structures advanced at fetch (global/path history registers indexing Gshare/TageLite, Ras depth) are checkpointed alongside the RenameCheckpoint and restored to their exact pre-branch value on squash; only a resolved (non-squashed) branch's confirmed direction/target permanently updates the trained tables (Gshare counters, TageLite tag/counter/useful bits, Btb entries).
  • Why: Real predictors speculate ahead of resolution by design — that is the whole point — but the speculative history used to index subsequent predictions must be undone exactly on a wrong path, or "ghost correlations" (MM5) corrupt indexing on the correct path, silently degrading measured accuracy below the ≥90%/≥95% targets (§4.7) without tripping the cosim oracle (predictor state is not architectural, so INV-1 cannot see it).
  • Enforced: BranchPredictor snapshots {history, ras_depth} into predictor-checkpoint storage at each speculative branch fetch (paired with the rename-side RenameCheckpoint, §16); on Flush, restore predictor history + RAS depth to the checkpoint; on resolve, commit the confirmed-direction counter update unconditionally (that part is ground truth regardless of what else squashed).
  • Detection: accuracy regression test — a synthetic mispredict-heavy kernel run with speculative-history-restore forced on vs. off; a measurable accuracy delta flags a leak. Debug assert ras.depth immediately after squash equals ras.depth recorded at the checkpointed branch's fetch.
  • Telemetry: predictor accuracy counters (§28.1 mispredict rate); Ras over/underflow counters (Threat T12).
  • Recovery: FATAL only if the ras.depth structural audit fails; a raw accuracy regression (no structural break) is an S2 performance-class defect (Failure Modes & Recovery), not a divergence — predictor state is never checked by INV-1.
  • Debug: dump the predictor history register value at the mispredicting branch's fetch cycle vs. its value immediately after squash-restore; diff the two.

7.13 INV-12 — Cache/MSHR response singularity (no duplicate or lost wakeup)

  • Statement: For any outstanding cache miss, at most one MSHR entry exists per unique cache-line address in flight per level; when the fill response arrives, every waiter blocked on that MSHR is signalled exactly once — never zero times (a hang, escalating to INV-10/F2) and never more than once (a double-signal corrupts RsEntry.src_ready bookkeeping or re-broadcasts wakeup against a since-reused PhysReg).
  • Why: A non-blocking cache's entire value proposition is hit-under-miss + coalesced in-flight misses (P9, Jouppi MSHR); a coalescing bug either hangs a waiter forever or double-signals wakeup, an INV-4-adjacent corruption vector (a stale-PhysReg wakeup can mark a since-freed/reallocated register ready).
  • Enforced: Cache::mshr keyed by line address; a second miss to an already-outstanding line joins the existing MSHR's waiter list instead of allocating a new entry; on fill, dequeue and signal every waiter exactly once, then retire the MSHR entry.
  • Detection: debug assert MSHR occupancy ≤ mshr_count (structural, INV-8-adjacent admission check); a per-waiter signalled bit asserted false-then-true, never set twice.
  • Telemetry: MSHR occupancy histogram; coalesced-miss counter (hits-under-miss saved); duplicate-signal assert counter (must always read 0).
  • Recovery: FATAL on a double-signal assert; a stuck (never-signalled) waiter surfaces as F2 commit deadlock (Failure Modes & Recovery cross-reference).
  • Debug: dump the MSHR table {line_addr, waiter_list, fill_cycle}; confirm the waiter count matches the actual blocked loads at the time of the hang/duplicate.

7.14 Enforcement summary

When Checks run
Every tick() (debug build) INV-2 head monotonic, INV-3 x0 reg state, INV-5 latch single-write, INV-8 occupancy ≤ capacity, INV-12 MSHR occupancy ≤ mshr_count
Every commit INV-1 golden compare, INV-9 counter bump, INV-4 audit (sampled), INV-7 speculative-load replay
Every squash INV-6 post-squash audit (no younger survivors, RAT==checkpoint), INV-4 conservation, INV-11 predictor history/RAS restore
Periodic (every N cycles, debug) INV-4 full free/RAT/old_phys partition audit
End of run() INV-1 final state, INV-9 committed==golden count, INV-10 termination cause, INV-11 accuracy-regression check, INV-12 zero duplicate-signal count

Release builds compile out per-cycle asserts (zero overhead); INV-1 co-sim and INV-9 counters always on. oooc cosim is the canonical full-strength enforcement mode.

8. Domain Model — RV64IM, Pipeline State, and Timing

The model has two layers that must stay in agreement: the architectural layer (RV64IM ISA state — what software sees) and the microarchitectural layer (pipeline + physical state — how the OoO core computes it). The golden execute_functional defines the meaning; the timing model defines the cycle cost. Co-sim asserts they commit identical architectural states.

8.1 RV64IM Instruction Encoding → DecodedInst

Fixed 32-bit instructions (no compressed C — PhaseDeferred). Six base formats; decode(raw: u32) -> DecodedInst selects format by opcode[6:0], then funct3/funct7, and emits the canonical fields.

Fmt Bits laid out (MSB→LSB) Has rd Has rs1 Has rs2 Imm bits assembled Example ops
R funct7 / rs2 / rs1 / funct3 / rd / op ✓ ✓ ✓ — Add Sub Mul Div
I imm[11:0] / rs1 / funct3 / rd / op ✓ ✓ — sext imm[11:0] Lw Jalr addi
S imm[11:5] / rs2 / rs1 / funct3 / imm[4:0] / op — ✓ ✓ sext {imm[11:5],imm[4:0]} Sw
B imm[12 10:5] / rs2 / rs1 / funct3 / imm[4:1 11] / op — ✓ ✓
U imm[31:12] / rd / op ✓ — — imm<<12 lui auipc
J imm[20 10:1 11 19:12] / rd / op ✓ —

decode() fill rules (one fact per row):

Field Source
op: Opcode (opcode, funct3, funct7) lookup; unknown → Trap::IllegalInstr flagged, op set to a poison sentinel
rd/rs1/rs2: RegId raw bitfields; formats lacking a field set it to RegId(0)
imm: i64 per-format reassembly + sign extension to 64 bits
fu: FuKind Add/Sub→Alu, Mul→Mul, Div→Div, Beq/Jal/Jalr→Branch, Lw/Sw→Agu(addr) then LoadStore(mem)
is_branch op ∈ {Beq, Jal, Jalr} (conditional + unconditional control transfer)
is_load op ∈ {Lw, …loads}
is_store op ∈ {Sw, …stores}

RV64 width notes: 64-bit xregs; *W ops (addw/subw/mulw/divw) operate on low 32 bits then sign-extend bit 31 → bit 63. RegId(0) (x0) is hardwired zero: reads return 0, writes discarded.

8.2 Architectural vs Physical Register Model

Layer State Owner Cardinality
Architectural ArchState{ xregs[32], pc, csr } ooo-isa (golden) 32 logical regs
Physical PhysRegFile{ regs: Vec<u64>, ready: Vec<bool> } ooo-rename ConfigParams.phys_regs (default 128)
Map Rat{ map: [PhysReg;32] } ooo-rename arch→phys, current speculative map
Recycle FreeList ooo-rename phys regs not in any live RAT/in-flight mapping

Renaming removes WAR/WAW (false deps); only true RAW survives, tracked by PhysRegFile.ready[p]. Per renamed instruction with dst rd:

  1. p_new = FreeList.pop(); ready[p_new] = false.
  2. old_phys = Rat.map[rd] (saved into RobEntry.old_phys for rollback/freeing).
  3. srcs read current Rat.map[rs1], Rat.map[rs2].
  4. Rat.map[rd] = p_new.
  5. x0 special-case: never allocate; Rat.map[0] permanently → a phys reg pinned to value 0, ready=true; rd==x0 instructions get a dummy dst that is never freed.

Worked RAT example (initial Rat.map[i] = Pi for i=0..31, free list = P32..P127):

Step Inst rd reads (phys) allocs new RAT entry old_phys
1 add x1,x2,x3 x1 P2,P3 P32 x1→P32 P1
2 mul x4,x1,x5 x4 P32,P5 P33 x4→P33 P4
3 lw x6,0(x4) x6 P33 P34 x6→P34 P6
4 beq x6,x0,L — P34,P0 — — —

Step 2 src1 already points to producer P32 → the dependency chain is captured purely in phys names, no name-matching at issue.

8.3 Pipeline-Stage State Machine + Reverse-Order Tick()

Per-instruction lifecycle (states stored across stage latches + ROB):

FETCHED -> DECODED -> RENAMED -> DISPATCHED(in ROB+RS) -> ISSUED -> EXECUTING
       -> COMPLETED(writeback, ready set) -> [waits at ROB head] -> COMMITTED | SQUASHED

OooCore::tick() advances stages in REVERSE pipeline order so a value produced in cycle C is visible to consumers only in C+1 (modeling latches — load-bearing determinism rule):

Order Stage Reads Writes
1 Commit ROB head(s), done flags ArchState (golden compare), free old_phys, detect trap/mispredict-at-head
2 Writeback FU outputs (ExecResult) PhysRegFile.ready[dst]=true, ROB done, broadcast dst to scheduler wakeup
3 Execute issued ops, BypassNetwork FU busy_until, produce ExecResult, compute mispredict
4 Issue/Select RS ready entries mark RsEntry.issued, bind IssuePort, start FU
5 Dispatch rename output allocate RobEntry + RsEntry; stall if ROB/RS full
6 Rename decode output RAT update, FreeList pop, checkpoint on branch
7 Decode FetchBuffer decode(raw) → DecodedInst
8 Fetch PC, BranchPredictor enqueue into FetchBuffer, predict next PC

After all 8, Cycle.0 += 1, Stats.cycles += 1. Wakeup broadcast in step 2 sets src_ready so the dependent can be selected in step 4 of the same tick only when bypass allows (back-to-back ALU); otherwise next tick.

8.4 Worked Trace — Dependent Sequence

Program (single-issue config for clarity; widths in §23 generalize):

I1: add x1, x2, x3      ; ALU, lat 1
I2: mul x4, x1, x5      ; Mul, lat 3, pipelined
I3: lw  x6, 0(x4)       ; Agu(lat1)+L1 hit(lat 2)
I4: beq x6, x0, L       ; Branch, lat 1, predicted not-taken

RAT after rename per §8.2: I1 dst P32, I2 dst P33 (src P32), I3 dst P34 (src P33), I4 srcs P34,P0.

RS entries at dispatch (issued=false):

ROB op src1 / rdy src2 / rdy dst fu
0 Add P2 ✓ P3 ✓ P32 Alu
1 Mul P32 ✗ P5 ✓ P33 Mul
2 Lw P34* ✗ — P34 Agu/LS
3 Beq P34 ✗ P0 ✓ — Branch

(* I3's address depends on P33; src1 shown as the addr-base phys = P33, dst P34 for the loaded value.)

Cycle-by-cycle (assume all four fetched cycles 1–4, single fetch/issue/commit width; bypass enabled):

Cyc Fetch Decode Rename Disp Issue Exec WB (broadcast) Commit
1 I1 — — — — — — —
2 I2 I1 — — — — — —
3 I3 I2 I1 — — — — —
4 I4 I3 I2 I1 — — — —
5 — I4 I3 I2 I1(P2,P3 rdy) — — —
6 — — I4 I3 — I1 (ALU,1cy) P32 —
7 — — — I4 I2(P32 woke) — — I1
8 — — — — — I2 (Mul c1/3) — —
9 — — — — — I2 (c2/3) — (I2 not done)
10 — — — — — I2 (c3/3) P33 —
11 — — — — I3 AGU — — I2
12 — — — — — I3 addr→LSQ — —
13 — — — — — I3 L1 hit(2cy) — —
14 — — — — — I3 L1 c2 P34 —
15 — — — — I4 — — I3
16 — — — — — I4 branch (resolve) —
17 — — — — — — — I4

IPC for this snippet = 4 committed / 17 cycles ≈ 0.235 — dominated by the serial RAW chain (renaming cannot help a true dependency chain; only ILP across independent chains does).

8.5 Branch Misprediction — Checkpoint, Squash, Restore

At rename of a branch, capture RenameCheckpoint{ rat: Rat (clone of map), free_head }. The branch carries its checkpoint id + predicted target. On resolve in Execute, ExecResult.mispredict set when actual ≠ predicted.

Worked: beq predicted not-taken at fetch (PC+4), wrong-path I5,I6 fetched/renamed speculatively. Branch resolves taken at cycle R:

Step (cycle R, Commit/WB stage detects at ROB head or at resolve) Action
1 Branch RobEntry.mispredict=true; identified as squash point
2 Squash all ROB/RS/LSQ entries younger than branch (rob_idx > branch)
3 RAT restore from the branch's RenameCheckpoint.rat
4 FreeList rollback to checkpoint.free_head (reclaim wrong-path phys regs)
5 Flush FetchBuffer + Decode/Rename latches
6 Redirect Fetch PC = branch target; BranchPredictor.update(pc, taken, target)

Penalty = cycles from branch fetch to redirect-refill = front-end depth. Default config: Fetch→Resolve ≈ 6–8 cycles; Stats.mispredicts++. First useful instruction enters at R+1 (fetch), reaches issue at R+ front-end depth.

8.6 Store-to-Load Forwarding + Disambiguation Violation

LoadStoreQueue holds LsqEntry{ rob_idx, is_load, addr: Option, value: Option, addr_ready, committed }. Program order via rob_idx. AGU produces address → addr_ready=true.

Forwarding (hit):

S: sw  x7, 0(x10)   ; rob 5, addr=0x2000 known, value=V
L: lw  x8, 0(x11)   ; rob 8, addr=0x2000 known

L scans older stores (rob_idx<8) with addr_ready; exact match rob 5 → forward value=V directly, no cache access, value filled, L completes. Partial/size-mismatch overlap → conservative stall (treat as unknown).

Disambiguation (unknown older store) — two policies:

Policy Behavior
Conservative older store addr_ready=false → load waits until that store's address resolves
Speculative load issues, gets cache value; at the store's address resolve, if it matches the speculated load → violation: squash load + younger (re-fetch from load), Stats mem-order-violation++

Stores write memory only at commit (in-order, head of ROB), so a squash before commit never corrupts memory.

8.7 Malformed / Edge Inputs — Modeled Response

Input / condition Detected at Modeled response
Illegal opcode decode() DecodedInst poison + Trap::IllegalInstr; flows to ROB; at commit head → precise trap, squash younger, set CSR cause
Misaligned load/store (addr % size ≠ 0) AGU/LSQ Trap::LoadAddrMisaligned/StoreAddrMisaligned attached to RobEntry.exception; precise at commit
Divide by zero Execute (Div FU) RV64IM spec: no trap — div → all-ones (-1), rem → dividend; FU completes normally
Signed overflow INT_MIN / -1 Execute (Div FU) spec result: quotient = INT_MIN, rem = 0; no trap
ROB full at dispatch Dispatch (step 5) structural-hazard stall: Rename/Decode/Fetch back-pressure freeze; Stats rob-full-stall cycle++
RS full Dispatch same structural stall (whichever resource is exhausted)
FreeList empty (no phys reg) Rename rename stall until a commit frees old_phys
Infinite loop / no progress run() bounded by run(max_cycles); returns Stats when Cycle.0 == max_cycles; co-sim still valid up to last commit
Access to unmapped VA Tlb Trap::LoadPageFault (minimal model); precise at commit

Precise-exception invariant: a trap is raised to architectural state only when the faulting instruction reaches the ROB head; all younger work is squashed, ensuring ArchState exactly matches the golden model's trap point.

8.8 IPC + Counter Definitions

Counter (Stats) Definition
cycles total ticks executed (Cycle.0 at end)
committed instructions retired in-order by CommitUnit (excludes squashed)
mispredicts branches with ExecResult.mispredict resolved
cache_misses L1 (and per-level) misses recorded by ooo-mem
IPC (derived) committed as f64 / cycles

Derived/auxiliary metrics reported by oooc stats: branch prediction accuracy = (branches − mispredicts)/branches; MPKI = mispredicts*1000/committed; cache MPKI = cache_misses*1000/committed; rob-full-stall %, rs-full-stall %, mem-order-violation count, average issue width = committed/active_cycles. Golden co-sim guarantee (§ Phase 12): the sequence of committed architectural states equals the functional model's, independent of any timing parameter — IPC varies with config, correctness does not.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

Architecture Decision Records. Each row is LOCKED for the lifetime of the blueprint; revisiting one requires a new ADR superseding it. Status of all rows: Accepted. Format follows the canonical microarchitecture-RFC convention (cf. gem5 design notes).

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
1 Implementation language Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78 C/C++; Python; Go Tight #[repr] structs for per-cycle state; sum types + exhaustive match model Opcode/FuKind/pipeline-stage state with zero cost; ownership kills aliasing bugs endemic to large mutable timing models (gem5's class of bug). C/C++ memory-unsafe at this mutable-state scale; Python <1M cyc/s (proj 188 already Python); Go has weaker sum types + GC pauses perturb deterministic stepping. C++ wins when the team owns a large gem5/SystemC ecosystem with existing timing models and must integrate with it directly; Python wins for <10K-cycle educational demos where throughput is irrelevant and script-edit cycles dominate (proj 188 use case).
2 Repo structure Cargo workspace, nine ooo-* member crates (ooo-isa, -frontend, -rename, -schedule, -execute, -mem, -rob, -core, -cli) Single monolithic crate; two crates (lib+bin); one crate per pipeline latch Crate boundary == architectural boundary; enforces the dependency DAG (isa→frontend/rename→…→core); each stage independently testable + benchable; prevents back-edges (e.g. ooo-isa cannot see timing). Monolith would let timing leak into the golden model. A monolithic crate wins for a prototype or a solo weekend project where compilation-unit build times matter more than architectural boundaries and the codebase is <5K LoC total. One crate per latch wins in a research group where each PhD student owns exactly one stage and merge conflicts must be minimized.
3 Target ISA RV64IM (RV64I base + M mul/div) Full RV64GC (FD float, A atomic, C compressed); x86-64; ARMv8 Open, fixed-length 32-bit encoding → trivial superscalar fetch/decode; smallest ISA that exercises every OoO mechanism (long-latency Div, Mul, loads, branches). FD/A/C are PhaseDeferred (decode-trap stubs). x86 variable-length defeats wide fetch; ARM license + complexity unjustified for a model. Full RV64GC wins when the simulation must run real Linux workloads or an OS kernel (floating-point, atomics, and compressed encoding are then mandatory). x86-64 wins when the target audience is studying x86 microarchitecture (Intel/AMD pipeline modeling research).
4 Func ↔ timing separation ooo-isa golden model is authoritative for values; timing crates own only scheduling Fuse semantics into the timing path; recompute values during execute execute_functional(&DecodedInst, &mut ArchState) is the single source of architectural truth; timing pipeline reorders when not what. Enables co-sim oracle (ADR 8) + lets a timing bug never corrupt correctness. Fusing them couples two hard problems. Fusing wins for a trace-driven simulator where instructions' values are pre-recorded in the trace and the timing model never needs to recompute them; the oracle comparison overhead is then zero and the code is simpler. Also wins when targeting maximum throughput on very long traces with a trusted value model.
5 Register management Explicit PhysRegFile + Rat + FreeList renaming ROB-based value storage (P6 style); Tomasulo CDB-only (values live in RS) Physical-register-file renaming is the modern (gem5 O3, modern x86/ARM) design; removes WAR+WAW false deps leaving only RAW via PhysReg.ready. RobEntry stores dst_phys/old_phys (pointers), not values → cheap commit (free old_phys). ROB-value scheme bloats RobEntry; CDB-only scheme can't model a separate PRF read port. ROB-value storage (P6 style) wins when modeling an original Pentium Pro–class microarchitecture historically or studying its energy-inefficiency. Tomasulo CDB-only wins for modeling very small in-order-ish cores (embedded, RISC-V E-class) where the RS is tiny and a PRF abstraction adds unnecessary indirection.
6 Scheduler topology Unified Scheduler (single shared issue queue) with per-FuKind select Per-FU distributed reservation stations; separate INT/MEM/FP queues One Vec<RsEntry> + wakeup CAM is simpler to make cycle-accurate + easier to validate; IssuePort/FuKind partitioning captured at select, not by physical RS banks. RsEntry.fu tags the destined unit. Distributed RS is more HW-faithful but multiplies state + complicates the wakeup broadcast for marginal model fidelity. Per-FU distributed RS wins when modeling a design that physically implements separate integer/load-store/FP RS banks with different capacities (ARM Cortex-A76, MIPS R10000 style) and the research question is specifically about queue-size asymmetry or port-specific stall behavior. Separate INT/MEM queues win when memory-order-constraint modeling must be decoupled from integer scheduling for performance at large RS sizes.
7 Time advancement Reverse-pipeline-order tick() (Commit→WB→Execute→Issue→Dispatch→Rename→Decode→Fetch) Event-driven discrete-event queue (gem5 native); forward-order stage stepping Load-bearing determinism rule: stepping consumers before producers means a value made in cycle C is visible only in C+1, modeling latches with zero explicit latch-copy bookkeeping. DES adds a scheduler + nondeterministic tie-break risk; forward order teleports same-cycle results, breaking timing. An event-driven DES queue wins when the simulation contains long periods of empty pipeline (the core is mostly idle) and skipping empty cycles in simulation time is critical for throughput on OS/application workloads measured in billions of cycles. Forward-order stepping wins for a simple in-order pipeline prototype where all stages are guaranteed to have no feedback edges (no bypass, no speculative wakeup).
8 Correctness oracle Lock-step golden co-simulation at commit Trust the timing model; end-of-run state compare only; unit tests only At every commit, the retired architectural delta MUST equal execute_functional on the same instruction stream; divergence aborts with cycle + instruction. Catches reorder/rename/forwarding bugs the cycle they manifest. End-of-run compare can't bisect; unit tests miss emergent interactions. End-of-run state compare wins when co-sim overhead (2–4× slowdown) is unacceptable for long-running throughput benchmarks (>100M cycles) where the correctness confidence already comes from extensive regression suites; only the final arch state diff matters. Unit tests only wins during a first-pass prototype phase where the timing model is barely functional and full pipeline interaction co-sim is premature.
9 Determinism / RNG Single seeded PRNG (ConfigParams.seed); no wall-clock, no thread nondeterminism std::HashMap default-seeded; ambient rand::thread_rng; OS entropy Deterministic replay from (seed, config, program) is mandatory for bisection (ADR 8) + reproducible benchmarks. Use BTreeMap/indexed Vec over HashMap in any iteration that touches timing. Default-seeded hashing or thread_rng makes runs irreproducible. Non-determinism wins for a production cache-replacement simulator where a random eviction policy is the research subject and reproducibility of individual eviction decisions is not the goal — only the aggregate miss-rate distribution matters statistically.
10 Microarch parameterization Runtime ConfigParams (fetch_width, issue_width, commit_width, rob_size, rs_size, lsq_size, phys_regs, predictor) Compile-time const/generics; cargo features per width One binary sweeps design space (oooc config/bench) without recompiling; enables CI matrices + Pareto studies. Compile-time constants would force N binaries; const-generic widths explode monomorphization + hurt build time. Compile-time generics win when the simulation must achieve maximum throughput on a single fixed configuration (e.g. a production silicon validation harness where the RTL spec is frozen) and the ~20% Rust const-generic specialization speedup is worth the build complexity.
11 Default direction predictor Gshare default; TageLite selectable via PredictorKind TAGE-lite mandatory default; static (always-taken/BTFNT); perceptron Gshare (global history XOR pc → 2-bit counters) is the simplest predictor that beats static + is the validation baseline; TageLite opt-in for accuracy studies. Mandatory TAGE complicates Phase 3 bring-up; perceptron adds dot-product cost without pedagogical/fidelity gain here. TAGE-lite as mandatory default wins when the simulation is targeting IPC fidelity on real workloads against a modern core (SPEC CPU2017, server benchmarks) where gshare's ~8–12% MPKI unacceptably inflates mispredict penalties relative to silicon. A perceptron predictor wins for long-history workloads (complex loops, indirect-heavy server code) where geometric-history correlation fails and the dot-product advantage is measurable.
12 Branch target + returns Btb (set-assoc) + Ras BTB only; indirect-branch predictor (ITTAGE) Btb predicts direct targets; Ras handles Jal/Jalr call/return pairs (the dominant indirect case). ITTAGE deferred — general indirect targets are rare in RV64IM micro-bench corpus. ITTAGE wins when the workload is a virtual-machine interpreter, JIT compiler, or OOP dispatch-heavy C++ binary where computed indirect branches (not call/return) constitute >15% of branches and BTB aliasing causes chronic front-end bubbles.
13 Memory hierarchy fidelity Set-associative Cache (L1/L2) + MSHRs + Tlb Fixed single-cycle memory; perfect cache; trace-driven latency Real OoO IPC is dominated by memory stalls; MSHRs model miss overlap (the whole point of an OoO load schedule). Miss latency feeds back into Scheduler wakeup timing. Fixed latency hides the behavior the simulator exists to study. A perfect-cache (single-cycle) model wins for studying pure instruction-level parallelism — rename/ROB/RS sizing, predictor accuracy, or widening sensitivity — where memory is intentionally removed as a confounding variable. A trace-driven latency model wins when the research team has validated hardware traces and wants to decouple cache-hierarchy modeling from microarchitecture modeling for independent publication.
14 Memory disambiguation Conservative LSQ default (load waits on any older unknown-address store); aggressive speculation opt-in Aggressive-by-default (speculate + violation-detect); store-set predictor; no disambiguation Conservative default is always-correct + simple to validate first; LsqEntry.addr: Option<u64> (None = unresolved) drives the wait. Aggressive mode (speculate, detect at commit, squash) is a Phase-8/10 opt-in measured against the conservative baseline. Store-set predictor is future work. Aggressive-by-default wins when the workload has high memory-level parallelism and few genuine store-load conflicts (e.g. a streaming compute kernel where virtually all loads are independent of nearby stores) — conservative waiting serializes memory-level parallelism that could be safely exploited. Store-set prediction wins when the research question is specifically about memory-dependence prediction accuracy and its IPC impact.
15 Store-to-load forwarding Exact-address match in LSQ forwards store value to younger load No forwarding (always go to cache); partial/overlap forwarding Eliminates the common producer→consumer memory dependency stall. Exact-match only (full-width) keeps it simple + correct; partial-overlap forwarding (sub-word/misaligned) deferred — flagged as a stall instead. No forwarding wins when modeling a simple in-order pipeline (where the store commits before the load issues by construction) or a non-blocking store buffer where forwarding logic is not present in the modeled hardware. Partial-overlap forwarding wins when modeling x86 scalar sub-register writes followed by wider reads (a common pattern in x86 ABI code) and the research goal is x86 memory-subsystem fidelity.
16 Index/handle safety Newtypes RobIdx(u16), PhysReg(u16), RegId(u8), Cycle(u64) Bare usize/u16 integers everywhere Type system forbids mixing a ROB index with a phys-reg id with a cycle count — the exact confusion class that silently corrupts a mutable timing model. Zero runtime cost (#[repr(transparent)]). Bare integers win in a throwaway prototype or a C port where the overhead of defining newtypes adds more lines than the codebase has bugs, or where the programmer can guarantee via code review that index-kind confusion will never occur.
17 no_std policy ooo-isa, -rename are no_std + alloc; upper crates std Everything std; everything no_std Keeps the golden model + rename core portable + dependency-light (could embed/reuse elsewhere); std confined to I/O-bearing crates (-core, -cli, cache stats). Full no_std would needlessly fight -cli ergonomics. Full std wins when the project has no embedded deployment target and the team wants unrestricted standard-library access throughout (filesystem, threads, networking for a remote-cosim use case). Full no_std wins when ooo-isa must be deployed as a firmware component or in a WASM sandbox where std is unavailable.
18 Benchmark harness criterion (statistical) + oooc bench for sim-throughput + IPC #[bench] (nightly, unstable); ad-hoc Instant timing Criterion gives stable-toolchain, noise-robust regression detection; report both model speed (target ≥1M simulated cycles/s single-thread) and modeled IPC. Nightly #[bench] violates MSRV/stable policy. Nightly #[bench] wins in a research context where stable API compatibility is irrelevant, the team always uses nightly Rust (e.g., for async/const-generic features), and the lightweight harness suffices for rough throughput checks. Ad-hoc Instant wins for a one-time measurement in an experiment script where criterion's warmup/sampling overhead is disproportionate to the measurement duration.
19 Fuzzing Random RV64IM program generator → co-sim must not diverge cargo-fuzz on raw decode() only; no fuzzing Structured generator emits valid RV64IM sequences (legal regs, terminating loops, mixed FUs); any commit-state divergence vs golden = bug. Raw-byte fuzzing of decode is a useful add-on but can't exercise the OoO pipeline interactions. Raw-byte cargo-fuzz on decode() only wins when the primary threat model is a malformed ELF/binary input crashing the simulator rather than a subtle pipeline correctness bug — i.e., when correctness is assumed and availability/robustness is the target.
20 Differential conformance oracle riscv-tests suite + differential vs Spike (golden ISA sim) Self-checking tests only; hand-written vectors riscv-tests is the ISA reference corpus; Spike is the canonical RV64 golden — oooc cosim diffs committed state against both. Authoritative external truth beyond our own golden model. Self-checking tests only win when the project is a new ISA extension prototype where riscv-tests and Spike do not yet cover the new instructions and hand-written vectors for those instructions are the only available ground truth.
21 CI GitHub Actions: fmt + clippy -D warnings + test + cosim + bench-smoke on the MSRV + stable matrix No CI; local pre-commit hooks only Enforces every gate (build, lint, golden co-sim, conformance subset) per push; MSRV pinned in matrix so 1.78 floor never silently breaks. No CI / local-only hooks win for a single-developer research artifact with <100-line change frequency where CI setup overhead exceeds the lifetime benefit, or for an air-gapped environment where GitHub Actions is unavailable.
22 MSRV policy Pin 1.78; bump only via an ADR + CI matrix update Track latest stable; no MSRV pin Reproducible builds + a stable contract for downstream reuse of the no_std cores. Edition 2021 features suffice; no need to chase new toolchains. Tracking latest stable wins when the team actively uses new Rust features (async, const-generic, SIMD intrinsics) as they stabilize and has no downstream consumers that constrain their compiler version.
23 Dependency posture Minimal, audited deps (criterion dev-only; a small seeded-PRNG; ELF/hex parse; clap for oooc) Heavy frameworks; a discrete-event-sim crate; an existing ISA-decoder crate Fewer deps → faster builds, smaller audit surface, full control of the timing-critical inner loop. Decoder is hand-written in ooo-isa (we own the golden semantics); no external DES crate (ADR 7 rejects DES). A DES framework wins when the simulation must model a heterogeneous SoC (CPU + GPU + interconnect + DRAM controller) where a shared event queue across components is necessary and the complexity is justified. An existing ISA-decoder crate wins for a time-constrained prototype where ISA correctness is needed immediately and the decoder crate has a verified conformance record.
24 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual) GPL; proprietary; single license Standard permissive Rust-ecosystem dual license; maximizes reuse + matches riscv-tests/RISC-V tooling norms. GPL wins when the project is academic-research infrastructure meant to prevent commercial enclosure of the simulator without contribution back. Proprietary wins for an industrial timing model where the competitive advantage lies in the accuracy of the timing model itself and the organization does not wish to disclose it.
25 Trap / privilege scope Minimal trap model + small CsrFile; no full privileged spec Full M/S/U privilege + virtual memory + interrupts Precise exceptions at commit need a Trap enum + a CSR subset (mcause/mepc-class), not a full OS-capable privilege stack. Privileged mode + page-table walks are explicit NON-GOALS. A full privileged spec wins when the simulation must boot Linux or an RTOS — any real OS workload requires M/S/U privilege, a page-table walker, and interrupt delivery. The full spec is necessary when modeling OS scheduling or virtualization overhead.
26 Multi-core / coherence Single-core only SMP + MESI coherence MESI/coherence is project 187's charter; single-core keeps the OoO model focused. Stated NON-GOAL. SMP + MESI wins when the research question is specifically cache coherence traffic, memory-consistency-model violations, false sharing, or NUMA effects — which require at minimum two cores and a coherence protocol to observe. Project 187 covers this scope.

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace, nine ooo-* member crates under crates/, governance at root, phase-aligned test/bench/fuzz/doc trees. Every dir below is annotated with purpose + owning phase (P0..P13). Source-of-truth crate boundaries match §"WORKSPACE CRATES".

10.2 crates/ — the nine member crates

Part 1a of 3 — ISA and frontend crates (ooo-isa, ooo-frontend):

  • crates/
    • ooo-isa/RV64IM decode + architectural state + golden functional model. no_std+alloc. P1
      • Cargo.tomlno deps on other ooo-* crates (the base)
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports Inst, DecodedInst, Opcode, RegId, ArchState; pub fn decode/execute_functional
        • decode.rsraw u32 -> DecodedInst; opcode/funct3/funct7 match; immediate extraction per format
        • opcode.rsenum Opcode {Add,Sub,Mul,Div,Lw,Sw,Beq,Jal,Jalr,...}; FuKind mapping table
        • arch_state.rsArchState{xregs:[u64;32],pc,csr}; x0-hardwired-zero write guard
        • functional.rsexecute_functional(): the golden per-inst semantics (sign/zero ext, M-ext mul/div)
        • csr.rsCsrFile: minimal CSR subset (mstatus/mcause/mepc/mtvec) + Trap enum
      • tests/
        • decode_vectors.rsdecode table vs hand-encoded RV64IM instructions
        • functional_isa.rssum-1..N, fib, div-by-zero trap; arch-state equivalence baselines
    • ooo-frontend/fetch + branch prediction. dep: ooo-isa. P3
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports FetchUnit, FetchBuffer, BranchPredictor, Btb, Ras, Gshare, Prediction
        • fetch.rsFetchUnit (pc, fetch_width, predictor handle) + FetchBuffer in-order queue
        • btb.rsBtb set-associative pc->target; alloc/lookup/evict
        • ras.rsRas return-address stack; push on Jal-link, pop on Jalr-ret; overflow wrap
        • gshare.rsGshare: global history XOR pc -> 2-bit saturating counters
        • tage.rsTageLite: tagged geometric-history tables (PhaseDeferred-richer predictor)
        • predictor.rstrait BranchPredictor{predict,update}; PredictorKind dispatch
      • tests/predict_accuracy.rsloop/branchy kernels; assert >90% gshare accuracy on tight loops

Part 1b of 3 — Rename, scheduling, and execute crates (ooo-rename, ooo-schedule, ooo-execute):

  • crates/
    • ooo-rename/register renaming. dep: ooo-isa. P4
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports Rat, FreeList, PhysReg, PhysRegFile, RenameUnit, RenameCheckpoint
        • rat.rsRat{map:[PhysReg;32]} arch->phys; x0 never renamed
        • free_list.rsFreeList queue of free PhysReg; alloc/free; empty => rename stall
        • phys_regfile.rsPhysRegFile{regs:Vec<u64>,ready:Vec<bool>}; read/write/mark_ready
        • rename.rsRenameUnit: map srcs, alloc dst, record old_phys; removes WAR/WAW
        • checkpoint.rsRenameCheckpoint{rat,free_head}; capture at branch, restore on mispredict
      • tests/rename_hazards.rsWAR/WAW elimination proof; checkpoint round-trip restore
    • ooo-schedule/out-of-order scheduler. dep: ooo-rename. P6
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports Scheduler, RsEntry, IssueQueue, IssuePort
        • rs.rsRsEntry{rob_idx,op,src1,src2,src*_ready,dst,fu,issued}; alloc/dealloc
        • scheduler.rsScheduler{entries}; per-cycle wakeup+select drive; RS-full structural stall
        • wakeup.rsbroadcast completing PhysReg -> set src_ready on matching entries (CAM model)
        • select.rspick oldest-ready entry per IssuePort (age-ordered); port contention
      • tests/wakeup_select.rsRAW chain wakes in order; oldest-first under contention
    • ooo-execute/functional units + bypass. dep: ooo-schedule. P7
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports FunctionalUnit, FuKind, ExecResult, BypassNetwork
        • fu.rsFunctionalUnit{kind,latency,pipelined,busy_until}; Alu/Mul/Div/Branch/Agu/LoadStore
        • bypass.rsBypassNetwork: forward ExecResult to dependents same/next cycle
        • latency.rslatency table: Alu=1, Mul=3, Div=20(iterative), Branch=1, Agu=1
        • writeback.rsWB path: write PhysRegFile, mark_ready, signal wakeup, set RobEntry.done
      • tests/exec_latency.rspipelined vs iterative timing; bypass eliminates WB-latency stall

Part 2 of 2 — Memory through CLI crates (ooo-mem, ooo-rob, ooo-core, ooo-cli):

  • crates/
    • ooo-mem/memory pipeline. dep: ooo-execute. P8/P9
      • Cargo.toml

        • lsq.rsLoadStoreQueue{loads,stores}; LsqEntry alloc, addr/value fill, commit drain
        • disambiguate.rsload vs older stores: exact-match / unknown-addr-wait / speculate+detect
        • forward.rsstore-to-load forwarding on exact-address match
        • cache.rsCache set-assoc + MSHRs + miss latency; CacheLevel L1/L2 chain
        • tlb.rsTlb set-assoc va->pa; miss penalty feeds issue/wakeup timing
      • tests/mem_order.rsSTLF correctness; conservative wait on unknown addr; ordering vs ROB
    • ooo-rob/reorder buffer + commit + recovery. dep: ooo-mem. P5/P10
      • Cargo.toml

        • lib.rsre-exports RobIdx, RobEntry, ReorderBuffer, CommitUnit
        • rob.rsReorderBuffer{entries,head,tail} circular; alloc in-order, ROB-full stall
        • commit.rsCommitUnit: retire up to commit_width/cycle in order; free old_phys
        • exception.rsprecise-exception at head; Trap propagation; squash younger

      • tests/commit_precise.rsin-order retire; precise trap point; flush leaves no leaked phys regs
    • ooo-core/the clocked engine. dep: all above + ooo-isa golden. P0/P2/P11/P12
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rsre-exports OooCore, Cycle, ConfigParams, Stats
        • core.rsOooCore: owns all stage units + ROB/RS/LSQ + config + stats + golden ArchState
        • tick.rstick(): stages in REVERSE order Commit->WB->Exec->Issue->Dispatch->Rename->Decode->Fetch
        • config.rsConfigParams{fetch/issue/commit_width,rob/rs/lsq_size,phys_regs,predictor}
        • stats.rsStats{cycles,committed,mispredicts,cache_misses}; ipc=committed/cycles
        • cosim.rslock-step compare committed arch-state vs ooo-isa golden; divergence detect
      • tests/
        • tick_order.rsno same-cycle teleport: producer C visible only at C+1
        • cosim_smoke.rsfull pipeline run == golden ArchState on sum-1..N
    • ooo-cli/the "oooc" binary. dep: ooo-core (+all). P0..P13
      • Cargo.toml[[bin]] name="oooc"
      • src/
        • main.rsarg parse (clap) -> dispatch subcommand
        • cmd_run.rsrun: load ELF/hex, execute, print exit code + IPC
        • cmd_trace.rstrace: per-cycle pipeline-stage dump (fetch..commit)
        • cmd_stats.rsstats: dump Stats counters + derived IPC/MPKI/MR
        • cmd_cosim.rscosim: run vs golden, report first divergence (pc, cycle, reg)
        • cmd_config.rsconfig: print/override ConfigParams knobs (toml/flags)
        • cmd_bench.rsbench: throughput (cycles/sec) + IPC across kernels

11. Core Schema and Core Types

Normative APIs. Every phase (## 12..## 25) builds against these exact signatures. Grouped by crate (dependency order). Bodies elided (lprompt CRITICAL RULE 10) — 1–2 illustrative lines only.

Cross-cutting invariants (apply to all types):

  • Ownership. OooCore owns every stage unit + structural resource (ROB/RS/LSQ/PRF/caches) by value. No Rc/Arc, no RefCell, no interior mutability. Stages borrow &mut OooCore sub-fields per tick; aliasing-free by construction.
  • Mutation. Per-cycle in-place mutation. ROB/RS/LSQ/FreeList are mutable ring/Vec state; readiness bits flip on wakeup. No allocation in the steady-state hot loop (capacities sized from ConfigParams at construction).
  • Concurrency. Single-threaded, deterministic. No shared mutability across threads; the model is Send but never run concurrently within one core. Determinism is structural (reverse-stage tick), not lock-based.
  • Persistence. Two serialized artifacts only: per-cycle trace (line-oriented or JSONL, trace subcommand) and end-of-run stats dump (stats subcommand, JSON). Internal structs are not persisted across runs; replay is reconstructed from (ELF, ConfigParams, seed).
  • Compatibility. Type names + public signatures are frozen; adding fields requires #[non_exhaustive] on public enums/structs. ConfigParams is forward-compatible via Default.

11.1 ooo-isa — ISA, decode, golden model

no_std + alloc. The functional reference. Owns architectural truth; no timing state.

pub struct Inst { pub raw: u32 }                  // raw 32-bit RV64IM word (no compressed C)

pub struct RegId(pub u8);                         // x0..x31; x0 hardwired zero, never renamed
pub struct CsrFile { /* minimal subset: mcause, mepc, mtval, mstatus */ }

#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum Opcode { Add, Sub, Mul, Div, Lw, Sw, Beq, Jal, Jalr, /* full RV64IM */ }

pub struct DecodedInst {
    pub op: Opcode,
    pub rd: RegId, pub rs1: RegId, pub rs2: RegId,
    pub imm: i64,                                 // sign-extended, format-specific (I/S/B/U/J)
    pub is_branch: bool, pub is_load: bool, pub is_store: bool,
    pub fu: FuKind,                               // routing hint -> ooo-execute
}

pub struct ArchState {
    pub xregs: [u64; 32],                         // xregs[0] MUST always read 0
    pub pc: u64,
    pub csr: CsrFile,
}

pub fn decode(raw: u32) -> DecodedInst;           // pure; total over RV64IM, illegal -> Opcode trap marker
pub fn execute_functional(d: &DecodedInst, st: &mut ArchState); // golden step; advances pc, writes xregs/csr
Type Ownership Mutation Notes
Inst copied through fetch none 4-byte; PhaseDeferred 2-byte C
DecodedInst flows fetch→commit, cloned into RobEntry context immutable post-decode fu selects scheduler routing
ArchState one golden copy owned by OooCore; core also has speculative arch view via PRF execute_functional mutates golden advanced only at commit (## 24)
decode pure fn — deterministic, table-driven
execute_functional pure-effect on st mutates st xregs[0]=0 enforced on every write; sign/zero-ext per op

Golden contract: sequence of ArchState snapshots produced by execute_functional in program order is THE reference; timing model must commit an identical sequence (## 24 co-sim).

11.2 ooo-frontend — fetch + branch prediction

Depends ooo-isa. Owns speculative PC + predictor state; redirected on resolve.

pub struct Prediction { pub taken: bool, pub target: u64 }

pub trait BranchPredictor {
    fn predict(&self, pc: u64) -> Prediction;
    fn update(&mut self, pc: u64, taken: bool, target: u64);   // called at branch resolve (execute/commit)
}

pub struct Btb { /* set-associative pc -> target; tag + valid */ }
pub struct Ras { /* return-address stack; push on Jal(rd=x1), pop on Jalr(rs1=x1) */ }
pub struct Gshare { /* GHR XOR pc -> 2-bit saturating counters */ }
// TageLite: Gshare-compatible direction predictor, multi-table tagged geometric history (Phase 3)

pub struct FetchUnit { /* speculative pc, fetch_width, Box<dyn BranchPredictor>, Btb, Ras */ }
pub struct FetchBuffer { /* bounded in-order queue<(DecodedInst, predicted_target, seq)> */ }
Type Ownership Mutation Concurrency/Persist
FetchUnit OooCore field pc advances/redirects per cycle predictor handle boxed (PredictorKind dyn dispatch chosen at config)
FetchBuffer OooCore field push (fetch) / pop (decode); bounded → backpressure stall drained on flush
BranchPredictor trait obj behind FetchUnit update mutates counters/history replay-deterministic; serialized only as accuracy stat
Btb/Ras/Gshare inside FetchUnit predict read-only, update/push/pop mutate RAS depth fixed; overflow wraps

Resolve contract: predict is read-only (fetch stage); update fires when a branch's true direction/target is known (## 18/## 22), then fetch is redirected and younger ops squashed.

11.3 ooo-rename — RAT, free list, physical register file

Depends ooo-isa. Removes WAR/WAW; leaves true RAW tracked via PhysReg readiness. old_phys accounting originates here and is the spine of recovery.

pub struct PhysReg(pub u16);                      // index into PhysRegFile

pub struct Rat { map: [PhysReg; 32] }             // arch reg -> current phys reg; entry 0 pinned to a zero-phys

pub struct FreeList { /* FIFO queue of free PhysReg + head index */ }

pub struct PhysRegFile {
    regs: Vec<u64>,                               // value per phys reg
    ready: Vec<bool>,                             // src_ready source-of-truth; set on writeback
}

pub struct RenameCheckpoint { rat: Rat, free_head: usize }  // snapshot at each branch rename

pub struct RenameUnit { /* maps rs1/rs2 via Rat; allocs dst from FreeList; records old_phys */ }

Rename step (per instr, Phase 4):

  1. src1 = rat.map[rs1]; src2 = rat.map[rs2] (x0 → pinned zero-phys, always ready).
  2. new = free_list.pop() for rd≠x0; old_phys = rat.map[rd]; rat.map[rd] = new; mark ready[new]=false.
  3. Stash old_phys in the RobEntry → freed at commit; restored at flush.
  4. On a branch: capture RenameCheckpoint{ rat.clone(), free_list.head }.
Type Ownership Mutation Recovery role
Rat RenameUnit remapped per rename restored wholesale from RenameCheckpoint on mispredict
FreeList RenameUnit pop@rename, push@commit(old_phys) / rollback@flush free_head rewound to checkpoint
PhysRegFile OooCore (shared read by execute/scheduler) regs set @ writeback, ready flips @ writeback values survive flush (only mapping rolls back)
RenameCheckpoint one per in-flight branch (small Vec) immutable once taken sized ≤ max in-flight branches

Invariant: phys_regs ≥ 32 + rob_size so the free list never starves with a full ROB. x0 maps to a single read-only zero-phys (never in free list).

11.4 ooo-schedule — reservation stations / unified scheduler

Depends ooo-rename. Owns the wakeup CAM + select logic.

pub struct RsEntry {
    pub rob_idx: RobIdx,
    pub op: Opcode,
    pub src1: PhysReg, pub src2: PhysReg,
    pub src1_ready: bool, pub src2_ready: bool,   // wakeup target bits
    pub dst: PhysReg,
    pub fu: FuKind,
    pub issued: bool,                             // set on select; entry freed after issue
}

pub struct Scheduler { entries: Vec<RsEntry> /* + age order + per-FU select */ }
pub struct IssueQueue { /* age-ordered window feeding IssuePorts */ }
pub struct IssuePort { /* binds a select slot to one FuKind */ }

Wakeup + select contract (Phase 6, the load-bearing OoO mechanic):

  • Dispatch: entry created with src*_ready = PhysRegFile.ready[src*] snapshot (already-ready sources start true).
  • Wakeup: a completing/bypassed PhysReg p is broadcast; every RsEntry with src1==p sets src1_ready=true (same for src2). CAM-style match over the window.
  • Select: per IssuePort/FuKind, pick the oldest entry with src1_ready && src2_ready && !issued && FU free; set issued=true.
  • Freeing: issued entries are reclaimed; window occupancy bounded by rs_size → structural stall when full.
Type Ownership Mutation Concurrency
Scheduler OooCore insert@dispatch, wakeup-set, select-clear per cycle single-thread; wakeup ordering deterministic by age then port
RsEntry inside Scheduler.entries readiness bits flip via wakeup squashed if rob_idx younger than a flushed branch

11.5 ooo-execute — functional units + bypass

Depends ooo-schedule. Owns latency tables + writeback path.

#[non_exhaustive]
pub enum FuKind { Alu, Mul, Div, Branch, Agu, LoadStore }

pub struct FunctionalUnit {
    pub kind: FuKind,
    pub latency: u32,                             // cycles to result
    pub pipelined: bool,                          // true: accept 1/cycle; false (Div): iterative, blocks
    pub busy_until: Cycle,                         // next-free cycle for non-pipelined FU
}

pub struct ExecResult {
    pub dst: PhysReg, pub value: u64,
    pub rob_idx: RobIdx,
    pub mispredict: bool,                          // branch FU sets on direction/target miss
}

pub struct BypassNetwork { /* forward ExecResult.dst+value to same/next-cycle dependent issues */ }

Latency table (default; ConfigParams-tunable): Alu 1 (pipelined), Mul 3 (pipelined), Div 20+ (iterative, non-pipelined), Branch 1, Agu 1, LoadStore = AGU+cache (## 21).

Type Ownership Mutation Notes
FunctionalUnit Vec in OooCore (port-bound) busy_until advances on iterative ops pipelined FU has no busy gate
ExecResult produced at result cycle consumed by writeback + bypass drives PhysRegFile.ready[dst]=true + wakeup broadcast
BypassNetwork OooCore per-cycle forward set, cleared each tick lets back-to-back dependent ALU ops avoid full writeback latency

Writeback contract: at result cycle, PhysRegFile.regs[dst]=value, ready[dst]=true, broadcast dst to Scheduler wakeup, mark RobEntry.done, set mispredict if branch resolved wrong.

11.6 ooo-mem — load/store queue + cache/TLB

Depends ooo-execute. Owns disambiguation + store-to-load forwarding.

pub struct LsqEntry {
    pub rob_idx: RobIdx,
    pub is_load: bool,
    pub addr: Option<u64>,                        // None until AGU computes
    pub value: Option<u64>,
    pub addr_ready: bool,
    pub committed: bool,                          // stores write memory only after commit
}

pub struct LoadStoreQueue { loads: Vec<LsqEntry>, stores: Vec<LsqEntry> }  // age-ordered

pub struct MemRequest  { pub addr: u64, pub is_write: bool }
pub struct MemResponse { /* value (load), latency, hit/miss */ }

pub struct Cache     { /* set-assoc, MSHRs, hit/miss latency, levels via CacheLevel */ }
pub struct CacheLevel { /* one L1/L2 stage: sets, ways, line size, latency */ }
pub struct Tlb       { /* set-assoc va -> pa */ }

Disambiguation + forwarding contract (Phase 8):

  • A load scans older stores (lower age) in LoadStoreQueue.
  • Exact-address addr match on a ready older store → store-to-load forward (value taken from store, no cache access).
  • An unknown older-store addr (addr_ready==false) → load waits (conservative) or speculates + detects a violation at commit (squash & replay).
  • Stores write Cache only when committed==true (post-ROB, in order).
Type Ownership Mutation Persist
LoadStoreQueue OooCore insert@dispatch, addr/value fill@AGU, drain@commit squashed on flush (younger than branch)
Cache/CacheLevel/Tlb OooCore line fill/evict, MSHR alloc/free miss/hit counts → Stats
MemRequest/MemResponse transient none latency feeds back into issue/wakeup timing (## 21)

11.7 ooo-rob — reorder buffer + commit

Depends ooo-mem. Circular buffer; in-order commit + precise exceptions.

pub struct RobIdx(pub u16);                       // ring index; also age key across RS/LSQ

pub struct RobEntry {
    pub pc: u64,
    pub dst_arch: RegId, pub dst_phys: PhysReg,
    pub old_phys: PhysReg,                         // FREED to FreeList at commit (recovery accounting)
    pub done: bool,                                // set at writeback
    pub exception: Option<Trap>,
    pub is_store: bool,
    pub mispredict: bool,
}

pub struct ReorderBuffer { entries: Vec<RobEntry>, head: usize, tail: usize }  // circular, in-order
pub struct CommitUnit { /* commit up to commit_width/cycle from head while done; handle trap/mispredict */ }

Commit contract (Phase 10): at head, if done:

  • exception.is_some() → set precise trap (mepc=pc), squash all younger, redirect to handler.
  • mispredict → flush younger, restore RenameCheckpoint, redirect fetch.
  • else retire: RAT already current; push old_phys to FreeList; commit store to cache; advance ArchState (golden compare). dst_phys stays mapped (it is now the architectural value).
Type Ownership Mutation Invariant
ReorderBuffer OooCore alloc@tail (dispatch), free@head (commit), squash on flush full ROB → dispatch stall (structural hazard)
RobEntry inside ROB ring done/exception/mispredict set by execute/mem old_phys is the ONLY freed reg at commit
CommitUnit OooCore retires ≤ commit_width/cycle stops at first not-done head (in-order)

11.8 ooo-core — clocked engine, config, stats

Depends on all crates + ooo-isa golden model.

pub struct Cycle(pub u64);

pub struct ConfigParams {
    pub fetch_width: u32, pub issue_width: u32, pub commit_width: u32,
    pub rob_size: usize, pub rs_size: usize, pub lsq_size: usize,
    pub phys_regs: usize,                          // ≥ 32 + rob_size
    pub predictor: PredictorKind,                  // Gshare | TageLite
}

pub struct Stats {
    pub cycles: u64, pub committed: u64,
    pub mispredicts: u64, pub cache_misses: u64,
}                                                  // ipc = committed as f64 / cycles

pub struct OooCore { /* FetchUnit, RenameUnit, Scheduler, FUs, LoadStoreQueue, Cache, Tlb,
                        ReorderBuffer, CommitUnit, PhysRegFile, ConfigParams, Stats,
                        Cycle, golden ArchState */ }

impl OooCore {
    pub fn tick(&mut self);                        // one cycle; REVERSE stage order
    pub fn run(&mut self, max_cycles: u64) -> Stats;
}
