tick() reverse-order rule (load-bearing determinism): advances stages Commit → Writeback → Execute → Issue → Dispatch → Rename → Decode → Fetch. A value produced in cycle C is visible to consumers only in C+1 — models real pipeline latches, no same-cycle teleporting.

Type Ownership Mutation Persist/Compat
Cycle OooCore global counter +1 per tick monotone; trace timestamp
ConfigParams constructed once, immutable during run none Default + builder; config subcommand dumps; forward-compatible
Stats OooCore counters bump per cycle/event stats subcommand → JSON; ipc derived
OooCore top-level owner of all state full per-cycle mutation not serialized; replay from (ELF, ConfigParams, seed) (## 24)

run contract: loop tick() until max_cycles or halt; co-sim (## 24) compares each committed RobEntry's effect against execute_functional on the golden ArchState; any divergence aborts with the offending (Cycle, RobIdx, pc).

11.9 Type relationship summary

DecodedInst --rename--> {dst_phys, old_phys, src1/src2 PhysReg} --dispatch--> RobEntry + RsEntry (+ LsqEntry if mem)
RsEntry.src*_ready  <--wakeup-- ExecResult.dst  <--writeback-- FunctionalUnit  <--select-- Scheduler
RobEntry.old_phys   --commit--> FreeList            RenameCheckpoint --flush--> Rat (+ FreeList head)
ArchState (golden)  ==co-sim==  committed RobEntry effects        Stats.ipc = committed / cycles

All indices (RobIdx, PhysReg, RegId) are newtype-wrapped u8/u16 for type safety — no bare integers cross stage boundaries; the compiler rejects index-kind confusion.

12. Phase 0 — Workspace, golden-model smoke, and the clocked-core skeleton

Bootstrap phase: nothing simulates timing yet. Goal is a compiling nine-crate workspace, the type shells that the rest of the blueprint fills in, a green CI gate, and one end-to-end smoke path that functionally (golden-model) executes a real RV64I program. The smoke proves the decoder + execute_functional + ELF/hex loader + CLI wiring before any pipeline exists.

12.1 Objectives

  • Stand up Cargo workspace, nine ooo-* member crates (deps per the crate spine, §10.2), edition 2021, MSRV 1.78.
  • Define shell types: Cycle, ConfigParams, Stats, OooCore + tick()/run() stubs; empty stage units (one per pipeline stage) holding no logic.
  • Wire CI (fmt → clippy -D warnings → build → test → MSRV check), Makefile, Dockerfile, ELF/hex loader.
  • Ship smoke: oooc run sum.hex decodes + functionally executes "sum 1..N", prints x10 result + placeholder Stats.
  • Establish cosim scaffold (golden ArchState stepper) and per-cycle trace line format (frozen schema, no fields populated yet).

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-1 (Rust), ADR-3 (RV64IM scope), ADR-11 (workspace layout), R7 (Spike / execute_functional oracle seeded here), P11 (riscv-tests tohost/fromhost convention wired in loader).

# Module / file Work
1 Cargo.toml (root) [workspace] members=[ "ooo-isa", … "ooo-cli" ], shared [workspace.package] (edition/MSRV/license), [workspace.dependencies] for thiserror, clap, object (ELF), bitflags.
2 ooo-isa/src/lib.rs no_std+alloc; declare Inst, DecodedInst, Opcode, RegId(u8), ArchState{xregs:[u64;32],pc,csr}, CsrFile stub; decode(raw:u32)->DecodedInst (Phase-1 fill, here returns Opcode::Add placeholder + a hand-decoded subset: addi,add,bne,jal for the smoke); execute_functional(&DecodedInst,&mut ArchState) covering that subset.
3 ooo-frontend … ooo-rob Each: pub struct <StageUnit> (e.g. FetchUnit, RenameUnit, Scheduler, FunctionalUnit, LoadStoreQueue, ReorderBuffer) with ::new() + #[allow(dead_code)] fields per spine signatures; no behavior. Index newtypes PhysReg(u16), RobIdx(u16) defined in their home crates.
4 ooo-core/src/config.rs ConfigParams w/ defaults: fetch_width=4, issue_width=4, commit_width=4, rob_size=192, rs_size=64, lsq_size=48, phys_regs=128, predictor=PredictorKind::Gshare. PredictorKind enum. Default impl.
5 ooo-core/src/lib.rs Cycle(u64); Stats{cycles,committed,mispredicts,cache_misses} + fn ipc(&self)->f64; OooCore{ cfg, stats, cycle, golden:ArchState, <stage units> }. tick(&mut self) = self.cycle.0+=1; (stub). run(&mut self,max:u64)->Stats: smoke mode loops execute_functional over golden state until trap/max, returns Stats.
6 ooo-isa/src/loader.rs load_hex(&str)->(Vec<u8>, u64 entry) (Verilog-style @addr/word lines) + load_elf(&[u8]) via object crate (RV64 little-endian, PT_LOAD segs into a sparse Memory). Memory{ read_u32/read_u64/write_* }.
7 ooo-cli/src/main.rs clap subcommands: run, trace, stats, cosim, config, bench. Phase 0: run/stats/config real; trace/cosim/bench print "unimplemented (PhaseN)" stub.
8 CI/build .github/workflows/ci.yml; Makefile (fmt lint build test msrv smoke); Dockerfile (rust:1.78-slim, build --release, smoke as healthcheck).

Trace schema (frozen now, emitted later):

cyc=<u64> stage=<FE|DE|RN|DI|IS|EX|WB|CM> pc=0x.. op=<Opcode> rob=<RobIdx> phys=<PhysReg> ev=<alloc|wakeup|issue|wb|commit|squash>

12.3 Deliverable

cargo build --workspace + cargo test --workspace green; oooc run examples/sum.hex --n 100 prints x10=5050 and a placeholder Stats{cycles:0,committed:100,...,ipc:NaN-guarded→0.0}; CI passes on Linux + macOS; docker build . smoke succeeds.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • 9 crates compile; dep graph matches spine (no cycles; ooo-core depends on all + ooo-isa).
  • oooc run examples/sum.hex --n 100 → x10=5050; --n 1 → x10=1; --n 0 → x10=0.
  • cargo fmt --check clean; cargo clippy --workspace -- -D warnings clean.
  • cargo +1.78.0 build --workspace succeeds (MSRV gate).
  • oooc config --dump prints default ConfigParams as TOML/JSON; round-trips via --config f.toml.
  • Smoke wall-time < 50 ms; binary built --release.

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Input Expected
examples/sum.hex sum 1..N loop (addi/add/bne) x10 = N(N+1)/2
tests/loader_hex.rs well-formed @80000000 hex bytes at right addrs, entry=0x80000000
tests/loader_elf.rs minimal RV64 static ELF PT_LOAD mapped, e_entry honored
tests/config_roundtrip.rs dump→parse ConfigParams structural eq
Malformed: bad_endian.hex odd-length word LoaderError::Truncated
Malformed: bad_magic.elf wrong e_machine (≠RISC-V) LoaderError::NotRiscv
Malformed: gap.hex @addr past mem cap LoaderError::OutOfRange, no panic

12.6 Pitfalls

  • x0 writeback: execute_functional must drop writes to RegId(0); smoke loop counter on x0 silently corrupts to nonzero → wrong sum. Root cause: missing zero-guard. Debug: assert xregs[0]==0 post-step.
  • Hex address vs offset confusion: loader storing at file offset not @addr → entry PC reads garbage. Debug: dump Memory map, diff vs expected base 0x80000000.
  • ipc() div-by-zero: cycles=0 in Phase 0 → NaN/inf in JSON. Guard if cycles==0 {0.0}.
  • Workspace-vs-crate clippy scope: CI running per-crate misses cross-crate lints; always --workspace.
  • Sign-extension of imm: i64 immediate from bne/addi must sign-extend 12-bit field; unsigned shift loops forever (no branch taken). Debug: print decoded imm for the branch.
  • no_std leak: ooo-isa accidentally pulling std (e.g. format! via println!) breaks the contract silently until a no_std downstream consumer fails. Gate with #![no_std] + CI --no-default-features build.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Golden stepper target ≥ 50M functional instr/sec (smoke is throwaway, but sets the cosim baseline; OoO timing model later targets ≥ 1M cycles/sec).
  • Keep DecodedInst ≤ 32 bytes (POD, Copy) — it lives in hot FetchBuffer/RsEntry paths later; oversize now propagates.
  • Memory sparse (page-keyed BTreeMap/HashMap<u64,[u8;PAGE]>), not flat Vec<u8> of 2^64.
  • --release mandatory for any timing number; debug build ~10–20× slower.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • --n 0 → empty sum, x10=0, loop body skipped (branch-not-taken at entry).
  • Empty/zero-length hex → LoaderError::Empty, exit code 2, no panic.
  • ELF with BSS (p_memsz>p_filesz) → zero-fill tail.
  • PC unaligned (not %4) on run → Trap::InstrAddrMisaligned surfaced, not panic.
  • Overflow on N=2^32-ish sum: wraps in u64 (architectural wrapping add), documented as expected.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Decoder returns placeholder for unhandled op in smoke DecodedInst.op unexpected → panic!("unimpl op @pc") (Phase 0 only) enumerate ops Phase 1
Loader maps nothing (silent empty) post-load assert entry∈mapped LoaderError, exit 2
run never terminates (bad branch) max_cycles/instr cap (default 10M) abort, print "no halt", exit 3
CI flake on MSRV pinned toolchain in CI + rust-toolchain.toml reproducible

12.10 Operational Notes

  • make smoke is the canonical local gate; CI runs the same target.
  • Logging via tracing env-filter; default oooc quiet, RUST_LOG=oooc=debug for loader/decode dumps.
  • oooc config --dump > base.toml is the seed for all later --config runs; commit examples/configs/{small,default,wide}.toml.
  • Exit codes: 0 ok, 2 input/loader error, 3 no-halt/cap, 101 panic (bug). Frozen across all phases.
  • Trace format string is now frozen — later phases populate fields but MUST NOT change column names/order (downstream trace/diff tooling depends on it).

13. Phase 1 — RV64IM decode, architectural state, and the functional golden model

Build ooo-isa into the canonical RV64IM functional oracle: bit-exact decode() for every R/I/S/B/U/J encoding, ArchState with x0-zero + CSR subset, and execute_functional() with trap semantics. All later timing-accurate stages co-sim against this; any divergence is a timing-model bug, never an ISA bug. Ref: RISC-V ISA manual Vol I (unpriv), riscv-tests rv64ui/rv64um, Spike golden sim.

13.1 Objectives

  • decode(raw: u32) -> DecodedInst total over all 2^32 words; unknown → Opcode::Illegal (no panic).
  • Full RV64I base (incl. *W 32-bit ops) + M-extension (MUL/MULH/MULHU/MULHSU/DIV/DIVU/REM/REMU + W variants).
  • execute_functional() = single architectural step: read ArchState, mutate xregs/pc/CSR, return/raise Trap. Bit-exact vs Spike.
  • Precise trap conditions: illegal-inst, ecall/ebreak, misaligned load/store/fetch, div-by-zero + signed overflow (RISC-V: no trap, defined results).
  • no_std + alloc; deterministic, side-effect-free except &mut ArchState + a Memory trait.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-3 (RV64IM ISA scope — RVC and F/D extensions excluded), ADR-4 (no privileged mode beyond M), R7 (Spike insns/*.h — authoritative RV64IM semantic reference for every opcode), P11 (riscv-tests tohost convention drives the acceptance test loop).

Module (ooo-isa/src/) Contents
inst.rs Inst{raw:u32}; field accessors opcode()=raw&0x7f, rd(), funct3(), rs1(), rs2(), funct7()
opcode.rs enum Opcode — all RV64IM + Illegal; enum FuKind re-exported to schedule/execute
reg.rs RegId(u8); RegId::ZERO; is_zero()
imm.rs per-format sign-extended immediate extractors → i64
decode.rs decode() dispatch: opcode field → format → funct3/funct7 → Opcode
state.rs ArchState{xregs:[u64;32], pc:u64, csr:CsrFile}; read_reg/write_reg (x0 sink)
csr.rs CsrFile subset: cycle,instret,mtvec,mepc,mcause,mstatus,mscratch
trap.rs enum Trap{IllegalInst(u32),Breakpoint,EnvCall,LoadMisaligned(u64),StoreMisaligned(u64),FetchMisaligned(u64)}; cause codes
exec.rs execute_functional(); ALU/MUL/DIV ops; mem via trait Memory
mem.rs trait Memory{ fn load(&self,addr:u64,size:u8)->Result<u64,Trap>; fn store(&mut self,addr:u64,size:u8,val:u64)->Result<(),Trap>; }

Immediate extraction (sign-extend to i64) + decode dispatch (opcode field → format):

I-imm = sext(raw[31:20], 12)                                   S-imm = sext({raw[31:25],raw[11:7]}, 12)
B-imm = sext({raw[31],raw[7],raw[30:25],raw[11:8],0}, 13)       U-imm = sext({raw[31:12],12'b0}, 32)
J-imm = sext({raw[31],raw[19:12],raw[20],raw[30:21],0}, 21)     // top 20 bits, low 12 zero (U)

opcode[6:0]          format  family
0110011              R       ALU reg-reg; funct7=0000001 => M-ext
0111011              R       *W 32-bit ALU + M-ext W
0010011 / 0011011    I       ALU imm / *IW; shifts use funct7+shamt
0000011              I       loads LB/LH/LW/LD/LBU/LHU/LWU (funct3=size+sign)
0100011              S       stores SB/SH/SW/SD
1100011              B       branches BEQ/BNE/BLT/BGE/BLTU/BGEU
1101111              J       JAL
1100111              I       JALR
0110111 / 0010111    U       LUI/AUIPC
1110011              I       ECALL/EBREAK/CSR ops

Semantics highlights (*W ops: operate on low 32 bits, sign-extend result to 64); SLLI/SRLI/SRAI use 6-bit shamt for RV64; M-ext: MULH* upper 64 of 128-bit product (i128/u128), DIV by 0 → -1 (all-ones), REM by 0 → dividend, signed overflow INT_MIN/-1 → quotient INT_MIN, rem 0.

13.3 Deliverable

ooo-isa crate + a thin in-crate harness run_functional(elf|hex, &mut Memory) -> ArchState: decodes + executes in program order to a halt sentinel (ecall with riscv-tests tohost convention). Drives riscv-tests rv64ui-p-* + rv64um-p-* to completion, reporting pass (tohost==1) or failing test number. Wired into oooc cosim later as the oracle.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • 100% of rv64ui-p-* (~40 tests) + rv64um-p-* (~8 tests) PASS (tohost==1).
  • decode() round-trips: never panics on any of 1M random u32; ≥ matched cases byte-identical to a reference decode table.
  • Differential vs Spike on 10k random valid instructions: post-state xregs/pc identical.
  • x0 invariant: after any write to RegId::ZERO, xregs[0]==0.
  • Functional throughput ≥ 20M inst/s single-thread (no timing model overhead).

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Input Expected
tests/fixtures/sum_1_to_n.hex loop sum 1..100 x10 == 5050
tests/fixtures/mul_div_edge.hex MUL,MULHU,DIV by 0, INT_MIN/-1 x-regs match table; no panic
tests/fixtures/illegal.hex 0x00000000, 0xFFFFFFFF Trap::IllegalInst raised
tests/fixtures/misaligned.hex LW/SW to addr%4!=0 Trap::LoadMisaligned/StoreMisaligned
tests/fixtures/ecall_halt.hex ECALL w/ tohost clean halt, Trap::EnvCall
rv64ui-p-*, rv64um-p-* upstream ELFs all PASS
malformed: truncated hex, odd-length word, reserved funct3/funct7, RV32-only shamt[5]=1 adversarial encodings Illegal raised, never panic

13.6 Pitfalls

  • B/J imm bit-scramble: branch/jump immediates are bit-permuted, not contiguous; off-by-one in B-imm[12:1] reconstruction → wrong target, passes simple tests, fails taken branches. Debug: unit-test each imm extractor vs known encodings.
  • *W not re-sign-extending: forgetting to sign-extend the 32-bit result to 64 bits → high garbage; ADDIW/SLLW silently wrong. Debug: assert bit63==bit31 post-op.
  • Shift amount width: RV64 uses 6-bit shamt; masking with &0x1f (RV32) truncates SLLI x,x,32. Use &0x3f for 64-bit, &0x1f for *W.
  • MULHSU signedness: mixed signed×unsigned — wrong cast (i128*i128 vs i128*u128 as i128) gives wrong upper word. Compare against Spike on negative operands.
  • x0 as source vs dest: x0 reads 0 always; writes discarded — but reading rs1=x0 then writing rd=x0 must still advance pc + retire (NOP). Forgetting commit path → hang.
  • Div-by-zero / overflow = no trap: RISC-V defines results, does NOT trap (unlike x86). A panic! on a/0 is a bug; must return -1 / dividend.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • decode() on hot path of every co-sim step → keep branchless field extraction; match on opcode compiles to jump table.
  • Avoid heap in decode: DecodedInst is Copy, fixed-size, no Vec/Box. M-ext i128 mul ~few ns; acceptable.
  • Memory trait dispatch: monomorphize generic <M: Memory> rather than dyn in the functional loop. Target ≥ 20M inst/s ⇒ < 50 ns/inst including decode + execute + Memory call.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • pc wrap at u64 max; misaligned pc (low bit set via JALR masks bit0 to 0 per spec).
  • AUIPC/JAL relative to current pc (capture pc before increment).
  • JALR clears LSB of target: (rs1+imm) & ~1.
  • Sign vs zero-extend loads: LB/LH/LW sign-extend, LBU/LHU/LWU zero-extend; LD full 64.
  • SLT/SLTU signed vs unsigned comparison; BLTU/BGEU unsigned; CSR read-only cycle/instret, writes to RO CSR → Illegal.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause First check
riscv-test N fails, others pass one opcode/imm wrong dump pc-trace, diff vs Spike at first divergent pc
infinite loop, no halt branch target/cond wrong or x0 write hang log branch resolutions
panic on random fuzz unhandled funct7/funct3 not mapped to Illegal exhaustive opcode-space test
*W results wrong only for large vals missing 32→64 sign-extend assert bit63==bit31
M-ext MULH* upper word off wrong i128 cast targeted neg-operand fixtures

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI: cargo test -p ooo-isa runs fixtures + downloaded riscv-tests (cached in Docker layer; pinned commit).
  • Spike diff harness gated behind --features spike-diff (needs spike binary) — skipped if absent, warns.
  • decode() + execute_functional() are the frozen API; later phases depend on exact signatures from §11 Core Types. Any change here ripples to all nine crates.
  • Logging via log crate at trace for per-inst dump; off by default for throughput.
  • Determinism: no clock/RNG/global state; same input ⇒ same ArchState, prerequisite for Phase 12 replay.

14. Phase 2 — In-order pipeline scaffold and the reverse-order tick

14.1 Objectives

  • Build OooCore clocked engine in ooo-core: stage structs, pipeline latches, Cycle counter, Stats.
  • Implement tick() advancing stages in REVERSE order (Commit→WB→Execute→Mem→Decode→Fetch) — the load-bearing determinism rule (result in cycle C visible only at C+1).
  • Working 5-stage in-order baseline (F/D/EX/MEM/WB) with stall + bubble logic; no OoO, no rename, no ROB yet — these are the timing skeleton that later phases bolt onto.
  • Drive functional execution via ooo-isa::execute_functional; the in-order pipe is pure timing — committed arch state MUST match the golden model bit-for-bit.
  • Produce real IPC via Stats { cycles, committed } for straight-line + branch programs.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-7 (tick-driven model — the reverse-order Commit→Fetch discipline is locked here), ADR-9 (single configurable core vs multi-core), R1 (gem5 fetch.cc — fetch-width loop), R5 (gem5 commit.cc — in-order commit loop), P2 (Tomasulo / H&P §C.6 — in-order baseline context for later OoO transition).

  • Files: ooo-core/src/{lib.rs, tick.rs, latch.rs, stage.rs, stats.rs, config.rs, inorder.rs}. inorder.rs is the Phase-2 baseline; later phases replace its body stage-by-stage.
  • Latch: struct Latch<T> { cur: Option<T>, next: Option<T> }; write_next(T), read()→&Option<T>, advance() moves next→cur then clears next. One latch per stage boundary: if_id, id_ex, ex_mem, mem_wb. Latches are the ONLY cross-stage state — stages never read each other's locals.
  • Stage payloads: IfId{ inst: DecodedInst, pc: u64 }, IdEx{ d: DecodedInst, rs1v, rs2v: u64 }, ExMem{ d, alu: u64, addr: u64 }, MemWb{ d, result: u64 }.
  • Tick loop (reverse): each substage reads its INPUT latch .cur, writes its OUTPUT latch .next. After all 5 run, advance() every latch. Reverse order means a younger stage's stall is observed before an older stage tries to push. Stage-driver signatures (no bodies). tick() invokes the five substages in reverse program order (Commit→Fetch), then advances every latch and increments the cycle counter; each substage reads its input latch .cur and writes its output latch .next:
// ooo-core/src/tick.rs — per-cycle stage driver (signatures only)
fn tick(&mut self);            // one cycle: substages in reverse order, then advance_latches() + cycle += 1
fn commit_wb(&mut self);       // MemWb.cur → arch writeback; committed += 1
fn mem(&mut self);             // ExMem.cur → MemWb.next  (data memory access)
fn execute(&mut self);         // IdEx.cur  → ExMem.next  (ALU / branch resolve)
fn decode_read(&mut self);     // IfId.cur  → IdEx.next   (decode + regfile read)
fn fetch(&mut self);           // pc        → IfId.next   (sequential / predicted)
fn advance_latches(&mut self); // next → cur for if_id, id_ex, ex_mem, mem_wb
  • Stall/bubble: stall: [bool; 4] per boundary. WB never stalls (single-cycle here). A stalled stage re-presents cur (does not advance that latch) and inserts a NOP bubble (IdEx::nop()) downstream. In-order RAW hazards (no forwarding yet) → stall Decode until producer reaches WB (≤2 bubbles). Branch resolved in EX → squash 1–2 wrong-path IfId/IdEx entries, redirect PC.
  • Fetch: sequential PC + 4; on a taken branch known only at EX, fetch the fall-through speculatively then squash (statically predict not-taken in Phase 2; real BPred is Phase 3).
  • Functional drive: WB stage calls execute_functional(&d, &mut self.golden) ordered by program order (guaranteed in-order here), so timing model's commit stream == golden stream by construction.
  • Stats: committed++ per WB retire; ipc = committed as f64 / cycles. tick.rs::run(max_cycles) loops until ECALL/halt sentinel or budget.

14.3 Deliverable

In-order cycle-accurate timing model: oooc run --inorder prog.hex executes RV64IM, prints final arch regs + Stats{cycles, committed, ipc}; committed state == golden; reproducible cycle counts.

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Golden equivalence committed arch state bit-exact vs execute_functional on all fixtures
IPC, no-hazard straight-line → 1.00 ± 0 (steady state, ignoring fill/drain)
RAW stall cost back-to-back dependent ALU = +2 cycles (no forwarding)
Determinism identical cycles across 100 reruns, same seed/config
Throughput ≥ 5 M sim-cycles/sec single-thread (release)

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expects
sum1toN.hex x10=N(N+1)/2; cycles ≈ 5 + 6·iters (loop branch penalty)
raw_chain.hex 8 dependent adds → cycles = 5 + 8·3 (stall pattern)
nop_sled.hex 100 NOPs → IPC→1.0, committed=100
taken_branch.hex beq taken → 2-cycle squash, PC redirect correct
malformed_truncated.hex odd byte count → Err(LoadError::Truncated), no panic
bad_opcode.hex illegal raw → decode traps, pipeline drains cleanly

14.6 Pitfalls

  • Same-cycle teleport: running stages in FORWARD order lets a value produced in EX feed Decode same cycle → IPC inflated, non-deterministic. Root: forgot reverse order. Debug: trace shows result consumed in cycle of production.
  • Double-advance latch: calling advance() inside a stalled stage drops an instruction (silent loss → committed < expected). Debug: assert committed == golden_step_count.
  • Bubble vs stall confusion: inserting a bubble AND advancing the stalled latch skips the held inst. Keep "hold input" and "emit NOP output" as the single stall action.
  • x0 writeback: writing WB result to x0 corrupts golden equivalence. Guard if rd.0 != 0.
  • Branch squash off-by-one: squashing the branch itself (not just younger) loses the branch's own retire → committed undercount.

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • Latch<T> of small Copy payloads — avoid heap; DecodedInst is Copy. No per-cycle allocation in the tick loop.
  • tick() hot loop: keep stage methods #[inline], branch-light; Stats updates are plain counters. Target ≥5 M cyc/s; profile with bench subcommand.
  • Reverse-order calls are direct method dispatch (no trait objects) in baseline; defer dynamic predictor dispatch to Phase 3.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty program → 0 committed, cycles = drain only, IPC = 0 (avoid NaN: define IPC=0 when cycles=0).
  • Single instruction → fill/drain dominates (5 cycles, IPC 0.2) — document steady-state vs transient.
  • Self-referential RAW on x0 → never stalls (x0 always ready).
  • Branch as last instruction → ensure pipeline drains without re-fetching past halt.
  • Back-to-back stores then load same addr → Phase 2 has no LSQ; in-order MEM serializes, correctness holds.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery/Action
committed ≠ golden steps per-commit assert in WB dump diverging PC, halt
cycles non-deterministic rerun hash mismatch bisect to first differing cycle in trace
infinite stall (deadlock) watchdog: cycles>max & committed stuck report stalled stage + held inst
IPC > 1.0 in-order sanity bound check flag forward-order regression

14.10 Operational Notes

  • oooc trace --inorder dumps per-cycle 5-stage latch contents (PC + opcode per stage) for visual pipeline diagram debugging.
  • --max-cycles N budget guard prevents runaway loops in CI.
  • Config knobs inert this phase except none of OoO sizes (rob/rs/lsq) used yet; ConfigParams parsed but baseline ignores width>1 (asserts issue_width==1).
  • This scaffold is the substrate for Phases 3–11: each later stage method swaps in its real unit while the latch/tick contract stays fixed.

15. Phase 3 — Fetch and branch prediction

Crate: ooo-frontend. Builds the speculative front end feeding the in-order scaffold (Phase 2): FetchUnit + FetchBuffer deliver fetch_width decoded insts/cycle; Btb/Ras supply targets at fetch; the direction predictor (Gshare → TageLite) supplies taken/not-taken; branch resolution at Execute redirects fetch and charges the misprediction penalty.

15.1 Objectives

  • Speculative fetch: fetch_width insts/cycle, sequential + predicted-redirect PC, stop block at first predicted-taken branch.
  • Btb set-associative pc → target; Ras for Jalr/call-return; BranchPredictor trait with Gshare (gshare baseline) then TageLite (tagged, multi-history).
  • Resolve at branch Execute → Prediction-vs-actual compare → redirect fetch, flush wrong-path, account misprediction-penalty cycles.
  • Measure: direction accuracy ≥92% gshare / ≥95% TageLite on riscv-tests; BTB hit-rate, RAS hit-rate counters in Stats.

15.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-10 (Gshare as default, TageLite optional — lock enforced here), ADR-6 (BTB set-associative, no correlating BTB), P6 (McFarling, Combining Branch Predictors — Gshare directional accuracy), P3 (H&P §C.3 — BTB and RAS design), R1 (gem5 fetch.cc — predictor-driven PC redirect reference), R8 (ChampSim branch plugin shape — structural reference for BranchPredictor trait).

Files under ooo-frontend/src/:

File Owns
fetch.rs FetchUnit { pc, fetch_width, predictor, btb, ras }, FetchBuffer (in-order ring of (DecodedInst, predicted_target, ghist_snapshot))
btb.rs Btb set-assoc (default 512 sets × 4-way), tag/index split, LRU victim, lookup/update
ras.rs Ras 16-entry circular stack, push/pop, top-of-stack pointer, overflow wrap
predict.rs BranchPredictor trait, Prediction { taken, target }
gshare.rs Gshare { ghr: u32, pht: Vec<u8> } — GHR XOR pc-bits → 2-bit saturating counter
tage.rs TageLite — 1 base bimodal + 3–4 tagged tables, history lengths {4,16,64,128}, geometric; tag+useful-bit; provider/altpred
resolve.rs BranchResolution { pc, taken, target, ghist_snapshot } → update, redirect signal
  • Fetch step (per cycle, in tick() fetch stage): for i in 0..fetch_width: decode(mem[pc]); if branch → btb.lookup(pc) for target, predictor.predict(pc) for direction; Jal uses BTB/decoded imm, Jalr ra-return uses Ras::pop, call (Jal/Jalr with rd=x1/x5) Ras::push(pc+4); on predicted-taken, set next pc = target, stop filling block. Snapshot GHR into each FetchBuffer entry.
  • Gshare predict: idx = (ghr ^ (pc >> 2)) & mask; taken = pht[idx] >= 2. Update: shift GHR <<1 | taken; bump/decrement 2-bit counter (sat 0..3).
  • TageLite predict: index each tagged table by pc ^ folded(ghist[0..Li]); longest tag-matching table = provider; on miss fall to base bimodal; allocate on mispredict in longer table with useful-bit reset; periodic useful-bit decay.
  • Resolution (Phase 7 Execute hook, scaffolded here at in-order Execute): compare resolved vs predicted; mispredict = (taken≠pred.taken) || (target≠pred.target); on mispredict redirect FetchUnit.pc = correct, squash FetchBuffer + younger pipeline stages, restore GHR from snapshot, predictor.update, btb.update.

15.3 Deliverable

oooc run --predict gshare|tage <elf> runs the in-order pipeline with speculative fetch; oooc stats reports IPC, direction accuracy, BTB/RAS hit-rate, mispredict count + penalty cycles; oooc trace shows predicted-target + resolve-redirect per cycle. --predict none (always-not-taken) baseline for comparison.

15.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Gshare direction accuracy (riscv-tests + dhrystone-ish loop bench) ≥92%
TageLite direction accuracy, same workloads ≥95%
BTB hit-rate on taken branches ≥90%
RAS hit-rate on returns ≥97%
Mispredict penalty accounting exactly fetch→resolve stage distance, counted in Stats.mispredicts-weighted cycles
Architectural correctness committed ArchState still matches golden model bit-exact (speculation never corrupts retired state)
Determinism identical accuracy/IPC across runs for fixed config+seed

15.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
tests/fixt/loop_1m.hex (back-edge taken 1M×) gshare ≥99.9% accurate after warmup; BTB hit 100% steady-state
tests/fixt/call_ret_depth8.hex RAS 100% on balanced calls; one mispredict on overflow at depth>16
tests/fixt/alternating_branch.hex (T,N,T,N…) gshare 2-bit ~50%, TageLite ≥99% (captured by history)
tests/fixt/indirect_switch.hex (Jalr jump-table) BTB target mispredicts until trained; accuracy logged
Malformed: btb_size=0 config reject at ConfigParams validate, error not panic
Malformed: Ras pop on empty returns BTB/fall-through target, no underflow panic; counter ras_underflow++

15.6 Pitfalls

  • GHR speculative-update vs commit-update mismatch → recovery restores wrong history. Root cause: updating GHR at fetch but rolling back from a stale snapshot. Debug: log ghist_snapshot per FetchBuffer entry, diff at resolve.
  • BTB aliasing (untagged/partial-tag) gives wrong target for different PCs same index → wrong-path fetch that still "resolves correct direction". Debug: full-tag assert mode comparing stored vs request PC.
  • RAS corruption on wrong-path call/return: speculative push/pop on squashed path leaves TOS wrong. Fix: snapshot RAS TOS+top entry in FetchBuffer, restore on flush.
  • Counting a not-taken correctly-predicted branch as a "BTB hit" inflates hit-rate. Define BTB hit only for taken+target-correct.
  • TageLite useful-bit never decaying → tables saturate, no new allocation, accuracy plateaus low. Debug: track allocation-failure rate; add periodic reset.
  • Same-cycle predict-then-redirect teleport violates reverse-order tick(): redirect must take effect at C+1. Symptom: IPC too high, zero penalty. Assert penalty ≥ stage distance.

15.7 Performance Concerns

  • Predict on critical fetch path: Gshare O(1); TageLite 4-table lookup + fold — keep folded-history incremental (rotate-XOR), not recomputed per access.
  • Btb as flat Vec indexed array, not HashMap, to keep sim throughput ≥1M cycles/s.
  • Avoid per-inst heap alloc in FetchBuffer; fixed ring buffer sized fetch_width × depth.
  • GHR snapshot per FetchBuffer entry is a u32 copy — cheap; TAGE full-history snapshot only the folded values needed.

15.8 Edge Cases

  • pc unaligned / fetch crossing page (TLB miss) → stall fetch, no false branch.
  • Branch as last inst in fetch block when predicted-taken: truncate block, do not over-fetch.
  • Jalr with rd=x0 and rs1=x1 = return → RAS pop; rd=x1 = call → push; both = swap.
  • Self-loop (Beq to own PC) — BTB/predictor must not livelock; resolve still advances.
  • fetch_width=1 (degenerate) and very wide (8) both honor block-truncation invariant.
  • x0-targeted branch / zero-displacement: still resolved, never special-cased into corruption.

15.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause Action
Accuracy < target but correct results predictor warmup / aliasing dump per-PC counters, check index hash
Committed state diverges from golden wrong-path effect leaked past flush verify squash covers all younger FetchBuffer + pipeline latches
IPC implausibly high redirect applied same cycle enforce C+1 latch; check penalty counter > 0
RAS hit-rate low on balanced code push/pop on speculative path not restored add RAS snapshot+restore
Nondeterministic accuracy iteration over HashMap / uninit GHR use ordered arrays, zero-init GHR

15.10 Operational Notes

  • Config knobs: predictor: PredictorKind::{None,Gshare,TageLite}, btb_sets, btb_ways, ghr_bits, ras_size, tage_tables, history-length set.
  • oooc config --show prints resolved predictor params; oooc bench --predict-sweep runs all predictors over fixtures emitting accuracy CSV.
  • Logging: OOOC_LOG=fetch traces predict/redirect; counters surface in Stats for the run/stats subcommands.
  • Wires forward: resolution hook is finalized in Phase 7 (Execute/Branch FU) and recovery integrated with RenameCheckpoint/ROB squash in Phase 10; this phase resolves at in-order Execute as a stand-in.

16. Phase 4 — Decode/rename (RAT, free list, physical register file)

Renames the in-order decoded stream (Phase 3 FetchBuffer → Phase 2 decode latch) into a true-RAW-only stream over PhysRegs, eliminating WAR/WAW. Implements ooo-rename: Rat, FreeList, PhysRegFile, RenameUnit, RenameCheckpoint. Still in-order single-wide here (superscalar widening = Phase 11); the PhysRegFile becomes the value store consumed by Phase 6 wakeup/select and Phase 7 writeback.

16.1 Objectives

  • Map arch regs → PhysReg via Rat; allocate dst from FreeList; record old_phys (for commit-time free + recovery).
  • Eliminate WAR/WAW false deps → only true RAW remain, tracked by PhysRegFile::ready[].
  • x0 never renamed: reads → fixed P0 (always-ready, value 0); writes → discarded (no alloc, no free).
  • Capture RenameCheckpoint at every branch (snapshot RAT + free-list head) for Phase 10 recovery.
  • Stall (no bubble corruption) when FreeList empty; deterministic, allocation order = program order.

16.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-5 (RAT + FreeList + PhysRegFile scheme — explicitly locks out history-buffer rename), P2 (Tomasulo register renaming in §C.6 and §C.7 — theoretical foundation), P1 (H&P §C.6 — Tomasulo RAT / PRF design), R2 (gem5 rename.cc — RAT update and free-list draw reference), R10 (BOOM docs — RAT sizing cross-check for phys_regs default).

File (in ooo-rename) Adds
src/phys.rs PhysReg(u16); const P0 = arch-zero phys reg; NUM_PHYS = ConfigParams.phys_regs (default 128).
src/rat.rs Rat { map: [PhysReg;32] }; fn lookup(RegId)->PhysReg, fn remap(RegId,PhysReg)->PhysReg(returns old), fn snapshot()->[PhysReg;32], fn restore(&[PhysReg;32]).
src/free_list.rs FreeList { q: VecDeque<PhysReg>, head: usize }; fn alloc()->Option<PhysReg>, fn free(PhysReg), fn head()->usize, fn rollback_to(usize). Init: P_{32..NUM_PHYS} free; P0..P31 = initial RAT mapping (arch i → Pi).
src/prf.rs PhysRegFile { regs: Vec<u64>, ready: Vec<bool> }; fn read(PhysReg)->u64, fn write(PhysReg,u64), fn is_ready, fn set_ready, fn mark_busy (on alloc). P0 pinned value=0 ready=true.
src/checkpoint.rs RenameCheckpoint { rat:[PhysReg;32], free_head:usize }; taken at branch rename.
src/rename.rs RenameUnit { rat, free_list, prf }; fn rename(&mut self, d:&DecodedInst) -> Result<RenamedInst, RenameStall>.

RenamedInst (new): { op, src1:PhysReg, src2:PhysReg, dst:PhysReg, old_phys:PhysReg, rob_idx_placeholder, ckpt:Option<RenameCheckpoint>, is_branch, is_load, is_store }. Feeds Phase 5 dispatch → RobEntry{dst_phys, old_phys} and Phase 6 RsEntry{src1,src2,dst,src*_ready}.

rename() algorithm (strict order, prevents self-WAR):

  1. Read sources first: src1 = rat.lookup(rs1), src2 = rat.lookup(rs2) (rs==x0 → P0).
  2. If d.is_branch → ckpt = Some(RenameCheckpoint{ rat.snapshot(), free_list.head() }).
  3. Allocate dst: if rd==x0 → dst=P0, old_phys=P0 (no alloc). Else dst = free_list.alloc()? (None → return RenameStall); old_phys = rat.remap(rd, dst); prf.mark_busy(dst) (ready=false).
  4. Emit RenamedInst. On stall: do NOT mutate RAT/free-list/PRF (idempotent retry next cycle).

Reverse-order tick() wiring (Phase 2 contract): rename stage reads decode latch, writes rename→dispatch latch; on full downstream latch or RenameStall, stage stalls and back-pressures decode (no instruction dropped/duplicated).

16.3 Deliverable

ooo-rename crate compiling + a rename stage integrated into OooCore::tick() that converts the in-order decoded stream to a PhysReg-renamed stream with old_phys recorded and per-branch checkpoints. CLI oooc trace --stage=rename <prog> dumps per-cycle arch→phys mappings + free-list occupancy. No commit/free yet (PRF grows until Phase 10 frees old_phys).

16.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • WAR/WAW: in a hand-crafted WAW chain (add x1..; add x1..) the two writes get distinct PhysReg dsts; no false dep edge appears.
  • x0: every rd==x0 write yields dst==P0, no FreeList::alloc call (assert alloc count unchanged); every rs==x0 read yields src==P0.
  • Allocation is in program order + 1:1 with non-x0 dst-producing insts; free_list.len() decreases monotonically pre-commit.
  • Checkpoint count == branch count; each ckpt.free_head == free-list head at that branch.
  • RAT invariant: ∀ arch i, rat.map[i] points to the phys reg holding the most recent definition (verified vs a reference shadow map).

16.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
rename/waw_chain.hex 3 writes to x1 → 3 distinct dsts; old_phys[k]==dst[k-1].
rename/war.hex add x3,x1,x2; add x1,x4,x5 → 2nd's dst ≠ x1's src phys; no stall.
rename/x0.hex add x0,x1,x2; lw x5,0(x0) → dst=P0, base src=P0; alloc-count==1 (lw only).
rename/exhaust.hex program needing >free count → exactly N−free stalls, then resumes (no deadlock w/o commit only if N≤free).
rename/ckpt.hex 4 branches → 4 checkpoints; snapshot RAT matches pre-branch RAT byte-for-byte.
Malformed: rename/bad_regid.hex (RegId>31) decoder rejects upstream; rename asserts rd<32 (panic in debug, RenameError in release).

16.6 Pitfalls

  • Source-after-dest remap (self-WAR): renaming dst before reading srcs breaks add x1,x1,x2 (src1 would see new phys). Root cause: ordering. Fix: srcs first (step 1 < step 3). Debug: trace shows src1 phys == dst phys.
  • Freeing dst at rename instead of commit: returns phys reg still live → corruption on later read. old_phys is freed at commit (Phase 10), never at rename. Symptom: cosim divergence on a value reused after a WAW.
  • x0 entering FreeList: if P0 ever recycled, a real value overwrites zero. Pin P0: never in free queue, prf.regs[0] immutable. Debug: assert prf.read(P0)==0 each cycle.
  • Checkpoint = shallow ref not snapshot: storing &rat instead of a value copy → restore sees post-branch state. Use [PhysReg;32] by-value snapshot. Symptom: recovery (Phase 10) restores wrong map.
  • Stall mutates state: partial alloc on a stall double-allocs on retry → free-list leak/double-use. Make rename() fully idempotent; allocate last, after the ? early-return.
  • Sizing phys_regs < 32: must be ≥ 32 + rob_size to never deadlock single-wide; assert at config load.

16.7 Performance Concerns

  • Rat::lookup/remap = O(1) array index; snapshot = 32-elem copy (64 B) — cheap, but per-branch ⇒ keep RenameCheckpoint Copy/[PhysReg;32] stack-allocated, no heap.
  • FreeList = VecDeque<PhysReg> (ring); alloc/free O(1) amortized; avoid Vec::remove(0).
  • PhysRegFile ready: Vec<bool> → consider BitVec (128 bits = 16 B) for cache-friendly Phase 6 wakeup scan. Target: rename stage < 30 ns/inst, no allocation on the steady-state hot path.

16.8 Edge Cases

  • rd==rs1==rs2 (add x5,x5,x5): srcs both = old phys of x5, dst = fresh phys, old_phys = old x5 — all consistent.
  • Branch with rd!=x0 (e.g. jal x1): checkpoint taken and dst allocated same cycle; checkpoint reflects RAT before this dst remap (snapshot in step 2, remap in step 3).
  • Store/load: is_store→no dst alloc (rd irrelevant) but srcs (base+data) renamed; lw→dst allocated normally.
  • Free-list head wrap in ring buffer: free_head for checkpoint must be a logical alloc-count cursor, not a physical ring index (else rollback math wrong).

16.9 Failure Analysis

  • Free-list leak (occupancy drifts down across runs): a freed-at-rename or never-freed old_phys. Detect: invariant free + live_in_RAT + in_flight_old_phys == NUM_PHYS checked each commit (wired Phase 10); assert here that pre-commit live==alloc_count.
  • Double-alloc (two RobEntries share a dst PhysReg): non-idempotent stall. Detect: mark_busy on an already-busy reg → assert.
  • Wrong old_phys (commit frees a still-live reg): RAT remap returned stale value. Detect: old_phys == prior rat.map[rd] assertion.
  • Cross-check: shadow functional ArchState (golden) — value read through final RAT mapping must equal golden arch reg at every commit (full check Phase 12).

16.10 Operational Notes

  • oooc trace --stage=rename columns: cycle | pc | inst | rs1→p | rs2→p | rd→p(old) | free# | ckpt?.
  • oooc stats adds: rename_stalls, phys_alloc, checkpoints, freelist_min_occupancy.
  • Debug feature --features rat-assert enables per-cycle RAT/free-list/PRF invariant checks (off in bench builds for throughput).
  • ConfigParams.phys_regs knob (default 128) tunable via oooc config; warn if < 32 + rob_size.
  • Determinism: alloc order = program order, no RNG; same config+ELF ⇒ identical rename trace (prereq for Phase 12 replay).

17. Phase 5 — Dispatch into the reorder buffer

Dispatch is the in-order frontier of the OoO machine: the last stage where program order is still explicit. It binds each renamed DecodedInst to a RobEntry (program-order slot, recovery metadata), reserves an RsEntry (and LsqEntry for mem ops), and stalls atomically on any structural hazard. After dispatch, instructions execute out of order; the ROB is what lets commit restore program order. Reference: gem5 O3CPU rename→IEW dispatch; H&P Ch. 3 (Tomasulo + ROB).

17.1 Objectives

  • Allocate one RobEntry per inst per cycle (up to issue_width) in program order into the circular ReorderBuffer (head/tail); record pc, dst_arch, dst_phys, old_phys, is_store, done=false.
  • Co-allocate RsEntry into Scheduler for all ops; co-allocate LsqEntry (load or store) for mem ops.
  • Enforce atomic, all-or-nothing structural-hazard stall: ROB full ∨ FreeList empty ∨ RS full ∨ LSQ full ⇒ stall this inst + all younger (in-order).
  • Maintain ROB occupancy invariants; expose is_full()/is_empty()/alloc()/free_head() API.
  • Deliverable: finite ROB with correct stalls, proven by a ROB-full stall test.

17.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-5 (ROB as the in-order commit queue — old_phys carried in RobEntry), ADR-8 (execution-driven: dispatch allocates ROB before execute), P2 (H&P §C.8 — ROB and Tomasulo's algorithm with precise interrupts), R5 (gem5 rob.hh / commit.cc — circular buffer structure reference), R2 (gem5 rename.cc — old_phys threading from rename into dispatch).

File Adds
ooo-rob/src/lib.rs RobIdx(u16), RobEntry, ReorderBuffer
ooo-rob/src/rob.rs circular buffer: head/tail/count, alloc/peek_head/free_head/is_full/is_empty/squash_after
ooo-core/src/stages/dispatch.rs DispatchUnit::tick() — drains rename latch, allocates ROB+RS(+LSQ)
ooo-schedule/src/lib.rs Scheduler::alloc(RsEntry)->Result<(),Full>, is_full()
ooo-mem/src/lib.rs LoadStoreQueue::alloc(LsqEntry)->Result<RobIdx,Full>, is_full()
  • Ring buffer: entries: Vec<RobEntry> len = rob_size; head = oldest (commit), tail = next free; count distinguishes full (count==rob_size) from empty (count==0) — never rely on head==tail alone.
    pub fn alloc(&mut self, e: RobEntry) -> Option<RobIdx> {
    if self.is_full() { return None; }
    let idx = RobIdx(self.tail as u16);
    self.entries[self.tail] = e;
    self.tail = (self.tail + 1) % self.cap; self.count += 1;
    Some(idx)
}
  • Dispatch order per cycle: for each inst in rename latch (oldest first): (1) probe all needed resources; (2) if any full → stall here, leave inst + younger in latch; (3) else commit allocation: rob.alloc → RsEntry{rob_idx, src1/2 ready from PhysRegFile.ready, dst, fu, issued:false} → if mem, lsq.alloc(LsqEntry{rob_idx,is_load,addr:None,addr_ready:false,...}).
  • Resource probe before mutate (two-phase): count free slots in ROB/RS/LSQ and FreeList first; never partially allocate then discover RS full (would leak a PhysReg/ROB slot). FreeList pop already happened in rename (Phase 4) — dst_phys/old_phys arrive in latch; dispatch does NOT pop FreeList, only ROB/RS/LSQ.
  • old_phys carried from rename = the prior RAT mapping for dst_arch; stored in RobEntry so commit can return it to FreeList. x0-destined insts: dst_arch=x0, dst_phys = the canonical zero phys reg, old_phys same — commit frees nothing.
  • Wire DispatchUnit into OooCore::tick() between rename and issue stages (reverse-order tick: dispatch runs after issue reads, before rename writes).

17.3 Deliverable

oooc run advances a program through fetch→decode→rename→dispatch; oooc trace --stage dispatch dumps per-cycle ROB occupancy + each allocated RobIdx. ROB/RS/LSQ are finite and stall correctly. cargo test -p ooo-rob + -p ooo-core green; ROB-full stall test passes.

17.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Allocation is strictly in program order: RobIdx sequence == fetch order (modulo wrap).
  • ROB occupancy ≤ rob_size always; is_full() true ⇒ zero allocs that cycle, stall propagates to all younger insts (no out-of-order admission).
  • A tight dependent chain longer than rob_size insts produces a ROB-full stall (dispatch IPC drops, Stats.cycles rises) without panic or overwrite.
  • old_phys correctly threaded: post-commit (Phase 10) FreeList returns to steady state — checked here by asserting each RobEntry.old_phys matches rename's pre-mapping.
  • RS-full and LSQ-full independently trigger identical atomic stalls.

17.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected
tests/fixtures/rob_inorder.hex 16 indep adds RobIdx 0..15 in fetch order; occupancy ramps to ≤rob_size
tests/fixtures/rob_full.hex long dep chain, rob_size=8 dispatch stalls at 8 in-flight; cycles > inst count
tests/fixtures/rs_full.hex many same-FU ops, rs_size=4 RS-full stall before ROB-full
tests/fixtures/lsq_full.hex back-to-back loads, lsq_size=4 LSQ-full stall; non-mem ops behind also blocked (in-order)
tests/fixtures/x0_dst.hex add x0, x1, x2 ROB slot allocated, old_phys==zero-phys, no FreeList churn
Malformed alloc when is_full() returns None; harness asserts no slot mutated

17.6 Pitfalls

  • head==tail ambiguity (full vs empty): root cause — using only two pointers. Fix: explicit count. Debug: assert count == (tail-head) mod cap.
  • Partial allocation leak: RS full discovered after ROB slot taken → orphaned ROB entry / lost PhysReg at commit. Root cause: mutate-before-probe. Fix: two-phase probe. Debug: FreeList size monotonically shrinks across a steady loop.
  • Out-of-order admission under stall: skipping a stalled inst to dispatch a younger ready one → program order violated, ROB no longer in-order ⇒ broken commit. Fix: stall is a hard barrier; never reorder at dispatch.
  • Wrong old_phys capture timing: reading RAT after this inst's own rename write → old_phys==dst_phys, commit double-frees or never frees. Root cause: capture must be the pre-update mapping (Phase 4 contract). Debug: assert old_phys != dst_phys for non-x0 dst.
  • src-ready snapshot staleness: RsEntry.src*_ready copied from PhysRegFile.ready at dispatch but a producer completes this cycle — reverse-order tick guarantees the snapshot is the C-1 value; wakeup (Phase 6) handles C. Mixing them double-counts readiness.

17.7 Performance Concerns

  • alloc O(1): index + modulo, no scan. Avoid % cost by power-of-two rob_size + mask, or branchless wrap.
  • Probe phase: O(issue_width) checks of three is_full() flags — keep count cached, not recomputed.
  • RobEntry size: pack to ≤32 bytes (pc:u64, dst_arch:u8, dst_phys:u16, old_phys:u16, flags:u8) → cache-friendly ROB array; target full ROB (e.g. 192 entries) in a few cache lines.
  • Sim throughput unaffected: dispatch adds O(issue_width) work/cycle; maintain ≥1M cycles/sec.

17.8 Edge Cases

  • rob_size==1: alloc then immediate full — serializes the machine; must still progress.
  • Wrap-around: tail wraps to 0 while head mid-buffer — RobIdx reuse only after commit frees the slot.
  • All-mem batch fills LSQ before ROB; all-ALU fills RS before ROB — whichever is smallest dominates the stall.
  • Branch at dispatch: still allocated normally (resolution + checkpoint already in rename Phase 4); no special dispatch path yet.
  • Empty rename latch (frontend stalled/mispredict bubble): dispatch is a no-op, ROB unchanged.

17.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause Detection
ROB overwrite / panic OOB full-check missing or head==tail confusion assert count<=cap in alloc
Deadlock (no progress, ROB never drains) dispatch stall but commit not freeing (Phase 10 wiring) watchdog: cycles with 0 commit + 0 dispatch > N
IPC too high vs golden timing out-of-order admission / infinite ROB cap-respect assert + occupancy histogram
FreeList exhaustion mid-run old_phys mis-threaded → no frees track FreeList size vs ROB occupancy delta

17.10 Operational Notes

  • Config: ConfigParams{rob_size, rs_size, lsq_size, issue_width} drive all caps; expose via oooc config.
  • oooc trace --stage dispatch prints cycle | RobIdx | pc | dst_phys | fu | stall_reason.
  • Stats: add dispatch_stall_rob, dispatch_stall_rs, dispatch_stall_lsq counters for bottleneck attribution.
  • Debug assertions (debug_assert!) on ring invariants compiled out in release; keep hot path branchless.
  • Determinism: dispatch is purely a function of latch + cap state; replay from seed reproduces exact RobIdx stream.

18. Phase 6 — Out-of-order issue scheduler (wakeup + select)

Phase 5 dispatched in-order into the ROB, recording dst/old_phys and reading source readiness from PhysRegFile. Issue still drained in program order. Phase 6 introduces ooo-schedule: the Scheduler holds RsEntry with per-source ready bits, wakes entries when a producer's PhysReg broadcasts, and selects the oldest ready entry per IssuePort/FuKind up to issue_width. Issue leaves program order; independent instructions overtake stalled ones.

18.1 Objectives

  • ooo-schedule crate: Scheduler/ReservationStation, RsEntry, IssueQueue, IssuePort; depends on ooo-rename.
  • Wakeup: completing PhysReg broadcast → set src1_ready/src2_ready on matching unissued entries (CAM match).
  • Select: per FuKind, pick oldest ready (!issued, both srcs ready) up to issue_width; mark issued, free slot at issue.
  • Reorder: independent ops issue ahead of a multi-cycle-stalled producer's dependents; out-of-program-order issue order recorded.
  • Determinism preserved: wakeup this cycle → eligible for select next cycle (no same-cycle teleport), wired into reverse-order tick().
  • Goal: IPC strictly above the in-order baseline on a chosen reorder kernel; zero golden-cosim divergence.

18.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-15 (unified RS vs split IQ — unified chosen), ADR-16 (age-based oldest-first select policy), P2 (Tomasulo's algorithm wakeup/select mechanism — the original RS spec), P8 (Gwennap — RISC vs CISC OoO scheduling analysis, rejected alternative informs ADR-15), R3 (gem5 inst_queue.cc — IQ wakeup dependency graph and ready-list reference).

File (crates/ooo-schedule/src/) Adds
lib.rs re-exports; pub use entry::RsEntry; scheduler::{Scheduler, IssueQueue}; port::IssuePort;
entry.rs RsEntry{rob_idx,op,src1,src2,src1_ready,src2_ready,dst,fu,issued}; age: u64 (dispatch seq)
scheduler.rs Scheduler{entries:Vec<Option<RsEntry>>, free:Vec<usize>, wake_bus:Vec<PhysReg>}
port.rs IssuePort{fu:FuKind}; per-port arbitration order; ports:&[IssuePort] from ConfigParams
wakeup.rs fn wakeup(&mut self, completed:&[PhysReg]) — CAM over entries, set ready bits
select.rs fn select(&mut self, width:u32)->Vec<RsEntry> — oldest-ready per FU, age-sorted
  • Allocate: dispatch_into_rs(&mut self, e: RsEntry) -> Result<RsIdx, RsFull>; pop free; set age = next_seq(); source ready bits read from PhysRegFile::ready[p] at insert (already-completed producers).
  • x0 (PhysReg mapped from RegId(0)): always ready; never blocks issue, never appears on wake bus.
  • Wakeup CAM: per cycle, for each p in completed, scan entries; if src1==p {src1_ready=true} (same src2). Modeled as parallel CAM; cost = entries × completed compares (bounded by rs_size × issue_width).
  • Select: collect ready = !issued && src1_ready && src2_ready; group by fu; sort by age asc; take ≤ port count for that FuKind; total ≤ issue_width. Emit RsEntry copies to ooo-execute; set issued=true (entry freed on writeback ack, Phase 7).
  • Speculative wakeup deferred: producer latency known at issue, but actual readiness marked by Phase 7 writeback. Phase 6 wakes only on real completion (conservative; back-to-back via bypass is Phase 7).
  • Wiring: tick() calls scheduler.wakeup(prev_cycle_completed) then scheduler.select(width); completed list latched from prior cycle's execute stage → C+1 visibility invariant.

18.3 Deliverable

oooc run --ooo-issue <kernel.hex> issues out of program order; oooc trace shows issue cycle < an older instruction's issue cycle when the older one is source-stalled. Scheduler integrated; cargo test -p ooo-schedule green; cosim clean.

18.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Reorder kernel IPC > in-order baseline (≥ +15% on reorder_kernel.hex)
Golden cosim 0 divergences across all fixtures
Wakeup correctness every issued entry has both src ready bits set at select
Select fairness oldest-ready issues first per FU (age-monotonic per port)
RS occupancy no entry issued twice; free slots reclaimed; no leak over 1M cycles
Throughput ≥ 1M cycles/s with rs_size=64, issue_width=4

18.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expect
reorder_kernel.hex long Div (lat 20) followed by 6 independent Add → Adds issue before Div's dependents; IPC > baseline
chain.hex strict RAW chain a→b→c→d → issues stay program-ordered (no reorder possible)
wakeup_fanout.hex one producer feeds 8 consumers → all 8 wake same cycle, issue over ≥2 cycles if issue_width<8
x0_src.hex ops sourcing x0 issue immediately (no wake wait)
rs_full.hex (malformed/stress) dispatch beyond rs_size → RsFull stall, no panic, no dropped inst
double_wake.hex same PhysReg broadcast twice → idempotent, no double-issue

18.6 Pitfalls

  • Same-cycle teleport: waking on this cycle's completions then selecting same cycle inflates IPC. Root cause: not latching completed list. Debug: assert issue_cycle(consumer) ≥ complete_cycle(producer)+1 in trace.
  • Stale ready at insert: reading PhysRegFile::ready at dispatch but producer completes between dispatch and first wakeup → missed wake if not also CAM-matched. Fix: insert-time read AND wake-bus match; assert no entry stuck ready-but-unwoken.
  • Age wraparound: age:u64 seq vs reused u16 rob_idx — sorting by rob_idx breaks across ROB wrap. Use monotonic dispatch seq for age. Debug: oldest-select picks wrong entry post-wrap.
  • Double-issue: entry not marked issued before next select, or freed before writeback ack. Symptom: golden value written twice. Debug: per-PhysReg write counter == 1.
  • x0 on wake bus: a write to phys-mapped x0 broadcasts and falsely wakes — but x0 writes are no-ops. Suppress x0 from completed. Debug: spurious early wakes on wakeup_fanout.

18.7 Performance Concerns

  • Wakeup CAM is O(rs_size × issue_width) compares/cycle — hot loop; use Vec<Option<RsEntry>> contiguous, branchless ready-bit OR, SoA split of (src1,src2) arrays for cache locality.
  • Select sort: avoid full sort; maintain age-ordered free-list or use partial-selection (select_nth) per FU; target < 200 ns/cycle at rs_size=64.
  • Avoid per-cycle alloc: reuse select() output buffer; wake_bus cleared not reallocated.

18.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty RS / no ready entries → select returns empty; stage bubbles cleanly.
  • All entries ready but issue_width < ready count → oldest issue_width issue, rest retry next cycle.
  • Single-FU contention: 4 ready Div but one Div FU port → 1 issues/cycle, age-ordered.
  • Producer of a not-yet-dispatched consumer completes early → consumer reads ready at insert (no wake needed).
  • Branch/store ops that produce no dst → no wake broadcast; still occupy/issue normally.

18.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Deadlock (ready entry never selected) watchdog: cycles-since-issue > rs_size → assert dump RS state, log age/ready bits
RsFull livelock dispatch stall counter monotonic, commit stalled upstream backpressure (Phase 5) must drain ROB
Cosim divergence at commit golden value ≠ committed bisect to first mis-issued entry via per-issue trace
Lost wakeup entry ready-by-bus but bit unset CAM coverage assert each cycle

18.10 Operational Notes

  • oooc trace --stage issue dumps per-cycle: {cycle, rob_idx, fu, age, issued, src_ready} for selected + waiting entries.
  • oooc stats adds: rs_occupancy_avg, issue_width_utilization, wakeups/cycle, select_stalls (ready-but-port-starved).
  • oooc config --rs-size N --issue-width W sweeps; record IPC vs rs_size curve (knee typically near ROB-limited region).
  • Determinism: issue order fully a function of (age, fu, ready); replay from seed reproduces identical issue trace.

19. Phase 7 — Execute: functional units, latencies, and the bypass network

Crate: ooo-execute (depends ooo-schedule). Consumes selected RsEntry from Scheduler; produces ExecResult; broadcasts dst: PhysReg for wakeup; forwards via BypassNetwork. Branch FU resolves mispredict.

19.1 Objectives

  • FunctionalUnit set: FuKind::{Alu, Mul, Div, Branch, Agu, LoadStore}; per-kind latency table (Alu 1, Mul 3, Div ~20 iterative, Branch 1, Agu 1).
  • Model pipelined (accept 1 op/cycle, busy_until = issue+1) vs iterative/unpipelined (Div blocks port until issue+latency).
  • ExecResult writeback: write PhysRegFile.regs[dst], set ready[dst]=true, broadcast for Scheduler wakeup.
  • BypassNetwork: forward producer value to dependent's issue same/next cycle so back-to-back dependent Alu chain runs at 1 cyc/op, not full WB latency.
  • Branch FU computes taken/target, sets ExecResult.mispredict, carries resolved target to redirect (consumed in Phase 10).
  • x0 writes squashed (never written to its phys reg's arch view; dst still allocated but value discarded on commit).

19.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-14 (configurable FU latency table — pipelined MUL, iterative DIV), ADR-17 (bypass network — forward results before PRF writeback), P3 (H&P §C.5 / §C.6 — bypass forwarding and pipeline stages), R4 (gem5 iew.cc — issue→execute→writeback wiring and bypass reference), R7 (Spike execute.cc — ground-truth semantic check for compute() result correctness).

File Content
ooo-execute/src/fu.rs FuKind, FunctionalUnit{kind,latency,pipelined,busy_until:Cycle}, LATENCY: [u32; N], fn accept(&mut self, e:&RsEntry, now:Cycle)->bool (false if busy)
ooo-execute/src/exec.rs compute(op:Opcode, a:u64, b:u64, imm:i64)->u64; signed/unsigned MUL/DIV/REM; div-by-0 → RV64 spec (-1 / dividend), overflow INT_MIN/-1→INT_MIN/0
ooo-execute/src/branch.rs resolve_branch(op,a,b,pc,imm,pred:&Prediction)->(taken,target,mispredict); Jalr target (a+imm)&!1
ooo-execute/src/result.rs ExecResult{dst,value,rob_idx,mispredict}; ExecPort{fu:FunctionalUnit, in_flight:VecDeque<(Cycle,ExecResult)>}
ooo-execute/src/bypass.rs BypassNetwork{ tags: Vec<PhysReg>, vals: Vec<u64> }; broadcast(r:&ExecResult), lookup(p:PhysReg)->Option<u64>, clear() per tick
ooo-execute/src/writeback.rs writeback(res, prf:&mut PhysRegFile, sched:&mut Scheduler): prf.write, ready[dst]=true, sched.wakeup(dst)
  • Pipeline model: each ExecPort holds in_flight deque keyed by completion cycle = issue_cycle + latency. Pipelined FU appends every cycle; iterative FU rejects accept while busy_until > now.
  • Same-cycle bypass: in reverse-order Tick, Execute runs before Issue; result broadcast into BypassNetwork is visible to Issue's operand read this tick → effective 0-bubble for latency-1 producers feeding select.
  • Wakeup timing: broadcast dst latency-1 cycles before completion (speculative wakeup) so a 1-cycle consumer issues the cycle the producer writes back; deferred to Phase 11 widening — Phase 7 uses non-speculative wakeup at completion.

19.3 Deliverable

ooo-execute integrated into OooCore.tick(): selected entries execute on typed FUs with realistic latencies; BypassNetwork forwards; PhysReg.ready set on WB; Branch FU flags mispredict. oooc trace shows per-FU occupancy + bypass hits.

19.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Dependent Alu chain add x2,x1,x1; add x3,x2,x2; … (N=100) 1 cyc/op via bypass (~N+const cycles), not N×(WB latency)
Independent Mul ×8, single pipelined Mul FU issue 1/cyc, all complete by cyc 8+3
Single iterative Div FU, 4 back-to-back Div serialized: ~4×20 cycles; port blocked between
compute() RV64IM result bit-exact vs execute_functional golden for all opcodes
Branch mispredict flag set iff resolved (taken,target) ≠ Prediction

19.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fx/exec/dep_chain.hex latency = N + pipeline_fill; bypass_hits = N-1
fx/exec/mul_burst.hex 8 muls retire, port never stalls (pipelined)
fx/exec/div_serial.hex div_port_busy_cycles ≈ 4×20; second div waits
fx/exec/div_by_zero.hex quotient=u64::MAX, rem=dividend (no trap)
fx/exec/div_overflow.hex INT64_MIN / -1 → quotient INT64_MIN, rem 0
Malformed: bypass on stale tag lookup after clear() returns None; consumer reads PRF, not bypass
Malformed: dst=x0 value discarded; ready set but commit writes nothing

19.6 Pitfalls

  • Double-bypass / stale forward: BypassNetwork not cleared each tick → consumer reads last cycle's value. Root cause: missing clear() in Tick. Debug: dump bypass tags per cycle; assert empty at tick start.
  • Iterative FU re-accept mid-op: accept ignores busy_until → two Divs collide, second corrupts result. Debug: log busy_until vs now; assert single in-flight on unpipelined.
  • Wakeup before value valid: broadcasting dst before WB writes PRF → consumer reads garbage if bypass misses. Fix: broadcast and write same cycle (Phase 7 non-speculative); cosim catches divergence.
  • Div-by-zero / overflow as host panic: native / panics → sim crash instead of RV64 spec result. Root cause: not guarding edge inputs in compute. Debug: unit-test the two corner inputs.
  • Branch target off-by-imm: using pc+imm for Jalr instead of (rs1+imm)&!1 → wrong mispredict. Debug: diff resolved target vs golden next-PC.

19.7 Performance Concerns

  • BypassNetwork.lookup is hot (every operand, every issue): use small fixed array indexed/scanned by PhysReg, not HashMap; ≤ issue_width broadcasts/cycle.
  • in_flight deque per port: bounded by latency; pop-front O(1). Avoid per-cycle full scan.
  • Target ≥ 1M sim-cycles/s single-thread; FU dispatch + bypass must stay branch-predictable (match on FuKind, no dyn dispatch).

19.8 Edge Cases

  • Latency-1 producer → latency-1 consumer: bypass must close 0-bubble.
  • Multi-cycle producer (Mul) → consumer: consumer wakes only at completion cycle, not issue.
  • Two FUs complete same cycle, same dst impossible (unique phys dst) — but two writes same WB port → port arbitration (Phase 11); Phase 7 = 1 WB port/FU.
  • Div result feeding a branch: branch FU stalls in scheduler until Div ready.
  • x0 as dst and as src simultaneously: src reads 0, dst write discarded.

19.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause
Dep chain takes N×latency not N bypass disabled / cleared too early
IPC > 1 impossible inflation same-cycle teleport (Execute reading future WB) — reverse-order violated
Div never completes busy_until never advanced; port deadlock
Cosim diverges only on M-ops signed/unsigned or div-edge mismatch in compute
Spurious mispredicts branch resolve uses wrong operand width / target calc

19.10 Operational Notes

  • oooc trace --fu dumps per-cycle FU occupancy, busy_until, bypass hit/miss, in-flight deque depth.
  • oooc stats adds bypass_hits, div_port_stall_cycles, fu_utilization[FuKind].
  • Config knobs (ConfigParams, default): latencies overridable per FuKind; pipelined flag per FU; count of each FU (Phase 11 scales).
  • Determinism: FU completion ordered by (completion_cycle, rob_idx); no wall-clock; replay-stable.

20. Phase 8 — Memory pipeline: LSQ, disambiguation, store-to-load forwarding

Models the memory subsystem of ooo-mem: AGU address generation, the split LoadStoreQueue, age-ordered memory disambiguation against older stores, exact-match store-to-load forwarding, and load/store ordering tied to ROB program order. Cache/TLB latency is stubbed (fixed-latency L1 hit) here; the full hierarchy lands in Phase 9 (## 21). Reference: gem5 O3CPU LSQUnit, H&P Ch. 3 memory disambiguation, Tomasulo memory ordering.

20.1 Objectives

  • AGU effective-address: addr = xreg[rs1] + imm computed on FuKind::Agu; produces addr_ready for the LsqEntry.
  • Split LoadStoreQueue (separate loads/stores vecs) age-ordered vs ROB; allocate at dispatch, in program order.
  • Memory disambiguation: a load scans older stores (rob_idx < load.rob_idx); resolve forward / wait / stall.
  • Store-to-load forwarding: exact addr+size match from youngest older store with known value → bypass, no cache access.
  • Conservative wait on unknown older-store addr (baseline); optional speculate + commit-time violation detect (PhaseDeferred toggle).
  • Stores write cache only at commit (in-order); loads non-speculative wrt older known stores. Invariant: committed mem state == golden ArchState.

20.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-19 (unified LSQ vs split LQ/SQ — unified chosen), ADR-20 (conservative store-to-load forwarding as default), P4 (H&P §C.9 / Tomasulo memory disambiguation section), P5 (RVWMO memory model spec — §A of RISC-V Unpriv. spec, single-thread ordering), R6 (gem5 lsq.cc / lsq_unit.cc — LSQ lifecycle and STLF reference).

Files (all ooo-mem): src/lsq.rs (LoadStoreQueue, LsqEntry), src/agu.rs (address gen), src/forward.rs (disambig + STLF), src/disambig.rs (predictor stub), src/mem_iface.rs (MemRequest/MemResponse), src/lib.rs re-exports.

// lsq.rs — LsqEntry per spine + size/state for ordering
pub struct LsqEntry { pub rob_idx: RobIdx, pub is_load: bool, pub addr: Option<u64>,
    pub value: Option<u64>, pub addr_ready: bool, pub committed: bool,
    pub size: u8, pub fwd_from: Option<RobIdx> /*aud*/ }
pub struct LoadStoreQueue { loads: Vec<LsqEntry>, stores: Vec<LsqEntry>, lsq_size: usize }
pub enum DisambigResult { Forward { value: u64, src: RobIdx }, Wait /*unknown older store*/,
    CacheAccess /*no overlap*/, PartialConflict /*overlap but size/align mismatch*/ }
  • AGU (agu.rs): when Agu FU completes for a mem uop, set entry.addr = Some(ea), addr_ready = true, then run wakeup → memory disambiguation. Misaligned EA → Trap::LoadAddrMisaligned/StoreAddrMisaligned recorded in RobEntry.exception.
  • Allocation: at dispatch (Phase 5) reserve LSQ slot per is_load/is_store; LSQ-full → structural stall (dispatch backpressure). rob_idx is the age key.
  • Disambiguation (forward.rs): on a load with known addr, scan stores with rob_idx < load.rob_idx, youngest-first. Logic: any older store with addr==None → Wait; else youngest exact (addr,size) match with value=Some → Forward; overlap but mismatched size/offset → PartialConflict (→ Wait, must replay after store commits); no overlap → CacheAccess.
  • STLF: Forward returns store value directly into PhysRegFile/bypass; sets ExecResult.value; no MemRequest issued; records fwd_from for cosim audit.
  • Store value: arrives separately (rs2 ready) — store may have addr before data; load matching a data-less store with known addr → Wait until data ready.
  • Ordering vs ROB: loads complete out of order but ROB commits in order; stores drain to cache only when at ROB head (commit_store() in Phase 10 calls into LSQ). LSQ entry freed on commit.
  • Speculate mode (config mem_spec: bool): load bypasses unknown older store (predict no-alias); a MemoryOrderViolation is flagged if a later older-store addr collides → squash load + younger at commit (replay path stubbed, wired fully Phase 10).

20.3 Deliverable

ooo-mem LSQ integrated into OooCore: loads/stores allocate, AGU generates addresses, disambiguation + STLF operate per cycle, stores commit in order. oooc run executes load/store programs with correct values; oooc cosim matches golden on store-heavy programs; oooc stats reports forwards, lsq_stalls, disambig_waits.

20.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Load-after-store to same addr forwards store value with 0 cache accesses (counter forwards increments; cache_accesses unchanged).
  • Conservative load behind unknown-addr older store stalls ≥1 cycle until store addr resolves (disambig_waits increments).
  • Cosim: committed ArchState.xregs/memory identical to golden across all Phase 8 fixtures, 0 divergence.
  • IPC for a no-conflict load-stream ≥ baseline; STLF cuts load latency from L1-hit (e.g. 3) to 1 cycle on matched pairs.
  • LSQ-full triggers dispatch stall (no silent drop); throughput ≥ 0.8 M cycles/s with LSQ active.

20.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Scenario Expected
fwd_basic.hex sw x1,0(x2); lw x3,0(x2) x3==x1, forwards==1, cache_accesses==0
fwd_size_mismatch.hex sb then lw overlapping PartialConflict→Wait, load reads after commit, no STLF
disambig_wait.hex store addr late, younger load disambig_waits>=1, correct value
no_alias_stream.hex independent loads/stores OoO complete, IPC>baseline, cosim ok
violation_spec.hex (spec mode) load bypasses then collides MemoryOrderViolation flagged, squash, replay, cosim ok
Malformed: misalign.hex lw at addr%4!=0 Trap::LoadAddrMisaligned in RobEntry, precise at commit
Malformed: lsq_overflow.hex floods LSQ beyond lsq_size dispatch stalls, no panic/overflow

20.6 Pitfalls

  • Same-cycle store→load teleport: forwarding a store value produced this cycle violates the reverse-Tick latch rule → load reads stale/early. Root: ordering disambiguation before store value latched. Debug: assert producing store's value was set in cycle ≤ C-1; trace fwd_from vs cycle.
  • Wrong forwarding store picked: forwarding from an older store when a younger-but-still-older store also matches → stale value. Must pick youngest store with rob_idx < load. Debug: dump matching-store set sorted by rob_idx.
  • Partial overlap silently forwarded: byte store + word load returns wrong bytes. Root: addr-equality check ignores size/offset. Mitigate: PartialConflict→Wait; debug with size-tagged fixtures.
  • Store commits but LSQ entry not freed: stale store keeps matching new loads → false forwards. Debug: assert LSQ store count == uncommitted stores in ROB.
  • Addr-known but data-unknown store: load forwards None value. Root: STLF gated only on addr match, not value.is_some(). Fix: require both; else Wait.
  • Misaligned load forwarding: trap raised but load already forwarded a value into PRF → corrupt rename state on squash. Order: raise trap before disambiguation.

20.7 Performance Concerns

  • Disambiguation is O(older-stores) CAM scan per load → bound by lsq_size; for size 32 acceptable, watch >128. Use rob_idx range filter, not full scan.
  • STLF removes L1 access → measurable IPC win on store-forward-heavy code; track forwards/loads ratio.
  • Conservative wait serializes ambiguous load/store → IPC loss; speculate mode trades replays for parallelism (report violations/loads).
  • Per-cycle LSQ scan dominates tick() hot path; keep LsqEntry cache-line-friendly (POD, no Box).

20.8 Edge Cases

  • Load and store same cycle same addr — store older by rob_idx → forward; load older → no forward (cache).
  • Store to x0-derived addr / addr 0 — valid, no special case.
  • Two stores same addr before one load → forward youngest only.
  • Load fully covered by overlapping older store with smaller size → PartialConflict, not forward.
  • Store at ROB head with addr unknown (AGU stalled) → blocks commit until resolved (precise ordering).
  • Load whose only older store is squashed (mispredict) mid-flight → re-disambiguate after squash; stale fwd_from must clear.

20.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
STLF returns wrong bytes cosim diverges at load's commit size-aware match; bisect to forward.rs
Store skips cache on commit golden memory mismatch assert every committed store issued MemRequest{is_write}
Load completes before older store addr known (non-spec) cosim value diff enforce Wait on any addr==None older store
Violation undetected (spec mode) divergence only under spec commit-time addr cross-check; disable spec to localize
LSQ entry leak LSQ-full stalls forever invariant check LSQ counts vs ROB each commit

20.10 Operational Notes

  • oooc trace adds LSQ column: per-cycle [L#/S# addr state fwd_from]; flag --lsq dumps full queue contents.
  • oooc stats: forwards, disambig_waits, lsq_stalls, violations, cache_accesses; derive stlf_rate.
  • ConfigParams.lsq_size and mem_spec knobs via oooc config; default mem_spec=false (conservative).
  • Determinism: disambiguation scan order fixed (youngest-older-first by rob_idx); no map iteration. Seeded replay reproduces forward/wait decisions bit-exact.
  • Cache/TLB still fixed-latency stub; Phase 9 (## 21) replaces CacheAccess resolution with real MSHR/miss timing feeding wakeup.

21. Phase 9 — Cache and TLB hierarchy

21.1 Objectives

  • Add a configurable, set-associative memory hierarchy to ooo-mem: L1D (+ optional unified L2), with MSHRs, miss-status tracking, and tiered miss latency.
  • Add Tlb (set-assoc VA→PA) feeding address translation before cache lookup; model TLB-miss penalty (page-walk latency, no full page tables this phase — flat identity + latency tax).
  • Feed variable memory latency back into LoadStoreQueue completion → producer PhysReg wakeup broadcast, so cache misses delay dependent issue (not a fixed-cycle load).
  • Make miss latency visibly move IPC; expose cache_misses (already in Stats) + new counters; ship a latency-sensitivity test.
  • NON-GOALS: coherence/MESI (project 187), prefetchers (PhaseDeferred), real page-table walk, write-buffer modeling beyond MSHR occupancy.

21.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-13 (cycle-accurate cache model vs abstract memory — cycle-accurate chosen), ADR-18 (MSHR-based miss handling, no blocking cache), P3 (H&P §B — cache hierarchy design, MSHR structure), P12 (Carlson et al., Sniper — abstraction-level contrast informs ADR-13 and ADR-5), R9 (Sniper/DRAMsim — miss-latency modeling reference, cited as the abstraction-level contrast).

Files (all in ooo-mem/src/): cache.rs (Cache, CacheLevel, MSHRs), tlb.rs (Tlb), extend lsq.rs (completion wiring), mem.rs (MemRequest/MemResponse, hierarchy struct).

// cache.rs
pub struct CacheLine { tag: u64, valid: bool, dirty: bool, lru: u32 }
pub struct CacheSet { ways: Vec<CacheLine> }            // ways.len() == assoc
pub struct Mshr { addr: u64, ready_cycle: Cycle, waiters: SmallVec<[RobIdx; 4]>, is_write: bool }
pub struct CacheLevel { sets: Vec<CacheSet>, line_bytes: u32, assoc: u32, hit_latency: u32,
    mshrs: Vec<Mshr>, max_mshrs: usize, next: Option<Box<CacheLevel>> } // next == lower level/DRAM
pub struct Cache { l1: CacheLevel /* .next = optional L2; L2.next = None => DRAM */ }

pub enum MemResponse { Hit { value: u64 }, Miss { ready_cycle: Cycle }, MshrFull, TlbMiss }
impl Cache {
    pub fn access(&mut self, req: MemRequest, rob_idx: RobIdx, now: Cycle) -> MemResponse;
    pub fn complete(&mut self, now: Cycle) -> SmallVec<[(RobIdx, u64); 4]>; // fills due this cycle
}
  • Index/tag: set = (addr >> log2(line)) & (nsets-1), tag = addr >> (log2(line)+log2(nsets)). Byte offset ignored (no sub-line modeling).
  • Lookup: TLB → cache. On TLB hit: index L1 set, tag-match all ways → Hit { hit_latency }; else MSHR path.
  • MSHR allocate on miss: coalesce to existing MSHR if same line (add waiter RobIdx); else new MSHR with ready_cycle = now + level.miss_latency; if mshrs.len()==max_mshrs → MshrFull (LSQ retries → structural stall).
  • Latency tax: l1.hit_latency (≈3–4), L2 hit (≈12–20), DRAM (≈100–300), TLB-miss walk (≈50). Configurable via ConfigParams (new fields).
  • LRU replace on fill; dirty victim → writeback request to next (occupies its MSHR/bandwidth, no stall this phase).
  • Cache::complete(now) scans MSHRs with ready_cycle == now, fills line, returns (rob_idx, value) waiters → LSQ marks LsqEntry.value, addr_ready already set → producer PhysReg.ready=true → scheduler wakeup broadcast at C+1.
  • Tlb: same set-assoc skeleton; translate(va)->Option<pa>; miss → fixed walk latency, fill entry. Flat VA==PA mapping (identity) — only latency is modeled.
  • ConfigParams additions: l1_sets,l1_assoc,l1_line,l1_lat, l2_enable,l2_*, dram_lat, tlb_entries,tlb_assoc,tlb_miss_lat, mshrs.

21.3 Deliverable

oooc run --config <toml> with cache knobs; oooc stats reports L1/L2 miss rate, TLB miss rate, avg memory latency, IPC. oooc config dumps effective hierarchy. A --mem-latency-sweep mode reruns one kernel across DRAM-latency values and tabulates IPC.

21.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Pointer-chase (working set ≫ L1) IPC vs all-hit IPC ≥ 3× lower (misses dominate)
DRAM latency 100→300 on miss-bound kernel monotonic IPC drop, ≥ 25%
L1 miss-rate counter vs hand-computed (streaming stride) exact match (±0 misses)
MSHR-full back-pressure observable LSQ structural stalls in trace
Golden co-sim committed arch state unchanged across all latency configs

21.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
tests/mem/stride_hit.hex working set < L1 → miss-rate ≈ 1/(line/elem); deterministic count
tests/mem/ptr_chase.hex each load misses → IPC ≈ 1/dram_lat region
tests/mem/stl_then_miss.hex store-forward (Phase 8) hits; cold load misses; ordering preserved
tests/mem/mshr_storm.hex N>max_mshrs concurrent misses → MshrFull stalls logged
tests/mem/tlb_thrash.hex strided pages > tlb_entries → TLB miss-rate ≈ 100%
Malformed: assoc=0, sets non-pow2, line < 4, mshrs=0 config validation error, no panic

21.6 Pitfalls

  • Same-cycle teleport: filling a line and waking the waiter in the same tick() violates the reverse-order latch rule → load appears 1 cycle early. Fix: complete() runs in Commit/WB region, wakeup visible C+1. Debug: trace producer cycle vs consumer issue cycle.
  • MSHR coalescing miss: two loads to same line allocating two MSHRs double-counts misses + under-uses bandwidth. Root cause: tag-compare before allocate skipped. Debug: assert ≤1 MSHR per line.
  • LRU update on miss-fill only: forgetting to touch LRU on hits → effectively random replacement, inflated miss rate. Debug: compare miss count to analytic for known stride.
  • TLB before/after disambiguation: translating after LSQ forwarding can forward across aliasing PAs; identity map hides it now but breaks if VA≠PA later. Debug: assert addr used for STL-forward is post-translation.
  • Writeback victim lost: dropping dirty eviction makes co-sim diverge only if a later load reads it — silent until then. Identity-store keeps golden correct; flag with dirty-eviction counter.
  • Counter at speculative access: incrementing cache_misses on a load later squashed inflates stats. Fix: count at commit, or tag-and-reconcile on flush.

21.7 Performance Concerns

  • Per-access cost: TLB lookup + cache index + tag-compare ×assoc — keep CacheSet.ways small, linear scan fine for assoc ≤ 16; avoid hashmap (cache-unfriendly).
  • MSHR scan in complete() is O(max_mshrs)/cycle — bound max_mshrs ≤ 32; use a min-heap by ready_cycle if it shows in profiles.
  • Memory hierarchy must not dominate sim throughput; target ≥ 500K cycles/s with full hierarchy enabled (vs ≥1M for ALU-only).
  • SmallVec waiters avoid per-miss heap alloc; preallocate sets/ways once at construction.

21.8 Edge Cases

  • addr straddling a line boundary: this phase treats access as single-line (loads are ≤8B, lines ≥16B, aligned) — assert alignment, trap on misalign per ISA.
  • Store hit on dirty line: mark dirty, no allocate; load to same line post-store: STL-forward (Phase 8) bypasses cache entirely.
  • L2 disabled: l1.next = None → L1 miss goes straight to DRAM latency.
  • MSHR ready_cycle ties: multiple fills same cycle → deterministic order by MSHR index.
  • TLB hit + cache miss: pay TLB hit_lat (0–1) then miss latency, not summed walk.
  • Zero-size working set / no loads: miss-rate counter denominator 0 → report N/A, not div-by-zero.

21.9 Failure Analysis
