Symptom Likely root cause
IPC insensitive to DRAM latency loads completing at fixed latency; wakeup not gated on ready_cycle
Miss rate ≠ analytic LRU not updated on hit, or wrong index/tag split
Co-sim divergence after cache add dirty victim dropped, or value forwarded from wrong PA
Deadlock under load MSHR-full not retried, or fill never dequeued (ready_cycle in past missed)
Phantom early load fill→wakeup same cycle (latch violation)

21.10 Operational Notes

  • Knobs surfaced in oooc config; defaults model a Skylake-ish L1D (32KB, 8-way, 64B, 4-cyc) + 256KB L2 + 200-cyc DRAM.
  • oooc trace --mem annotates each load: hit/miss, level, MSHR id, ready_cycle.
  • New Stats fields piggyback existing cache_misses; add l2_misses, tlb_misses, mem_cycles for avg-latency derivation (avg = mem_cycles / mem_accesses).
  • Deterministic: hierarchy state is pure function of (config, access stream, cycle); replay (Phase 12) reproduces miss timeline bit-exact from seed.
  • Validation gate before Phase 10: latency-sensitivity test green + co-sim clean across the full config sweep.

22. Phase 10 — Commit, precise exceptions, and speculation recovery

Crate: ooo-rob. Brings CommitUnit + the Flush/recovery path online so the OoO core retires in program order with precise exceptions and full mispredict recovery. First phase where committed architectural state can be co-checked against the golden model end-to-end.

22.1 Objectives

  • In-order CommitUnit::commit(): retire ≤ commit_width head RobEntry per cycle, stop at first not-done.
  • Free old_phys to FreeList on each commit; update ArchState golden mirror; write committed stores to ooo-mem.
  • Precise traps: at ROB head with exception: Some(Trap), squash all younger, redirect fetch to handler PC, set CSRs.
  • Branch mispredict recovery: restore Rat from RenameCheckpoint, roll back FreeList, squash ROB/RS/LSQ younger than the branch, redirect fetch.
  • Invariant: sequence of committed arch states == golden execute_functional sequence, timing-independent.

22.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-5 (RenameCheckpoint captured at rename, consumed here at recovery — the commit/flush contract), ADR-24 (resolve-time mispredict recovery vs commit-time — resolve-time chosen for latency), P2 (H&P §C.8 — in-order commit and precise-exception via ROB), P9 (RISC-V Privileged Spec §3.1 — mepc/mcause/mtvec trap semantics), R5 (gem5 commit.cc — squash logic and old_phys reclaim reference).

Files: ooo-rob/src/commit.rs (CommitUnit), ooo-rob/src/flush.rs (recovery), ooo-rob/src/rob.rs (ReorderBuffer squash ops), wired in ooo-core/src/tick.rs as the first stage of tick().

Step Mechanism
Commit scan walk head forward ≤ commit_width; stop at !entry.done (structural in-order gate)
Reg reclaim on commit of reg-writer: free_list.push(entry.old_phys); rat already points new dst_phys
Store write is_store entry → lsq.commit_store(rob_idx) drains addr/value to Cache; non-speculative
Golden mirror execute_functional(&decoded, &mut self.golden); assert phys_rf[dst_phys] == golden.xregs[dst_arch]
Advance head = (head + n) % rob_size; stats.committed += n

CommitUnit::commit(&mut self, rob, prf, free_list, lsq, golden, stats) -> CommitOutcome where CommitOutcome ∈ {Normal, Trap{pc,cause}, Mispredict{rob_idx,correct_target}}.

Recovery (flush.rs), signature + the five ordered effects (no bodies — logic is the ordered list, not restated as code):

fn recover_mispredict(rob: &mut ReorderBuffer, rat: &mut Rat, free_list: &mut FreeList,
    sched: &mut Scheduler, lsq: &mut LoadStoreQueue, fetch: &mut FetchUnit, br_idx: RobIdx);
  1. Read cp = rob[br_idx].checkpoint — the RenameCheckpoint captured at this branch's rename.
  2. Restore *rat = cp.rat (arch→phys map) and free_list.rollback(cp.free_head) (reclaim phys regs allocated by squashed instructions).
  3. rob.squash_after(br_idx) — tail resets to br_idx+1, clearing younger entries.
  4. sched.squash_after(br_idx) / lsq.squash_after(br_idx) — drop every RsEntry/LsqEntry younger than the branch.
  5. fetch.redirect(rob[br_idx].correct_target) — resume fetch at the correct-path PC.

RenameCheckpoint stored in RobEntry (or side table keyed by RobIdx) at Phase 4 rename; consumed only here. FreeList::rollback(head) resets the allocation head (free list is a ring; squashed phys regs become free again, never double-freed because RAT restore makes them unreachable).

Trap path: same squash_after(head) but redirect to trap-vector CSR (mtvec), set mepc=entry.pc, mcause=Trap, switch privilege (minimal). Mispredict resolves speculatively at execute but recovery commits at the branch's commit only if we use commit-time recovery; we use resolve-time recovery (faster) keyed by ExecResult.mispredict, with commit as backstop for ordering correctness.

22.3 Deliverable

ooo-rob exposing working CommitUnit + flush path, integrated into OooCore::tick(). oooc run on a program with a trap (e.g. ecall/illegal) and a hard-to-predict branch retires correctly; oooc cosim over those programs reports zero divergence.

22.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • ≤ commit_width retired/cycle, strictly in order (assert monotonic RobIdx at commit).
  • Trap test: faulting inst commits as trap, all younger squashed, fetch redirected to mtvec, mepc exact.
  • Mispredict test: post-recovery Rat/FreeList byte-identical to a from-scratch rename at branch target.
  • Golden equivalence: every committed xregs[dst_arch] == golden; 0 divergences over ≥ 100K committed insts.
  • No phys-reg leak: free_list.len() returns to baseline after a full squash (conservation check).

22.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Expects
tests/fixtures/trap_illegal.hex illegal opcode at PC X → commit trap, mcause=2, mepc=X, younger squashed
tests/fixtures/trap_ecall.hex ecall → environment-call trap, precise mepc
tests/fixtures/mispredict_loop.hex data-dependent branch, ≥ 30% mispredict; cosim zero divergence
tests/fixtures/squash_conservation.hex deep wrong-path then recovery; free_list count == pre-branch
tests/fixtures/store_at_commit.hex speculative store on wrong path must NOT reach Cache
Malformed: rob_full_trap.hex trap while ROB full → no deadlock; commits then squashes
Malformed: double_free.hex squash region overlapping committed entry → must reject, not double-free old_phys

22.6 Pitfalls

  • Same-cycle squash-then-commit: recovering and committing the branch in one tick can free old_phys twice. Root cause: branch is both committer and squash-pivot. Fix: squash region is strictly younger than pivot (br_idx+1..tail). Debug: assert free_list set-disjoint from live rat entries.
  • Stale FreeList head: rollback to wrong free_head reclaims regs still in use → silent data corruption. Root cause: checkpoint taken after allocation, not before. Capture free_head pre-alloc in rename. Debug: golden mismatch on first wrong-path-tainted commit.
  • Store leak on wrong path: writing store at execute not commit → speculative store hits Cache. Root cause: bypassing the commit gate. Stores drain only in commit_store. Debug: oooc trace cache-write log on wrong-path inst.
  • Lost ROB entries on wrap: circular squash with head>tail modular range mis-clears. Debug: assert (tail-head) mod rob_size == live_count.
  • Imprecise trap: a younger inst's exception reported before older inst commits → non-precise. Root cause: not gating trap on ROB head. Only the head entry's exception triggers; younger traps wait.

22.7 Performance Concerns

  • Commit is in-order ≤ W/cycle → can bottleneck IPC if ROB drains slowly; target commit not the limiter (W=4 default).
  • Squash cost: O(younger entries) for ROB/RS/LSQ clear; use generation-tag invalidation (bump epoch, lazy-skip) instead of memset to keep squash O(1) amortized.
  • Mispredict penalty target: 8–15 cycles (fetch redirect → first useful commit), matching gem5 O3 defaults.
  • Keep RenameCheckpoint shallow (RAT = 32×u16 + 1 usize ≈ 72 B) so capture/restore is a cheap memcpy.

22.8 Edge Cases

  • x0 as dst: never renamed → no old_phys to free; commit must skip free_list.push.
  • Trap on a store: store must NOT drain to memory (faulting inst writes nothing).
  • Mispredict on the ROB head with empty younger window: recovery is a no-op flush + redirect only.
  • Nested speculation: inner branch resolves before outer; restore must use the correct per-branch checkpoint, not latest.
  • ROB full + head not done (long-latency div/load miss): no commit, no deadlock — issue stalls upstream naturally.
  • Self-redirect: predicted target == correct target but mispredict flag set spuriously → guard with target compare.

22.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause First probe
Cosim diverges N insts after a branch FreeList/RAT restore wrong dump checkpoint vs fresh rename at br_idx
free_list shrinks over time leaked old_phys (x0 or squash) conservation assert per 1K commits
Deadlock, IPC→0 head never done (FU stuck / store unack) trace head RobEntry ready bits
Trap reported late/early trap not gated to head log (head, exception) each commit
Wrong-path store visible store drained pre-commit cache write-trace filter speculative

22.10 Operational Notes

  • oooc trace --stage=commit emits per-cycle retired RobIdx, freed PhysReg, trap/mispredict events.
  • oooc cosim runs golden lock-step at every commit; on mismatch dumps both ArchStates + offending RobEntry and bisects to the first diverging commit (Phase 12 hook).
  • Config knobs honored: commit_width, rob_size. Recovery latency reported in Stats.mispredicts + new recovery_cycles counter.
  • Determinism: recovery is pure function of checkpoint + RobIdx; replay from seed reproduces identical squash sequence.

23. Phase 11 — Superscalar widening and performance counters

23.1 Objectives

  • Widen every in-order stage to W-wide: fetch_width/issue_width/commit_width from ConfigParams; rename/dispatch follow fetch_width.
  • Multiple FunctionalUnits per FuKind behind IssuePorts; model port contention (≤1 issue/port/cycle, structural stall when ports saturated).
  • Scale PhysRegFile/ReorderBuffer/Scheduler/LoadStoreQueue to wide allocation: alloc/free up to W PhysReg/RobEntry per cycle.
  • Build the complete Stats set: IPC, mispredict rate, cache miss rate, ROB/RS occupancy, FU utilization, stall-cause breakdown.
  • Deliverable: configurable N-wide OooCore + perf-counter report, validated by a width-scaling study (IPC vs issue_width 1→8).

23.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-21 (per-port FU assignment vs fungible pool — fungible pool chosen), ADR-22 (superscalar width configurable 1–8), P2 (H&P §C.9 — superscalar and multiple-issue details), P7 (Eyerman & Eeckhout stall-cause decomposition — IPC attribution model for StallCause enum), R4 (gem5 iew.cc — issue→execute widening reference), R10 (BOOM docs — per-port FU assignment cross-check, rejected alternative in ADR-21).

Crate / file Change
ooo-frontend/src/fetch.rs FetchUnit::fetch() returns up to fetch_width DecodedInst; stop at first predicted-taken branch in the line (fetch-block truncation).
ooo-rename/src/rename.rs RenameUnit::rename_group(&mut self, insts: &[DecodedInst]) -> Result<Vec<Renamed>, RenameStall>; allocate ≤fetch_width PhysReg from FreeList; intra-group RAW: later op in same group reads earlier op's new PhysReg (sequential RAT walk, not snapshot).
ooo-rob/src/rob.rs ReorderBuffer::alloc_group(n) reserves n contiguous RobEntry slots; CommitUnit::commit() retires ≤commit_width, stops at first not-done/exception.
ooo-schedule/src/scheduler.rs Scheduler::select() returns up to issue_width RsEntry, bound by per-port FU availability; age-ordered, oldest-ready-first per port.
ooo-execute/src/fu.rs Vec<FunctionalUnit> pool keyed by FuKind; IssuePort { fu_kinds: Vec<FuKind> }; port_map: FuKind -> Vec<port_id>.
ooo-execute/src/bypass.rs BypassNetwork widened to W result lanes/cycle; one CAM broadcast per completing PhysReg lane.
ooo-core/src/stats.rs extend Stats with occupancy histograms + StallCause enum counters; sample each tick().
ooo-core/src/config.rs validate phys_regs ≥ 32 + rob_size; rs_size ≥ issue_width; reject impossible widths. 
// ooo-core/src/stats.rs  (widened)
pub enum StallCause { RobFull, RsFull, LsqFull, PhysRegEmpty, PortBusy, FetchEmpty, None }
pub struct Stats {
    pub cycles: u64, pub committed: u64, pub mispredicts: u64, pub cache_misses: u64,
    pub fu_busy_cycles: [u64; 6],          // per FuKind utilization
    pub rob_occ_sum: u64, pub rs_occ_sum: u64,   // /cycles -> avg occupancy
    pub stall: [u64; 7],                   // indexed by StallCause
    pub issued_per_cycle: [u64; 9],        // 0..=issue_width histogram
}                                          // ipc = committed as f64 / cycles
  • Port-contention algorithm: select() greedily assigns ready entries to free ports in age order; an entry whose only FuKind ports are taken → remains, increments StallCause::PortBusy.
  • Reverse-order tick() discipline unchanged: widening does not let a same-cycle producer wake a same-cycle consumer.

23.3 Deliverable

  • oooc run --issue-width N --fetch-width N boots an N-wide core; oooc stats prints the full counter report (table below).
  • oooc bench --scaling runs the width-scaling study, emits CSV (issue_width, IPC, mispredict_rate, fu_util...).
  • All Phase 0–10 cosim invariants still hold at every width (committed ArchState == golden).

23.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
IPC scaling (dhrystone-like, 1→4 wide) strictly increasing, ≥1.6× at 4-wide vs 1-wide
IPC saturation 4→8 wide <10% gain (ILP-limited) — proves contention modeling realistic
Sum of stall-cause counters == cycles where issued < issue_width (accounting closes)
committed/cycles vs counter-derived IPC bit-identical
Cosim divergences across all widths 0
FU utilization sum/cycles ≤ num_FUs per kind (no over-issue)

23.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fixtures/scaling/matmul.hex IPC monotone↑ over widths {1,2,4}; reported in scaling.golden.csv
fixtures/scaling/scaling.golden.csv per-width IPC within ±2% of recorded baseline
fixtures/stats/stall_breakdown.golden exact StallCause counts for a fixed seed/config
fixtures/stats/port_contention.hex 4 back-to-back Mul w/ 1 Mul FU → PortBusy ≥ 3
malformed: issue_width=0 config error IssueWidthZero, nonzero exit
malformed: phys_regs < 32+rob_size config error PhysRegUnderflow, refuse boot
malformed: issue_width > rs_size error RsTooSmall

23.6 Pitfalls

  • Intra-group rename via stale RAT snapshot → later same-group op reads producer's old PhysReg, silent RAW miss. Root cause: group renamed against one RAT image. Debug: oooc trace --stage rename; assert each op's src == most-recent in-group dst.
  • Double-counting FU utilization when a pipelined FU spans cycles → util >100%. Root cause: counting per-issue not per-busy-cycle. Fix: increment on occupancy, not on accept.
  • Port-assignment non-determinism → IPC jitter across runs. Root cause: iterating ready set in HashMap/unstable order. Fix: age-sorted stable order; assert determinism in cosim replay.
  • Wide commit retiring past a not-done head → architectural state corruption. Root cause: scanning for done entries instead of stopping at head. Fix: commit strictly head-contiguous.
  • Stall-cause attribution gaps → counters don't sum to lost-slot cycles. Root cause: a slot blocked by two causes counted once or zero. Fix: attribute to first blocking cause in pipeline order, exactly one per lost slot.
  • FreeList exhaustion under wide rename stalls silently → mistaken for low ILP. Debug: watch StallCause::PhysRegEmpty vs RobFull.

23.7 Performance Concerns

  • select() is O(rs_size × issue_width); wakeup CAM is O(rs_size × W lanes) — dominates wide-config sim time. Keep rs_size ≤ 128; bitset-ready vectors over linear scans.
  • Per-cycle Stats sampling must be branch-light: increment arrays, no allocation, no formatting in the hot loop (format only at report).
  • Sim throughput target: ≥1M simulated cycles/sec single-thread at 4-wide, rob_size=192, on a modern x86-64 core.

23.8 Edge Cases

  • issue_width > available ready entries → issue all ready, rest of ports idle (not a stall — issued_per_cycle bucket, not StallCause).
  • Single FU of a kind + group of N same-kind ops → N-1 PortBusy stalls, serialized.
  • commit_width group where entry[k] has an exception → commit [0,k), trap at k, squash rest (precise).
  • x0 destination inside a wide group → no PhysReg consumed, FreeList not drained.
  • Fetch-block truncated by predicted-taken branch → group narrower than fetch_width; downstream widths must tolerate ragged groups.

23.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery / response
IPC regresses raising width scaling study non-monotone bisect: isolate contention vs rename-stall via stall breakdown
Counter accounting open (Σstall ≠ lost slots) assert in --validate-counters fail CI; pinpoint mis-attributed cause
Cosim diverges only at wide config per-commit golden compare reduce to minimal failing width; likely intra-group rename bug
Throughput drops <1M cyc/s oooc bench --throughput profile select/wakeup; cap rs_size

23.10 Operational Notes

  • Knobs: --fetch-width, --issue-width, --commit-width, --rob-size, --rs-size, --phys-regs, --num-fu Alu=N,Mul=M,... via ConfigParams.
  • oooc config --dump prints resolved widths + derived constraints; --validate-counters enables accounting asserts (off in fast runs).
  • Report formats: oooc stats --format {table,json,csv}; scaling study CSV is the canonical artifact for the width study and feeds Phase 13 perf benchmarks.
  • Reference: gem5 DerivO3CPU issueWidth/dispatchWidth params and FUPool; H&P Ch.3 on ILP limits motivate the 4→8 saturation expectation.

24. Phase 12 — Golden co-simulation and deterministic replay

Crates: ooo-core (cosim engine, replay, bisection) + ooo-cli (cosim, bench subcommands). Depends: ooo-isa (ArchState, execute_functional, decode), full pipeline (OooCore::tick). Premise: timing is free to reorder/speculate; the committed architectural state stream must be bit-identical to the in-order ooo-isa golden model. Co-sim is the equivalence oracle; deterministic replay is the reproducibility guarantee.

24.1 Objectives

  • Lock-step commit comparator: at every committed RobEntry, replay one golden step, diff ArchState; flag divergence with {cycle, pc, reg, got, want}.
  • Golden runs driven by the OoO commit order (one functional step per retiring inst), never ahead — same dynamic program, same path.
  • Deterministic replay: (program_hash, ConfigParams, seed) tuple → byte-identical Stats + commit trace across runs/hosts.
  • Divergence bisection: from a diverging run, binary-search the committed-inst index of first divergence; emit minimal repro + pre-state dump.
  • cosim CLI mode: run any ELF/hex, assert zero divergence, exit nonzero + report on first mismatch.

24.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-23 (internal golden cosim vs external Spike oracle — internal execute_functional for per-commit check, external Spike for static corpus), ADR-7 (deterministic tick discipline — the replay/bisection mechanism depends on it), P2 (H&P §C — Tomasulo / ROB precise semantics that cosim validates), P11 (riscv-tests tohost convention — drives the pass/fail oracle in harness), R7 (Spike execute.cc — oracle for execute_functional semantic alignment).

Files / modules (ooo-core):

File Adds
cosim.rs Cosim, Divergence, commit_hook, step_golden
replay.rs ReplaySeed, RunDigest, replay(), Rng (deterministic)
bisect.rs bisect_divergence, MinRepro
core.rs (edit) OooCore::tick calls cosim.on_commit(&entry, &decoded, &phys_rf)

Cosim core (signature; algorithm described in the four bullets below, not restated in code):

pub struct Cosim { golden: ArchState, committed: u64, on: bool }
pub struct Divergence { pub cycle: Cycle, pub idx: u64, pub pc: u64,
    pub reg: RegId, pub got: u64, pub want: u64 }
impl Cosim {
    // called by CommitUnit per retired entry, in program order — see "Drive order" below
    pub fn on_commit(&mut self, e: &RobEntry, d: &DecodedInst,
        rf: &PhysRegFile) -> Result<(), Divergence>;
}
  • Drive order: golden pc must equal OoO committed pc before each step — catches control-flow divergence (bad branch resolve / RAS) before any reg diff masks it.
  • Register diff: after execute_functional advances golden by one step, x0 destinations always compare equal (skip); otherwise diff the committed physical value rf.regs[e.dst_phys] against golden.xregs[e.dst_arch] — first mismatch raises Divergence{idx, pc, reg: e.dst_arch, got, want}, then committed += 1.
  • Stores: compare via LSQ committed write {addr,value} vs golden memory delta (ArchState mem image); loads validated implicitly through later dependent regs.
  • CSR/traps: on e.exception=Some(trap), golden must take same trap (compare mcause/mepc); precise-exception point = same committed idx.
  • Replay: ReplaySeed{program_hash:u64, cfg:ConfigParams, seed:u64}; Rng = SplitMix64 seeded by seed, sole source of any tie-break randomness (replacement victim, equal-age select tie). replay(seed)->RunDigest{stats:Stats, commit_crc:u64}; commit_crc = CRC32 over (pc,dst_arch,value) stream.
  • Bisection: bisect_divergence(seed) re-runs with cosim disabled to commit-count N, then binary-searches k∈[0,N): run k commits, enable strict compare for commit k; first failing k = root. Emits MinRepro{seed, first_bad_idx:k, pre_state:ArchState}.

24.3 Deliverable

oooc cosim <prog> [--config f] [--seed s] → runs to completion, prints OK: N insts, 0 divergence, IPC=x.xx or DIVERGE @cycle C idx K pc=.. reg x.. got=.. want=.. (exit 3). oooc bench --replay --runs R proves identical RunDigest across R runs. cargo test -p ooo-core cosim_ green.

24.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Divergence on conformant programs 0 over all Phase-1 golden fixtures + Phase-13 corpus
Replay determinism commit_crc + every Stats field identical across ≥100 runs, 3 host archs
Bisection accuracy reported first_bad_idx = true first divergence on 20 injected-bug seeds
Cosim overhead ≤ 2.0× vs cosim-off; throughput ≥ 250K committed-insts/s with cosim on
Divergence report latency flagged on the first mismatching commit (idx exact, not off-by-one)

24.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Expected
fixtures/cosim/sum_1_n.hex OK, committed=golden, IPC reported
fixtures/cosim/branchy.hex 0 divergence despite mispredict/flush traffic
fixtures/cosim/loadstore.hex store-to-load forward path matches golden mem
fixtures/replay/digest_sumN.json frozen {commit_crc, cycles, committed, mispredicts, cache_misses}
fixtures/cosim/inject_wrong_rd.hex (malformed: harness forces bad dst_phys) DIVERGE @idx K, reg=rd, exit 3
fixtures/cosim/inject_badbranch.hex DIVERGE on pc mismatch (control), not reg
fixtures/replay/seed_mismatch.json two seeds → two distinct commit_crc (no false determinism)

24.6 Pitfalls

  • Golden runs ahead of OoO → compares wrong inst, masks real bug. Root: stepping golden in fetch/decode not commit. Debug: assert golden.committed == ooo.committed each tick.
  • x0 written in PhysRegFile → diff shows phantom nonzero. Root: forgot x0-never-renamed guard at compare. Debug: dst_arch.0==0 ⇒ skip/expect 0.
  • Speculative wrong-path commit → golden steps a never-retired inst. Root: cosim hook fired before squash filtered ROB head. Debug: hook only on truly-committed entries post-flush.
  • Nondeterministic tie-break → replay CRC flips run-to-run. Root: select/replacement used HashMap iteration or wall-clock. Debug: route all ties through seeded Rng; ban HashMap in hot path (use BTreeMap/age-ordered Vec).
  • f64 IPC in digest → host FP rounding breaks cross-arch determinism. Root: storing IPC float. Debug: digest stores integer committed/cycles, derive IPC only for display.
  • Store value compared before commit → forwarded-but-squashed store diffs. Root: comparing at execute not commit. Debug: only committed LSQ stores touch golden mem.

24.7 Performance Concerns

  • Golden step is cheap (one execute_functional); overhead is the per-commit clone-free compare — keep ArchState step in-place, no per-commit ArchState copy.
  • CRC fold incremental per commit (rolling), not a final pass over a buffered trace → O(1) memory.
  • --no-cosim fast path: comparator behind a branch predicted-not-taken; zero-cost when off via monomorphized Cosim<Off>.
  • Bisection is O(N log N) commits worst case; cap re-runs with cached pre-state snapshots every 2^k commits to amortize.

24.8 Edge Cases

  • x0 destination (e.g. add x0,..): skip reg compare, golden still steps PC.
  • Multi-commit/cycle (commit_width>1): compare each retired entry in program order within the cycle, golden steps once per entry.
  • Trap at commit head: golden must trap on same idx; compare mepc/mcause, then resync at handler PC.
  • Store with no arch reg (Sw): no reg diff; validate via golden mem image at later load.
  • Empty/zero-inst program: 0 committed, commit_crc of empty stream is a fixed constant; replay still deterministic.
  • Jalr to computed target: control compare catches RAS/BTB target bugs even when rd (link) happens to match.

24.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Reg divergence per-commit reg diff Divergence w/ idx+reg; exit 3; suggest bisect
Control divergence pc-before-step check reported as reg=0xFF sentinel; first sign of bad predictor recovery
Replay nondeterminism CRC mismatch across runs dump per-commit CRC delta to locate first divergent commit; audit Rng sources
Bisection wrong idx injected-bug oracle test indicates snapshot/replay impurity; fall back to linear scan
Golden desync (count drift) committed counter assert hard panic in debug; points to wrong-path/squash leak into cosim hook

24.10 Operational Notes

  • cosim on by default in CI for every fixture; --no-cosim only for perf bench numbers.
  • Divergence report is the canonical bug artifact: {seed, idx, pc, reg, got, want, pre_state} → reproduce via oooc cosim --replay-seed.
  • RunDigest JSON checked into fixtures/replay/; CI fails on any digest drift (gate against silent timing/Stats regressions).
  • Bisection helper exposed as oooc cosim --bisect <prog>; emits MinRepro for filing against the offending phase (rename/issue/LSQ/commit).
  • Determinism contract documented as load-bearing: no HashMap iteration, no threads, no wall-clock, no uninit reads in the per-cycle path; Rng seed is the only entropy.

25. Phase 13 — Conformance, differential vs Spike, fuzzing, and benchmarks

The project's "done" gate. Three independent oracles converge: riscv-tests (ISA conformance), Spike (differential cross-check), and a fuzz corpus (self-cosim invariant). Plus a criterion harness enforcing section-4 throughput + IPC regression gates.

25.1 Objectives

  • Run rv64ui + rv64um riscv-tests through the full timing core (OooCore::run) with per-commit golden cosim from Phase 12; all buckets green (PASS via tohost).
  • Differential harness: OooCore committed ArchState stream == Spike committed-state log, instruction-by-instruction, on a shared corpus.
  • Fuzz: random RV64IM generator → cosim never diverges, sim never panics/hangs (bounded cycle budget).
  • Criterion benches assert sim-throughput targets (§4: ≥1M sim-cycles/s single-thread) + emit IPC report on microbenchmarks; CI regression gate ≤3% throughput drop, ≤1% IPC drift.

25.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Key references: ADR-23 (Spike-oracle subprocess for static corpus — wired here), ADR-12 (fuzzer target as ooo-fuzz dev crate), P11 (riscv-tests tohost/fromhost HTIF convention — drives pass/fail detection), P12 (Carlson et al., Sniper — throughput-vs-fidelity calibration target for ≥1M cycles/s benchmark), R7 (Spike full binary — subprocess oracle for commit-log diff), R8 (ChampSim — benchmark methodology reference for IPC microbench suite structure).

File Adds
ooo-core/tests/riscv_tests.rs harness: load ELF → OooCore::run(max=10M) → check tohost==1; cosim on every commit
ooo-cli cosim subcmd extend: --spike <path> → spawn Spike --log-commits, parse, lockstep-diff
ooo-core/src/cosim.rs SpikeOracle { proc: Child }, fn next_commit(&mut self) -> CommitRecord; CommitRecord { pc, inst, rd, wdata }
ooo-fuzz (new dev crate) RandProgGen { seed, len, mem_window } → Vec<Inst>; fn gen() -> Program; arbitrary via rand_chacha (deterministic from seed)
ooo-core/benches/throughput.rs criterion: bench_run(corpus) → sim-cycles/s; bench_ipc(corpus) → IPC table
scripts/build-riscv-tests.sh clone+build riscv-tests at pinned SHA; emit fixtures/riscv-tests/*.elf
  • riscv-tests harness: ELF loader → flat memory; tohost/fromhost MMIO trap → terminate; PASS iff tohost==1, FAIL decodes test number tohost>>1.
  • Spike oracle: subprocess, spike -l --log-commits --isa=rv64im pk-less bare ELF; parse commit log lines core 0: 0x<pc> (0x<inst>) x<rd> 0x<wdata>; one CommitRecord per retire.
  • Diff loop: advance OooCore to next commit, pull RobEntry retire → (pc, dst_arch, wdata from PhysRegFile), compare vs SpikeOracle::next_commit; mismatch → emit bisection report (first divergent PC + arch-reg deltas).
  • RandProgGen: weighted opcode mix (ALU 50%, MUL/DIV 8%, branch 22%, load/store 20%); branch targets bounded forward/back within program; loads/stores masked to mem_window (aligned); inject x0 writes, max-int DIV (overflow), div-by-zero; guarantee termination via a decrementing loop-counter register + cycle budget.
  • Cosim invariant under fuzz: run program on OooCore with internal golden ArchState (Phase 12) — every commit must match; no Spike needed for fuzz (self-checking), Spike used for static corpus.

25.3 Deliverable

cargo test -p ooo-core --test riscv_tests green; oooc cosim --spike clean on corpus; oooc bench + cargo bench produce target/criterion + reports/ipc.md + reports/throughput.md; fuzz target (cargo +nightly fuzz run cosim or seeded loop) runs 10M instructions with zero divergence/panic/hang.

25.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
rv64ui buckets 100% PASS (all ~40 tests)
rv64um buckets 100% PASS (mul/div/rem)
Spike diff on corpus (≥20 progs, ≥1M dyn inst total) 0 divergences
Fuzz budget ≥10M dyn instructions, 0 cosim divergence, 0 panic, 0 hang (every prog commits within budget)
Sim throughput ≥1.0M sim-cycles/s (release, default 4-wide config)
IPC microbenches within ±5% of published H&P/gem5 expectations; reported per-bench
Regression gate (CI) throughput drop ≤3%, IPC drift ≤1% vs baseline JSON

25.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
fixtures/riscv-tests/rv64ui-p-add.elf tohost==1 (PASS); cosim 0 diverge
fixtures/riscv-tests/rv64um-p-div.elf PASS; div-by-zero → all-ones, overflow → MIN per spec
fixtures/corpus/dhrystone.hex, linpack-tiny.hex Spike diff clean; IPC logged
fixtures/fuzz/seed_000.bin (replay seed) deterministic identical commit stream across 2 runs
Malformed: bad_tohost.elf (no tohost sym) harness errors MissingTohost, not hang
Malformed: truncated ELF / illegal opcode golden raises Trap::IllegalInst; cosim agrees Spike traps; no panic
Malformed: infinite loop prog (no progress) cycle-budget abort → RunResult::Timeout, reported not crashed

25.6 Pitfalls

  • Spike log mismatch on x0 writes: Spike omits x0-dest commits from log; OooCore retires them. Root cause: ABI nop. Fix: filter rd==x0 both sides before diff. Debug: dump both streams, align on PC.
  • fence/CSR commits diverge: Spike logs CSR side-effects; minimal CSR model differs. Fix: whitelist-skip CSR/fence/ecall records in diff (out of timing-model scope). Debug: tag record kind.
  • Spike PC-ordering ≠ commit-order under speculation: ensure we diff committed (retired) stream only, never speculative writeback. Bug shows as phantom extra commits at mispredict. Debug: assert ROB-head monotonic PC.
  • Fuzz non-termination: unbounded branch backedge → infinite loop, masked as "hang." Root cause: gen didn't bound loop trips. Fix: mandatory countdown reg + budget abort != failure. Debug: log committed count at timeout.
  • Memory-aliased fuzz loads: uninit memory reads diverge if cosim and timing model seed memory differently. Fix: zero-init both; share one memory image. Debug: first-load PC.
  • DIV/REM edge semantics: INT_MIN / -1 overflow + x/0 must match RV spec exactly; off-by-one in golden vs FU. Debug: targeted directed test before fuzz.

25.7 Performance Concerns

  • Spike subprocess IPC (pipe parsing) dominates diff wall-time — batch-read log, parse with a hand-rolled scanner (no regex) → keep diff at ≥500K commits/s.
  • Fuzz throughput: reuse one OooCore (reset, not realloc) per program; pool PhysRegFile/ROB vecs to avoid per-prog allocation churn.
  • Criterion: pin CPU governor / taskset; warm-up ≥3 iters; measure release lto=thin; exclude ELF-load from timed region.
  • Throughput counted as Stats.cycles / wall_seconds; report also dyn-inst/s (IPC-normalized).

25.8 Edge Cases

  • Self-modifying code in fuzz: forbid stores into text window (gen invariant) — timing model has no I-cache invalidation.
  • Misaligned load/store: golden + FU must both trap (or both emulate) identically; pick trap, assert in diff.
  • Empty program / single ecall: harness must PASS-or-clean-exit, not divide-by-zero on IPC (cycles==0 guard → IPC=0).
  • Max ROB pressure fuzz: long dependency chain saturates RS — verify no deadlock (select starvation), commits still drain.
  • Branch-heavy fuzz: mispredict flush every few inst — recovery path stress; assert checkpoint/free-list integrity post-squash.

25.9 Failure Analysis

Symptom Likely cause Triage
riscv-test FAIL tohost=N failed assertion N in test map N→test source, check that opcode's FU/golden
Cosim diverge at PC X timing path commits wrong value (rename/bypass/LSQ bug) bisect: dump RAT, PhysReg ready, LSQ forward at X
Spike diff but self-cosim clean Spike-config mismatch (ISA string, reset PC, CSR) align spike args; check whitelist filters
Fuzz hang non-terminating gen prog confirm budget abort fires; tighten gen termination
Throughput regression alloc in hot tick path / lost inlining flamegraph tick(); diff vs baseline criterion JSON
IPC drift scheduler/predictor logic change per-bench IPC table delta; bisect commit

25.10 Operational Notes

  • CI matrix: riscv-tests job (debug, full cosim), bench job (release, regression gate vs committed baseline.json), fuzz job (nightly, time-boxed 30 min, corpus minimized + checked in on new crash).
  • Pin riscv-tests + Spike to fixed SHAs in scripts/; cache built ELFs as CI artifact.
  • oooc bench --json emits machine-readable report; gate script compares + fails PR on threshold breach; update baseline only via explicit --bless.
  • Reproducibility: every fuzz finding reduced to a seed + config; replay via oooc run --seed --config; deterministic (Phase 12 invariant).
  • Reports published to reports/ (IPC table, throughput, predictor accuracy, cache MPKI) — the project's final artifact set.

Threat Model

Trust boundary

   UNTRUSTED                         |   TRUSTED (oooc simulator process)
   ---------------------------------+------------------------------------------
   guest program (ELF/hex bytes)    |   ooo-isa decode + execute_functional
   guest data / initial memory      |   OooCore tick()/run() engine
   ConfigParams (CLI knobs, JSON)   |   golden ArchState co-sim
   random fuzz programs (Phase 13)  |   bounded guest memory arena, Stats
  • Boundary = the simulator OS process. Guest code is data interpreted by a model, never executed natively, never lifted/JITted — it cannot escape the model, issue syscalls, touch host memory, or affect host control flow. No unsafe reachable from guest-controlled paths; #![forbid(unsafe_code)] in ooo-isa/-frontend/-rename/-schedule/-rob.
  • Two untrusted inputs only: (a) the guest program + its memory image, (b) the ConfigParams. Everything reachable from those is hardened below.
  • Threat goal: a hostile or malformed guest/config must yield a bounded, deterministic, diagnostic outcome (trap, fault, validation error, or divergence report) — never host crash, panic, hang, OOM, UB, or silent wrong IPC.

Threat enumeration

# Threat Vector Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Non-terminating guest (infinite loop / livelock) guest never reaches halt run(max_cycles) hard bound; wfi/ebreak halt detect; no-commit watchdog (N cycles w/ 0 commit ⇒ deadlock) cycle counter ≥ max_cycles; Stats.committed stalls cycles, committed, last-PC, halt reason clean exit code 2 (MaxCyclesReached); dump partial Stats
T2 Illegal / undefined / reserved opcode crafted Inst.raw decode() returns Opcode incl. Illegal; never panic on unknown bits decode classifies non-RV64IM ⇒ Trap::IllegalInstruction mispredicts-style trap counter; offending PC + raw precise exception at commit; ROB squash younger; run returns trapped Stats
T3 Memory-access DoS (huge/sparse addresses, unbounded growth) loads/stores to arbitrary 64-bit VA guest memory = bounded arena (cap mem_bytes, default 256 MiB); paged sparse map w/ hard ceiling; no host allocation per guest addr addr ∉ arena ⇒ access fault; arena alloc would exceed cap fault count, faulting VA, arena high-water Trap::LoadAccessFault/StoreAccessFault; precise at commit
T4 Pathological ConfigParams (gigantic rob_size/phys_regs/lsq) CLI/JSON knobs ConfigParams::validate() pre-run: range caps (rob_size≤4096, phys_regs≤2048, widths 1..=16, lsq_size≤512); reject NaN/zero widths validation runs before any allocation rejected-config error w/ offending field+value+limit exit code 64 (EX_USAGE); no allocation performed
T5 Divergence-injection (timing/function mismatch program) program that surfaces a microarch bug golden co-sim compares committed ArchState every commit (Phase 12) first mismatching arch reg/PC at commit ⇒ Divergence committing PC, expected vs actual reg, ROB idx, cycle report, not crash: halt at first divergence; bisection harness narrows to instr
T6 Resource-accounting bug (PhysReg leak / double-free in FreeList) dispatch/commit/squash interleavings FreeList invariants: free ∪ in-use ∪ RAT-mapped = phys_regs; alloc from non-empty only; commit frees exactly one old_phys; squash restores via RenameCheckpoint debug_assert! on every alloc/free; periodic conservation audit (free_count + mapped == phys_regs) leak/double-free assert w/ PhysReg id, cycle abort in debug; in release, conservation-audit error halts run before corruption spreads
T7 Integer overflow in cycle/counter math >2^64 cycles, counter wrap Cycle(u64) + Stats counters use checked_add/saturating_add; busy_until saturating; debug overflow-checks on overflow ⇒ None on checked path saturated-counter warning flag in report saturate + flag; IPC computed from saturated values marked unreliable
T8 Supply-chain risk (malicious/yanked transitive dep) crate deps deny.toml (advisories deny, license allowlist, source = crates.io only); minimal deps (no_std core crates use only alloc); cargo audit in CI; pinned Cargo.lock cargo deny check gate in CI SBOM artifact; advisory IDs CI fails closed; no release on advisory
T9 Operator mistake (wrong config ⇒ misread IPC) human runs wrong knobs echo full resolved ConfigParams in every report (run/trace/stats/cosim/bench); config hash in header n/a (prevention) config block + config_sha256 in every Stats dump re-run with corrected, comparable hash
T10 Malformed ELF/hex loader input truncated/lying headers loader bounds-checks every section vs arena; rejects overlapping/out-of-range segments; entry PC must be in arena parse-time validation loader error w/ offset/field exit code 65 (EX_DATAERR); nothing executed
T11 Non-determinism leak (hidden state ⇒ unrepeatable replay) HashMap iteration order, time, RNG deterministic structures (indexed Vec, BTreeMap); single seeded RNG for fuzz/replay; no wall-clock in model replay from (seed, config) must bit-match prior Stats replay-mismatch diff divergence report; treat as T5-class bug
T12 Pathological branch/predictor state (RAS/BTB blowup, history poisoning) adversarial branch streams fixed-size Btb/Ras/Gshare tables (config-capped); RAS wraps, never grows; mispredict recovery via checkpoint is O(1)-bounded table indices masked to size; recovery depth bounded by rob_size mispredict rate, RAS over/underflow counters bounded flush; no unbounded work per cycle

Invariants enforced under attack

  • Bounded work per cycle: every tick() does ≤ O(issue_width × rs_size + rob_size) work; no guest input induces unbounded per-cycle cost (T1, T12).
  • No panic on guest data: any path reachable from guest bytes returns Result/Trap, never panic!/unwrap on guest-controlled values. Panics reserved for simulator-internal invariant breaks (T6) and are themselves bounded.
  • Conservation: PhysReg count, ROB occupancy, LSQ occupancy are conserved across dispatch/commit/squash; audited (T6).
  • Memory containment: guest VA space ⊆ arena; every access fault-checked before host indexing; no host pointer derived from guest value (T3).
  • Bounded counters: all cycle/stat arithmetic checked or saturating; IPC denominator never zero (guarded) (T7).

Severity / response classification

Class Examples Response
Contained guest fault T2, T3, T10 precise trap/fault, continue or clean halt, report
Bounded resource limit T1, T4, T12 reject (config) or bounded halt (cycles), diagnostic exit code
Correctness alarm T5, T6, T11 halt at first detection, emit divergence/conservation report, never proceed silently
Build/release gate T8 CI fails closed before artifact
Operator UX T9 echo config + hash; prevention-only

Out-of-scope (state explicitly)

  • No sandboxing of the host process beyond Rust safety + bounded arena — oooc is a developer tool, not a hostile-multitenant service; OS-level isolation (seccomp/containers) is the operator's responsibility.
  • No side-channel / timing-attack resistance: the model deliberately exposes microarchitectural timing (that is its purpose); it is not a constant-time engine.
  • No privileged/OS threat surface (minimal trap model only; full M/S/U privilege, paging exploits are PhaseDeferred).
  • No multi-core / coherence attack surface (single-core; MESI is project 187).

Failure Modes & Recovery

Taxonomy of how the simulator fails, ordered by severity. F1 (cosim divergence) and F2 (deadlock) are the two highest-severity classes — they invalidate every result. Each entry: symptoms → root causes → detection → automatic recovery → manual recovery → operator actions → forensic artifacts → long-term mitigation.

Severity scale

Sev Meaning Examples Gate
S0 results silently wrong F1 cosim divergence, F6 counter inconsistency block release; fix before any IPC number is trusted
S1 sim wedged, no progress F2 deadlock, F5 livelock, F7 non-termination block release
S2 internal invariant broken, may corrupt state F3 PhysReg leak/double-free, F4 RAT-restore mismatch, F4b S2L forwarding error, F9 predictor-state leak, F10 MSHR coalescing failure block release
S3 crash on bad input F8 panic on adversarial input harden; must degrade to Trap, not panic

Failure catalog

ID Name Sev Primary suspect crate
F1 Cosim divergence (committed != golden) S0 ooo-rename / ooo-rob / ooo-execute
F2 Commit deadlock (0 commits for K cycles) S1 ooo-schedule (wakeup/select) / ooo-mem
F3 PhysReg leak / double-free S2 ooo-rename FreeList / ooo-rob CommitUnit
F4 RAT-restore mismatch after mispredict S2 ooo-rename RenameCheckpoint / ooo-rob Flush
F4b Store-to-load forwarding error S2 ooo-mem LoadStoreQueue
F5 Livelock (issue oscillation) S1 ooo-schedule select / age order
F6 Counter inconsistency (commit count != golden) S0 ooo-rob CommitUnit / Stats
F7 Non-termination (run never returns) S1 ooo-core run loop / ooo-frontend redirect
F8 Panic on adversarial input S3 ooo-isa decode / ooo-mem addr
F9 Predictor state corruption after squash (ghost correlations) S2 ooo-frontend BranchPredictor / Btb/Ras/Gshare/TageLite
F10 MSHR coalescing failure (duplicate/lost fill signal) S2 ooo-mem Cache MSHR

F1 — Cosim divergence (committed architectural state != golden) [S0, gravest]

The defining correctness failure: at commit of instruction i, the timing model's retired ArchState (xreg / pc / csr) disagrees with execute_functional replay. Timing may differ from real silicon, but the sequence of committed architectural states must be bit-exact with the golden model.

  • Symptoms: oooc cosim aborts with DIVERGE @ commit=<n> pc=<hex> reg=x<k> got=<hex> exp=<hex>; or a downstream conformance test (riscv-tests) fails on output check while in-order baseline passes.
  • Root causes (ranked by empirical frequency):
    1. Renaming bug — stale RAT mapping, wrong old_phys recorded, x0 accidentally renamed (must always read 0).
    2. Recovery bug — RenameCheckpoint restores wrong RAT/FreeList head after mispredict; ROB squash off-by-one (squashes the branch itself or spares a younger entry).
    3. Forwarding bug — BypassNetwork delivers a value one cycle early (same-cycle teleport) or to the wrong PhysReg; src_ready set without the matching dst broadcast.
    4. S2L forwarding returns wrong store value or wrong size (see F4b).
    5. Wrong functional semantics in ooo-isa (sign/zero extension, M-ext DIV-by-zero / overflow corner per RISC-V manual).
  • Detection: lock-step compare every commit (Phase 12). After each CommitUnit retire, replay the same DecodedInst through golden ArchState, diff xregs/pc. Cheap (one functional step per commit); always on under cosim, sampled under run.
  • Automatic recovery: none at runtime — divergence is a correctness violation, not a transient. The harness halts at the first divergent commit (fail-fast) to preserve the smallest reproducer; it does not attempt to continue.
  • Manual recovery (divergence bisection — the canonical methodology):
    1. Re-run with --trace --from-cycle=<C-2000> to dump per-cycle pipeline state (FetchBuffer, RAT, FreeList, RS entries, ROB head/tail, LSQ) around the failing commit.
    2. Binary-search in time: the last commit where state matched golden bounds the bug below; walk forward to the first mismatch. The producing instruction of the wrong reg is the suspect.
    3. Trace that PhysReg backward: rename (which RobIdx allocated it, what old_phys), wakeup/select (who broadcast it), execute/bypass (what value, what cycle), to localize crate.
    4. Bisect in config: shrink to minimal ConfigParams that still diverges (width=1, rob_size small, single FU). If divergence vanishes at width=1 it is a superscalar/port-contention or checkpoint-aliasing bug.
  • Operator actions: never publish IPC from a build with any open S0; pin the failing seed+config in CI as a permanent regression fixture.
  • Forensic artifacts: diverge.json {commit_idx, pc, reg, got, exp, seed, config}; the ±2000-cycle trace; the minimized program; the producing-instruction provenance chain.
  • Long-term mitigation: golden co-sim wired from Phase 1 (in-order baseline) so divergence is caught before OoO complexity lands; per-commit assert that RobEntry.dst_phys value == golden result; invariant RAT[0] == phys-zero checked each rename; fuzzed random-program differential (Phase 13) on every CI run.

F2 — Commit deadlock (no instruction commits for K cycles) [S1]

ROB head never retires; Stats.committed flat while Stats.cycles climbs. Watchdog: K = 10_000 cycles with zero commits ⇒ declare deadlock.

  • Symptoms: oooc run hangs; watchdog fires DEADLOCK: 0 commits in 10000 cycles, ROB head=<idx> done=false.
  • Root causes:
    • Wakeup never fires — completing FU did not broadcast its dst PhysReg, so a dependent RsEntry's src_ready stays false forever (the classic missed-wakeup).
    • Select starves — never picks the ROB-head's producer (age order broken; younger always wins).
    • Resource leak — FreeList exhausted (see F3) so rename stalls, dispatch stalls, ROB head's operand never produced.
    • Memory hang — load waits on an MSHR/store-address that never resolves; LSQ disambiguation forces a wait on an older store whose addr_ready never gets set.
    • Circular structural stall — ROB full waiting on head, head waiting on RS, RS waiting on FreeList, FreeList drained by uncommitted entries.
  • Detection: deadlock watchdog in OooCore::run; plus per-cycle "head not done" counter. Augment with a forward-progress invariant: in any window of rob_size + max_latency cycles, at least one of {fetch, commit, an FU completion} must occur.
  • Automatic recovery: none for correctness; watchdog terminates the run and dumps state. (No flush — a flush would mask the bug.)
  • Manual recovery: dump the dependency graph at the wedge — for the ROB-head instruction, list its source PhysRegs, who should produce them, and that producer's RS state (issued? ready? in-flight FU? busy_until?). A "ready producer that never issues" ⇒ select bug; an "issued producer whose dst was never broadcast" ⇒ wakeup bug; a "load waiting on unknown store addr" ⇒ disambiguation bug.
  • Operator actions: capture the seed+config; reduce K for faster repro in CI of the minimized case.
  • Forensic artifacts: deadlock.dump {cycle, ROB head/tail + each entry's done/exception, every RsEntry {src ready bits, issued, fu, rob_idx}, FreeList depth, LSQ pending loads + their blocking store}.
  • Long-term mitigation: assert "every issued RsEntry eventually broadcasts within latency+1 cycles"; assert FreeList depth + in-flight phys == phys_regs; cap any LSQ load wait and flag if exceeded.

F3 — PhysReg leak / double-free [S2]

Physical register accounting drifts: a PhysReg is returned to the FreeList twice, or never returned, eventually exhausting it (⇒ F2 deadlock) or aliasing a live value (⇒ F1 divergence).

  • Symptoms: FreeList underflows (leak — no frees) or contains duplicates (double-free); often surfaces downstream as F2 or F1.
  • Root causes: CommitUnit frees dst_phys instead of old_phys; flush path frees PhysRegs of squashed entries that rename had already rolled back via checkpoint (double-free); checkpoint restore rolls FreeList head but commit also frees the same regs.
  • Detection: continuous conservation invariant — free_count + sum(allocated_to_live_rob_entries) == phys_regs checked every tick; free-of-already-free detected by a per-PhysReg is_free shadow bit (debug builds).
  • Automatic recovery: none; assert-fail in debug, hard-stop in release with a leak report.
  • Manual recovery: replay with phys-reg lifetime logging — log every alloc (rename, RobIdx) and free (commit vs flush) with cycle; the duplicate/missing free is in that log.
  • Operator actions: treat any conservation-invariant breach as release-blocking.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-PhysReg lifetime log; FreeList depth time-series.
  • Long-term mitigation: single ownership rule — only commit frees old_phys; only checkpoint-restore rolls the FreeList head; the two paths are mutually exclusive per instruction. Encode as debug_assert at both sites.

F4 — RAT-restore mismatch after mispredict [S2]

After a branch mispredict, the restored RAT/FreeList does not match what rename had at the branch, so post-recovery instructions read stale physical mappings ⇒ F1.

  • Symptoms: divergence specifically after a mispredict (clean before the first branch); oooc trace shows post-flush RAT != checkpoint RAT.
  • Root causes: RenameCheckpoint captured after (not before) renaming the branch's own dst; checkpoint taken at wrong pipeline point; FreeList head not part of the checkpoint; nested/in-flight checkpoints overwritten (checkpoint table too small for outstanding branches).
  • Detection: on flush, assert restored RAT equals the checkpoint snapshot bit-for-bit; cosim divergence localized to first post-mispredict commit.
  • Automatic recovery: the flush is the recovery path (restore RAT + roll FreeList + squash ROB/RS/LSQ younger than branch + redirect Fetch); the failure is when that path is itself buggy.
  • Manual recovery: dump checkpoint at the mispredicting branch's rename and compare against post-flush RAT; verify FreeList head matches RenameCheckpoint.free_head.
  • Operator actions: ensure checkpoint capacity ≥ max in-flight branches in config.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-branch checkpoint snapshots; flush event log {branch RobIdx, restored head, squashed range}.
  • Long-term mitigation: capture checkpoint before allocating the branch's dst; checkpoint = {rat, free_head} exactly; bound outstanding branches to checkpoint-table size and stall rename otherwise.

F4b — Store-to-load forwarding error [S2]

A load receives a wrong value from the LSQ: wrong store matched, partial-overlap mishandled, or a violation missed.

  • Symptoms: divergence on a load result; only with overlapping load/store addresses.
  • Root causes: matched a younger store (must forward from the youngest older store that fully covers the load); size/alignment overlap mishandled (partial overlap must not forward — must wait); forwarded from a store whose value wasn't ready; speculated past an unknown older-store address without a commit-time violation check.
  • Detection: cosim divergence on the load; debug assert "forwarded store is older + fully covers + value ready".
  • Automatic recovery: on a detected ordering violation at commit, squash the load and younger instructions, re-execute (memory-ordering replay).
  • Manual recovery: dump LSQ at the load's issue — list older stores, their addr_ready/value/overlap; identify the mis-selected forwarder.
  • Forensic artifacts: LSQ snapshot + forwarding decision log {load RobIdx, chosen store RobIdx, overlap bytes}.
  • Long-term mitigation: conservative default (unknown older-store addr ⇒ load waits) until disambiguation hardened; explicit full-cover-or-wait rule; commit-time violation detector always on.

F5 — Livelock (issue oscillation) [S1]

Instructions issue but the same set re-issues/replays without net forward progress; commits stall though FUs are busy.

  • Symptoms: nonzero issue activity, ~0 commits; issued toggling on the same RsEntries.
  • Root causes: select not age-ordered (two ready entries ping-pong for one port); speculative load replay loop (violation → squash → re-issue → re-violate); bypass marks ready then writeback clears it.
  • Detection: same forward-progress watchdog as F2 but with nonzero issue count ⇒ classify as livelock; per-RsEntry issue-count cap.
  • Automatic recovery: none; watchdog terminates.
  • Manual recovery: trace select decisions per port per cycle; confirm strict oldest-ready (lowest RobIdx age) ordering.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-port select log; per-RsEntry issue counter.
  • Long-term mitigation: select MUST be deterministic oldest-ready; issued is monotone until completion (never re-cleared by bypass); cap replay attempts per load.

F6 — Counter inconsistency (committed count != golden instruction count) [S0]

Stats.committed disagrees with the number of golden functional steps for the same program — a counting bug even if register state happens to match.

  • Symptoms: cosim passes register checks but committed != golden_steps; IPC computed off a wrong numerator.
  • Root causes: double-counting a commit; counting squashed/speculative instructions as committed; not counting a fused/multi-uop entry once.
  • Detection: at end-of-run assert Stats.committed == golden_step_count; per-commit the golden model steps exactly once.
  • Automatic recovery: none; hard-stop.
  • Manual recovery: diff commit-count vs golden-step-count over time to find the cycle where they part.
  • Forensic artifacts: committed-vs-golden count time-series.
  • Long-term mitigation: golden steps only on confirmed (non-squashed) commit; committed += 1 at exactly one site in CommitUnit.

F7 — Non-termination (run never returns) [S1]

run(max_cycles) exceeds budget without the program reaching its halt/ecall.

  • Symptoms: hits max_cycles cap; distinct from F2 (commits are happening, just never reaching the end — e.g., a misredirect loop).
  • Root causes: branch predictor + fetch redirect oscillating on the same PC; golden program lacks a halt and the cap is too low; an infinite loop in the workload (legitimate — operator misconfig).
  • Detection: max_cycles budget always set; compare committed-count growth (F2 vs F7 split).
  • Automatic recovery: run returns Stats with a terminated_by_cap flag rather than looping forever.
  • Manual recovery: inspect committed PC stream for a cycle; if it advances normally the workload simply needs a larger cap; if it loops on a redirect, it's a frontend bug.
  • Forensic artifacts: tail committed-PC stream; redirect log.
  • Long-term mitigation: mandatory max_cycles; CI workloads carry known cycle bounds; frontend redirect must monotonically follow resolved branch outcome.

F8 — Panic on adversarial input [S3]

Malformed ELF/hex, illegal opcode, or out-of-range address panics instead of trapping.

  • Symptoms: Rust panic / unwrap on decode, immediate extraction, or MemRequest.addr out of bounds.
  • Root causes: decode unwraps an unknown opcode instead of returning an illegal-instruction Trap; address arithmetic overflow; LSQ indexes past bounds.
  • Detection: fuzzing (Phase 13 random-program generator) + property test "decode never panics; returns Trap on illegal".
  • Automatic recovery: convert to architectural Trap (illegal-instruction / load-fault) routed through precise-exception handling — degrade gracefully, never abort the process.
  • Manual recovery: minimize the fuzz input; add as a decode/trap unit fixture.
  • Forensic artifacts: panic backtrace + the minimized input bytes.
  • Long-term mitigation: no unwrap/panic on any input-derived path in ooo-isa/ooo-mem; all illegal/fault conditions become Option<Trap>; fuzz target on every CI run.

F9 — Predictor state corruption after squash (ghost correlations) [S2]

Speculative predictor history (global/path history registers, Ras depth) is not restored exactly on squash, so wrong-path training data permanently poisons subsequent indexing (INV-11 violation) — a silent, non-crashing accuracy regression rather than a crash or divergence.

  • Symptoms: measured prediction accuracy drifts below the ≥90% (gshare) / ≥95% (TageLite) targets (§4.7) after a change to fetch/squash code, with no cosim divergence and no crash — the model still runs, just predicts worse.
  • Root causes: global/path history register not checkpointed/restored on squash (wrong-path history poisons subsequent indexing); Ras depth not rolled back on squash (stale return addresses mispredict later Jalrs); TageLite useful-bit aging updated from a squashed (not-yet-resolved-correctly) prediction instead of only on confirmed resolution.
  • Detection: accuracy-regression CI gate (§28.6 SLO) comparing a fixed mispredict-heavy corpus run against the pinned baseline; a widening delta flags a leak (INV-11).
  • Automatic recovery: none — this is silent degradation, not a crash; only the CI accuracy gate catches it.
  • Manual recovery: dump the predictor history register value at checkpoint time (branch fetch) vs. at squash-restore time; diff to localize which structure (history register, RAS, useful-bit) failed to roll back.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-branch predictor-checkpoint log; accuracy time-series across CI runs.
  • Long-term mitigation: predictor checkpoint captured/restored symmetrically with RenameCheckpoint (INV-11); accuracy-floor CI gate blocks any merge that regresses gshare/TageLite below their SLO (§28.6).

F10 — MSHR coalescing failure (duplicate or lost fill signal) [S2]

A second miss to an already-outstanding cache line allocates a redundant MSHR entry (violates INV-12) instead of joining the existing waiter list, or the fill-time waiter-dequeue logic signals a waiter zero or more-than-one times.

  • Symptoms: either a load blocked on a miss never wakes (surfaces as an apparent F2-class hang, but the root cause is memory-side coalescing, not the scheduler), or a wakeup fires twice for one completing load, double-marking a since-reused PhysReg ready (an INV-4-adjacent corruption vector).
  • Root causes: MSHR lookup keyed incorrectly (e.g. by request index instead of line address) so a second miss to the same line misses the coalescing check; fill-time waiter-list dequeue loop off-by-one (skips the last waiter or double-iterates the first).
  • Detection: debug assert MSHR occupancy ≤ mshr_count (structural, INV-8/INV-12-adjacent); a per-waiter signalled bit asserted false→true exactly once, never twice.
  • Automatic recovery: none; assert-fail in debug on double-signal, hard-stop in release; a lost signal is caught by the same deadlock watchdog as F2.
  • Manual recovery: dump the MSHR table {line_addr, waiter_list, fill_cycle}; compare the intended waiter count against the delivered signal count for the offending line.
  • Forensic artifacts: MSHR occupancy time-series; per-line waiter/signal log.
  • Long-term mitigation: single coalescing invariant enforced at the Cache boundary — one MSHR entry per line, an explicit waiter list, dequeue-and-signal-exactly-once on fill (INV-12); a property test injects concurrent same-line misses and asserts exactly one MSHR entry plus all waiters signalled exactly once.

Cross-cutting debugging methodology

Tool Purpose
oooc trace --from-cycle/--to-cycle per-cycle dump of all stage state (the primary forensic instrument)
oooc cosim lock-step golden compare; fail-fast at first divergence (F1/F6)
deadlock/livelock watchdog K-cycle no-commit detection (F2/F5) split by issue activity
conservation + checkpoint asserts catch F3/F4 at the breach, not downstream
config bisection width=1 / single-FU minimal repro to localize superscalar vs core bugs
seed pinning every failure becomes a deterministic CI regression fixture

Determinism is the meta-mitigation: deterministic replay from {seed, config} (Phase 12) guarantees every failure above reproduces exactly, so divergence/deadlock bisection always converges to the same offending cycle.

Operational Readiness

Framing: oooc is a CLI simulator + research crate set, not a service. No uptime, no on-call rotation, no request latency. "Operational readiness" = reproducible experiments, trustworthy numbers, fast triage of wrong/slow/nondeterministic runs. The crate returns data (Stats + trace records); it does not log to stderr in steady state.

28.1 Metrics surface (the Stats set)

All counters live on ooo-core::Stats, incremented at their stage, snapshot at end of run(). Derived ratios computed at report time.

Metric Source Definition Target / sanity band
IPC committed / cycles retired insts per cycle 0.3–3.0 (config-bound by issue_width)
mispredict rate mispredicts / branches resolved-wrong branches gshare ≤12%, TageLite ≤6% on SPEC-int-like
cache miss rate cache_misses / mem_accesses per CacheLevel L1 ≤8%, L2 ≤30% (workload-dep)
ROB occupancy per-cycle head..tail fill mean + p50/p95 / rob_size <100% (else ROB-full stalls)
RS occupancy live RsEntry count mean+p95 / rs_size watch saturation
LSQ occupancy live LsqEntry count loads+stores / lsq_size watch saturation
FU utilization busy_until busy-cycles / total per FuKind Div/Mul low; Alu high
stall-cause histogram dispatch/issue stall reason tally see 28.2 identifies the binding constraint
sim throughput wall-clock host cycles/sec ≥1M sim-cycles/sec single-thread

ipc = committed as f64 / cycles. Occupancy + utilization require per-cycle accumulation (sum of fill counts ÷ cycles); they are not end-state reads.

28.2 Stall-cause histogram (the diagnostic core)

Every cycle, each pipeline stage that fails to advance records exactly one primary stall cause (highest-priority structural blocker). Tallied into a histogram.

StallCause = FetchBufferFull | FrontendMispredictRedirect | RenameNoFreePhysReg
           | RobFull | RsFull | LsqFull | NoReadyToIssue | FuBusy(FuKind)
           | LoadMissPending | StoreToLoadWait | DispatchWidthExhausted | None

Reading: a workload dominated by RobFull → window too small (raise rob_size); RenameNoFreePhysReg → raise phys_regs; FuBusy(Div) → add a Div FU / it is divide-bound; NoReadyToIssue → dependency-chain (RAW) limited, not structural; LoadMissPending → memory-bound, tune cache. The histogram + occupancy together pinpoint the bottleneck before touching any knob.

28.3 Structured tracing (per-cycle pipeline dump)

Trace is data, not log lines: a Vec<CycleTrace> (or NDJSON stream from the trace subcommand). One record per cycle.

CycleTrace { cycle: Cycle,
  occupancy: { fetch, decode, rename, dispatch, rs, rob, lsq } (counts),
  insts: [ InstTrace { rob_idx: RobIdx, pc: u64, op: Opcode,
                       ts: { fetch, decode, rename, dispatch, issue, exec, wb, commit }, // Cycle per stage, None if not reached
                       phys_dst: PhysReg, mispredict: bool, stall: StallCause } ],
  stalls: { per-StallCause count this cycle } }

Per-instruction stage timestamps make a pipeline waterfall trivial to render (Gantt per rob_idx). trace subcommand emits NDJSON to stdout; size-bounded by --max-cycles / --trace-window C0:C1 to avoid GB dumps on >1M-cycle runs.

Cosim divergence log (separate, produced by cosim): on the first commit whose architectural result ≠ execute_functional golden state, emit one Divergence record and stop:

Divergence { cycle, rob_idx, pc, reg: RegId,
             got: u64, expected: u64,           // xreg or pc mismatch
             golden_pc: u64, sim_pc: u64,
             recent_commits: [last N RobIdx+pc] } // bisection breadcrumb

28.4 Logging philosophy

  • Library crates (ooo-isa..ooo-rob, ooo-core) never print. They return Stats, Vec<CycleTrace>, Result<_, SimError>. Determinism forbids hidden I/O in the tick() loop.
  • Only ooo-cli does I/O. tracing crate behind a feature flag (trace-events), default off — enabled tracing perturbs nothing in the model (events are emitted from CLI-level wrappers, not inside tick()).
  • Levels: error = SimError (bad ELF, config invalid, divergence); warn = soft caps hit (trace truncated); info = run summary; debug/trace = redundant with CycleTrace (prefer the data path).
  • No metric is computed from log scraping. Everything quantitative flows through Stats/CycleTrace.

28.5 Experiment dashboard (research harness)

Not a Grafana/ops dashboard — an experiment report generated from Stats + CycleTrace (CLI stats --format json → notebook/plot).

Panel Input Purpose
IPC bar / sweep Stats.ipc across configs compare knob settings
Stall breakdown (stacked) stall histogram which constraint binds, per config
Occupancy time-series CycleTrace.occupancy ROB/RS/LSQ fill over time, find saturation
FU utilization per-FuKind busy% rebalance FU mix / ports
Pipeline waterfall InstTrace.ts latency anatomy of a hot region
Mispredict + cache-miss rates frontend / memory health

Harness reads a manifest (28.7), runs the sweep, collects stats.json per point, plots. No live streaming; runs are batch + finite (run(max_cycles)).

28.6 SLO-style targets

Target Threshold Enforced by
Architectural equivalence zero divergence on riscv-tests + fuzz (Phase 12/13) cosim exit code; CI gate
Determinism identical Stats + commit trace for identical (seed, config, program) byte-compare two runs in CI
Sim throughput ≥1M sim-cycles/sec (release, single core, no trace) bench subcommand regression
Throughput regression ≤10% slowdown vs last tagged release CI bench compare
Prediction accuracy floor gshare ≥88%, TageLite ≥94% on the standard kernel set Phase 3 conformance

"Zero divergence" is the hard SLO — any commit mismatch is a P0 correctness bug, not a tuning issue.

28.7 Reproducible experiment manifest

Single TOML pins everything needed to reproduce a number. config subcommand validates; run/cosim/bench consume it.

[sim]
program  = "bench/dhrystone.elf"   # or .hex
max_cycles = 5_000_000
seed     = 0xC0FFEE                 # RNG for fuzz / any tie-break randomness

[config]                            # -> ConfigParams
fetch_width = 4
issue_width = 4
commit_width = 4
rob_size = 192
rs_size = 64
lsq_size = 48
phys_regs = 256
predictor = "TageLite"             # PredictorKind

[output]
stats = "out/dhrystone.stats.json"
trace_window = "0:0"               # off; "1000:2000" for windowed dump

Manifest + oooc version (semver) + git SHA fully determine the output. Manifests are checked into the experiment repo alongside the resulting stats.json.

28.8 Deployment as a crate

  • Distributed as a Cargo workspace; ooo-cli ships the oooc binary, lib crates publishable individually.
  • Semver: ConfigParams fields, Stats fields, CycleTrace/Divergence schema, and CLI flags are the public contract. Adding a field = minor; removing/renaming or changing a metric's meaning = major. Timing-model changes that alter Stats for a fixed manifest are breaking (documented in CHANGELOG with the IPC delta).
  • MSRV 1.78, edition 2021; ooo-isa is no_std + alloc.
  • Dockerfile pins toolchain for bit-reproducible CI runs (same Stats across hosts — model is integer-only, no float nondeterminism in the timing path; IPC float derived at report time).

28.9 Incident response / runbooks

Symptom First moves Likely cause
"IPC looks wrong" echo resolved ConfigParams (config --show); read stall histogram; check committed vs golden inst count misread knob; dominant stall (RobFull/RsFull/FuBusy); off-by-config not bug
"Cosim diverges" read Divergence record (cycle/pc/reg/got/expected); bisect via recent_commits; re-run golden-only on that pc rename/ROB ordering bug, store-to-load forwarding, or decode of an edge instruction
"Sim hangs / no progress" dump in-flight ROB (head..tail, oldest RobEntry.done flags) + RS ready bits; deadlock detector wakeup never fires (lost broadcast); ROB head stuck on un-done load; circular structural stall
"Results nondeterministic" confirm seed pinned in manifest; check no HashMap iteration order in tick(); verify two runs byte-equal unseeded RNG, hash-order dependence, or hidden I/O inside the model
"Throughput dropped" bench vs last tag; profile tick(); check trace accidentally enabled trace feature on; allocation in hot loop; cache-unfriendly struct growth
"Mispredict rate spiked" confirm predictor knob; per-branch-pc histogram from trace predictor misconfig; BTB too small; RAS underflow on deep calls

Deadlock detector (built into run): if committed does not advance for 2 * rob_size cycles while ROB is non-empty, abort with SimError::Deadlock, auto-dumping the ROB head entry + its blocking StallCause + RS/LSQ readiness — turns a hang into an actionable record.

28.10 Readiness checklist (gates the blueprint "done")

  • Stats exposes every metric in 28.1; ratios match hand-computed spot checks.
  • Stall histogram sums to cycles * width accounting; no None leakage on real stalls.
  • trace/cosim/stats/bench subcommands emit the 28.3 schemas; NDJSON parses.
  • Zero divergence on riscv-tests + 10k fuzz programs; determinism byte-compare green in CI.
  • ≥1M sim-cycles/sec on the reference kernel (release); bench regression gate wired.
  • Manifest round-trips: config --validate + identical Stats from a re-run.
  • Deadlock detector fires on a seeded lost-wakeup fixture and produces the dump.

Compatibility & Versioning

Defines what callers may depend on across oooc releases, the stability tier of each public surface, and how microarchitectural model evolution is versioned. SemVer 2.0.0 applies to the workspace as a single versioned unit (all nine ooo-* crates share one version; lockstep release).

Public surface (stability tiers)

Surface Tier What is guaranteed Breakage =
OooCore::new/tick/run, Stats fields Stable Signatures + field meaning fixed within a major major bump
ConfigParams (fields, defaults) Stable-additive New knobs only, defaulting to current behavior removing/retyping a knob = major
trace JSONL schema Stable Field names, types, per-cycle record shape renaming/removing field = major
stats output schema Stable Counter names, units, ipc formula major
ISA support matrix Versioned RV64IM now; FD/A/C deferred (documented) dropping an extension = major
ooo-isa::decode/execute_functional (golden) Stable Architectural semantics frozen per ISA-manual rev major
Internal types: RsEntry, RobEntry, LsqEntry, Scheduler, PhysRegFile, wakeup/select internals Unstable None; refactor freely patch OK
PredictorKind variants, latency tables, FU mix Model-versioned See microarch versioning below minor (IPC delta)
  • Primary public API surface = OooCore + ConfigParams + Stats + the trace/stats formats. Everything else is #[doc(hidden)]-eligible or behind pub(crate).
  • oooc CLI subcommand names (run trace stats cosim config bench) + their flag long-names are Stable; short flags may change (patch).

ISA support matrix (documented, versioned)

Extension Status Since Notes
RV64I base Supported 1.0 full integer, x0-zero, sign/zero ext, traps skeleton
M (mul/div/rem) Supported 1.0 Mul/Div FUs, iterative Div latency
F/D (float) PhaseDeferred — decode rejects; documented non-goal until float FU lands
A (atomics) PhaseDeferred — single-core; LR/SC/AMO not modeled
C (compressed) PhaseDeferred — 16-bit re-expansion not implemented
Zicsr (CSR subset) Partial 1.0 only CSRs the trap model needs
  • Matrix is part of the public contract: oooc config --isa prints it; an unsupported opcode raises a documented IllegalInst trap, never silent UB.
  • Adding a deferred extension = minor (additive). Removing/regressing a supported extension = major.

ConfigParams evolution rules

  • New knob → must default to current observable behavior (no IPC change for existing configs). Add via #[serde(default)] + a Default value matching prior semantics.
  • Removing a knob, narrowing its range, or changing its type/default = breaking (major).
  • Config files are versioned: top-level schema_version. Loader accepts current + previous schema; unknown future keys → hard error (no silent drop), so a typo never becomes a default.
  • Knob value validation (e.g. phys_regs >= 32 + rob_size, issue_width <= rs_size) is part of the contract; invalid combos rejected at OooCore::new, not at runtime.

Trace & stats format stability

  • trace: newline-delimited JSON, one record per cycle (or per instruction in --by-inst). Stable keys: cycle, stage, rob_idx, pc, op, phys_dst, event. Additive only; consumers MUST ignore unknown keys.
  • stats: stable counter names — cycles committed mispredicts cache_misses ipc plus the Phase 11 extended set (per-FU utilization, RS/ROB/LSQ occupancy, predictor accuracy). ipc = committed as f64 / cycles is frozen.
  • Schema carries format_version; bumped (minor) when fields are added, (major) if a field's meaning changes. Golden fixtures pin exact byte output for regression.

Golden co-sim & Spike pinning

  • The lockstep guarantee: the sequence of committed architectural states EXACTLY matches ooo-isa::execute_functional, independent of timing — invariant across all versions, all configs.
  • Differential validation pins one Spike revision (recorded as SPIKE_REV in CI + CHANGELOG). Bumping the pinned Spike is a documented event; a divergence newly exposed by a Spike bump is treated as our bug unless attributable to an ISA-manual erratum.
  • oooc cosim exit codes are stable: 0 match, 2 divergence (prints diverging PC + commit index), 3 Spike unavailable.

Determinism guarantee

  • Contract: identical (program, ConfigParams, seed, engine-version) → byte-identical Stats and trace, on any platform, any thread count.
  • Enforced by: reverse-order Tick() (no same-cycle teleport), no wall-clock/RNG outside the seeded generator, no HashMap iteration in model state (ordered containers only), no float in timing decisions.
  • Determinism may break only at a model-version change (below); never silently within one engine-version.

Microarchitectural model versioning

  • A change altering observable timing/IPC for any fixed config (latency table, predictor algorithm, wakeup/select/age policy, LSQ disambiguation, cache/MSHR/TLB behavior) is a minor-version event, even if no API changes.
  • Such changes MUST be in CHANGELOG under a ### Model heading with: affected configs, direction + magnitude of IPC delta on the benchmark suite, and rationale. Stable-tier API/format stays untouched.
  • Pure perf/refactor with bit-identical Stats across the benchmark suite = patch.
  • Stats and trace carry engine_version; replay across a model-version boundary is allowed to differ and tooling flags the mismatch rather than asserting equality.

MSRV policy

  • MSRV = Rust 1.78, edition 2021. Raising MSRV = minor (documented in CHANGELOG); never a patch. CI gates the floor on the exact MSRV toolchain.
  • ooo-isa stays no_std + alloc (embeddable golden model); breaking that = major.

What is explicitly NOT guaranteed

  • Absolute sim throughput (target ≥1M simulated cycles/sec) is a goal, not a contract; perf may shift within a major.
  • Exact internal state layout, private type sizes, panic message text, Debug formatting of internal types.
  • IPC numbers as cross-version constants — they are model-versioned (see above), not API.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Six test classes, each tied to a §7 invariant. Every test is deterministic (fixed seed/config); no wall-clock or thread nondeterminism. Tests gate CI; failures block promotion.

Test class → invariant map

Class Targets §7 invariant enforced
Unit decode, RAT/FreeList, wakeup/select, bypass, LSQ forward, ROB order, recovery INV-1 (decode via golden), INV-4 (RAT/FreeList), INV-5 (wakeup timing), INV-7 (LSQ forward), INV-2 (ROB order), INV-6 (recovery)
Integration run ELF/hex end-to-end + per-commit cosim INV-1
Differential committed state vs Spike; IPC vs gem5 ±tol INV-1, INV-9
Property cosim no-diverge, PhysReg conservation, in-order commit, determinism, no over-alloc INV-4, INV-2, INV-5, INV-8
Fuzz random RV64IM gen → cosim + no-panic + no-hang INV-1, INV-10 (no-panic robustness: §4.9)
Conformance riscv-tests rv64ui/rv64um ledger INV-1

Unit tests (per crate)

Crate Test Assertion
ooo-isa decode_per_encoding each RV64IM encoding (R/I/S/B/U/J) → exact DecodedInst fields; table-driven, one row per Opcode
ooo-isa imm_extract sign-extend B/J/S/I immediates; bit-permutation correctness
ooo-isa x0_hardwired execute_functional write to RegId(0) is dropped; reads = 0
ooo-isa mul_div_edge Div by 0 → −1 / DIVU → all-ones; signed overflow MIN/-1; Mul high bits
ooo-rename rat_freelist_accounting alloc N → FreeList shrinks N; commit frees old_phys; head/tail invariant
ooo-rename rename_kills_war_waw back-to-back same-arch-dst gets distinct PhysReg; only RAW remains
ooo-rename checkpoint_restore RenameCheckpoint round-trip restores Rat + free_head byte-identical
ooo-schedule wakeup_marks_ready broadcast PhysReg sets src1_ready/src2_ready on matching RsEntry only
ooo-schedule select_oldest_ready among ready entries per FuKind, oldest rob_idx chosen; respects issue_width
ooo-execute bypass_back_to_back dependent ALU pair issues N+1 (bypass), not N+latency+wb
ooo-execute fu_latency_table busy_until = cycle+latency; pipelined FU accepts 1/cycle, iterative Div blocks
ooo-mem stl_forward_exact load exact-addr match on older store → forwarded value, no cache access
ooo-mem disambig_unknown_store older store with addr_ready=false → load stalls (conservative path)
ooo-mem cache_tlb_hit_miss hit latency vs miss+MSHR latency; Tlb va→pa fill
ooo-rob commit_in_order OoO completion, in-order retire; head-only commit; done gating
ooo-rob precise_exception trap at head squashes younger; arch state matches pre-trap commit point
ooo-rob flush_recovery mispredict at non-head: ROB/RS/LSQ younger-than-branch squashed, fetch redirected

Target: ≥90% line + ≥85% branch coverage per crate (cargo llvm-cov), gated in CI.

Integration tests (ooo-core)

  • Fixtures: hand-written .hex micro-kernels in tests/fixtures/ — sum_1_n, fib, memcpy, branchy_loop, dep_chain (RAW chain stress), pointer_chase (load-latency stress), mul_div_mix.
